# Slice 3.5 — Deterministic Upgrades: Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: use `superpowers:subagent-driven-development`
> (recommended) or `superpowers:executing-plans` to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use
> checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
>
> **Deliverable note.** This document IS the artifact. Execute it on branch
> `slice-3.5-upgrades`, cut from `main` at the Slice 3 completion commit.
>
> **Predecessors — do not re-execute:** Slice 0, Slice 1, Slice 2 and Slice 3 are complete.
> Their plans are historical records. Start from [HANDOFF.md](../../../HANDOFF.md).

**Goal:** an upgrade of the pinned gbrain deployment is one operation with a snapshot in front of
it and a rollback behind it, every version's migration playbook is hand-written deterministic code
recorded in a ledger, and the gate that decides success is `gbrain doctor` compared against a
baseline — not a hope.

**Scope:** `v0.50.0.0 → v0.50.5.0`, one jump, on the dev stack, plus the machinery that makes the
next jump routine.

**Spec:** `C:\Users\juanp\.claude\plans\we-want-to-do-snug-wolf.md` § *Upgrade flow*.
**Roadmap:** [`2026-09-14-brainstand-roadmap.md`](./2026-09-14-brainstand-roadmap.md) § *Slice 3.5*.

---

## Global Constraints

These are the roadmap's global constraints, cut down to the ones that bite *this* slice. Every
task's requirements implicitly include this section.

- **G1 — Never fork. Pin exact semver.** Images install `github:garrytan/gbrain#v${GBRAIN_VERSION}`.
  **Never `latest-stable`, `main`, `master` or `HEAD`** — `lint:forbidden` rule `moving-pin`
  catches it. This slice *moves* the pin; it never makes it moving.
- **G2 — Drive gbrain only through its published contracts.** CLI exec is the control lane,
  allowlisted **argv arrays, never shell strings**, `--json` where available. HTTP MCP is the data
  lane. The file plane covers only what `config set` refuses.
- **G3 — No reaching past the contracts.** No importing past gbrain's `exports` map, no reading
  gbrain's tables, no patching, **no screen-scraping human-readable CLI output.** This constraint
  is load-bearing here: `apply-migrations` has no `--json`, and the schema version is only
  available as prose. See D5 and U6 — we record what the contracts expose and file the rest as an
  upstream contribution. We do not regex it.
- **G4 — Every registry entry is backed by a contract test at the pin.**
  `test/unit/registry.test.ts` enforces it mechanically (`every operation family is covered by a
  contract test file`, `operations.json matches registry.ts`).
- **G5 — An LLM may PROPOSE a migration checklist. It never executes one.** Playbook steps are
  hand-written code in this repo, reviewed like code. Judgement residue is a checklist item a
  human acknowledges. There is no third category.
- **S2 — Privilege separation holds.** The control plane holds no brain `DATABASE_URL` and a
  read-only `GBRAIN_HOME`. Only `gbrain-ops` holds brain DB creds and a writable `GBRAIN_HOME`.
  **The control plane never gains the Docker socket or a Docker CLI** — that is why the executor
  is a host script (D1).
- **S3 — Every file-plane write serializes through `gbrain-ops` behind the control-DB advisory
  lock**, with atomic-write semantics. `post-upgrade` is a file-plane writer (U4). So is
  `check-update` (it writes its cache file).
- **Never run `gbrain autopilot --install` in a container.** `lint:forbidden` rule
  `autopilot-install`.
- **Never run `gbrain upgrade` or `gbrain self-upgrade`.** The product owns the upgrade lane. We
  swap images; we never let the binary rewrite itself. `GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE=off` stays set in
  every container, and Task 1 verifies v0.50.5.0's `.env` guardrail has not quarantined it.
- **Caddy is the only service publishing host ports.** Do not add a `ports:` entry to anything.
- **Never weaken a test or a gate to make it pass.** If a gate is wrong, fix the gate in this plan
  with evidence and say so in `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` — do not relax an assertion in place.

**Entry gate (verify before Task 1):** Slice 3 exit gate green — `bun run lint:forbidden`,
`bunx tsc --noEmit`, `bun test test/unit`, `bun test test/integration`, `bun test test/contract`,
`bun test --parallel=1 test/e2e` all green on `main`.

## Prior-slice learnings folded in — do not rediscover these

| # | Learning | Source | How this slice uses it |
|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | A clean supervisor drain measured **1150 ms**, not the 40s budget | Slice 0 V5 | The quiesce window is budgeted generously but measured and recorded; do not design around 40s |
| L2 | `jobs supervisor start --detach` **always exits 0** regardless of lock state | Slice 0 finding | Never treat a start exit code as evidence; poll `status --json` |
| L3 | Supervisor has an undocumented exit code **4 = `LOCK_LOST`** | Slice 0 finding | Any supervisor wrapper treats 4 as "someone else owns this", not an undifferentiated crash |
| L4 | `doctor --json` has **no top-level `blockers` key** | Slice 0 V6 | The upgrade gate walks `checks[]`. See D5 |
| L5 | `pg_advisory_lock` on a pooled handle leaks and fails to exclude — **reserve a connection** | Slice 1 / Slice 3 L10 | Any new lock use reserves, exactly like `src/ops/lock.ts:31` and `src/scheduler/leader.ts:27` |
| L6 | A Space source is visible to an unqualified read only when `config.federated` is literally `true`; volumes provisioned **before** the F4 fix keep it unset | Slice 3 F4 / F28 | Gate C's populated brain must be provisioned on a post-F4 stack; an unqualified-search failure on a warm stack is F28, not an upgrade defect |
| L7 | A restore loses gbrain's file plane, so `scripts/restore.ts` re-materializes the pin before services boot | Slice 3 F3 | Our snapshot **captures `gbrain-home` directly**, so rollback restores the file plane rather than re-deriving it. `reMaterializeFilePlane()` stays as the backup path's fallback |
| L8 | `CONTROL_DATABASE_URL` must be **defaulted inside each test file**, never required as an export | Slice 3 F1 | Every new DB-backed test file defaults it in its own header |
| L9 | `DECLARED_TABLES` is duplicated in **two** files | Slice 3 F2 | Task 3 updates `test/integration/auth.test.ts` **and** `test/integration/schema.test.ts` |
| L10 | An e2e `beforeAll` must wait on `/.well-known/gbrain` **200** as well as console health; `restore.e2e` runs first and leaves the stack cold | Slice 3 F5 | Gates B and C inherit that readiness wait verbatim |
| L11 | Claim is 20 hits / 15 min in-process; OAuth `/token` is 50 / 15 min on gbrain-serve | Slice 2 carried | Repeated gate runs need a control-plane + serve restart, not `down -v` |
| L12 | `schema list` **silently ignores `--json`**; `embed --json` emits no JSON at all | Slice 0 / Slice 3 R3 | An accepted flag is not an implemented flag. Every new operation's `--json` support is proven by a contract test, never assumed |
| L13 | `gbrain search` with a non-empty `--federated-read` treats that list as the **full** scope | Slice 2 carried | Gate C's content assertions qualify their scope explicitly |
| L14 | Neither `scripts/backup.ts` nor `scripts/restore.ts` names `scheduler` in its stop/start lists | Slice 3 carried | Task 6 fixes it in both, in passing, because the quiesce order is the same defect class |

## Upstream reality this plan is built on

Established by direct reconnaissance of the pinned checkout and a shallow clone of v0.50.5.0.
**These are facts, not assumptions.** Each is re-proven by a contract test where a task depends on
it; anything that turns out false goes to `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` with the command and output.

| # | Fact | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| U1 | `check-update` **never sets an exit code** — always 0, including on network failure | Branch on `--json`'s `.error`. An exit-code check is a silent lie |
| U2 | `check-update` reads `VERSION` off **master**, not a release API; `published_at` is always `''` | "Latest" may be a version nobody vetted. Detect offers; a human selects (D7) |
| U3 | `apply-migrations` has **no `--json`**; exit codes are 0 / 1 / 2 | Exit codes only. Parsing its prose is a G3 violation |
| U4 | `post-upgrade` does a **lock-free read-modify-write on `config.json`**, specifically `self_upgrade.*`, and the autopilot channel races it | Run it only inside the quiesce window, through ops' advisory lock |
| U5 | `GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE` env **overrides** config | Our `off` survives whatever `post-upgrade` writes — provided v0.50.5.0's `.env` guardrail has not quarantined the var (Task 1 probes this) |
| U6 | There is **no schema-version surface in any published contract** — no table read we are allowed, no JSON field, only prose | The ledger records binary version + doctor's `schema_version` check **status**. The gap is filed as an upstream contribution, not scraped |
| U7 | An **older binary against a newer schema silently succeeds** (`applied:0`, no warning); doctor calls it `warn`, and `warnings` **exits 0** | An exit-code-only gate cannot see "binary behind DB". D5 exists for this |
| U8 | **No down-migrations exist anywhere upstream**; `OrchestratorResult` has no rollback state | Rollback is snapshot-restore or it is nothing (D4) |
| U9 | `supervisor stop`'s `reason:'drained'` cannot distinguish a clean SIGTERM exit from a `MAX_CRASHES` exit in the same 40s window | Verify via the audit log's `exit_code` + `status --json`, never the stop report alone |
| U10 | **v0.50.0.0 → v0.50.5.0 ships zero schema migrations and zero capability drift** — `migrate.ts`, the operation registry, `HARNESS_ADAPTERS` and the 7 schema packs are byte-identical | The Schema step is a real no-op on this delta. Say so in the ledger; prove the schema and rollback halves against a fixtured failure (D9) |
| U11 | Exactly one new playbook file exists in the delta — `skills/migrations/v0.50.2.0.md` — and it says "This release adds no schema migration". `v0.50.3.0` does not exist | Four versions, one playbook, three explicit `noPlaybookRequired` rows with evidence (D6) |
| U12 | v0.50.5.0 is a **security-hardening wave**: OAuth self-registration scope ceiling, transcript secret redaction, `.env` guardrail quarantine of `GBRAIN_*` vars, `bunfig.toml` preload lockdown. At our pin, `serve --http`'s `server.close()` has no timeout bound | This, not schema, is where the delta can break us. Task 1 probes it before any machinery is built |

## Honesty rules the upgrade lane must encode

Each is a trust bug if violated, not a UX bug.

1. **A check that never ran is not a pass.** `check-update` returning `error: 'network_error'`
   renders as *"could not check"*, never as *"up to date"*. Upstream itself refuses to fabricate
   this; we must not undo that.
2. **"Latest" is upstream's master `VERSION`, not a vetted release.** The banner offers a target.
   A human selects it. Nothing auto-upgrades, ever.
3. **A drained PID is not a clean drain.** Cross-check the supervisor audit log's `exit_code`
   before recording a quiesce as clean.
4. **Doctor exit 0 is not health.** `warnings` exits 0, and "this binary is behind the DB" is a
   warn. Parity is per-check (D5).
5. **Rollback is only real if it is one operation** and it restores the file plane too. A restore
   that needs a second manual step is not a rollback.
6. **A step that cannot be written as deterministic code is a checklist item**, not a "best
   effort" step. There is no third category (G5).
7. **A ledger row that says `applied` must name what was applied** — including *"upstream ships no
   playbook for this version"* with the evidence that established it.
8. **`completed` and `awaiting_admin_decision` coexist honestly.** A pending checklist item keeps
   the banner up and blocks nothing else; it must survive control-plane restarts.
9. **The pin in `.env` is not evidence the binary moved.** Verify the running binary against the
   declared pin at runtime (Task 2) — today nothing does.
10. **A snapshot nobody restored is not a backup.** Every snapshot this slice takes is verified by
    checksum, and the rollback path is exercised by the exit gate, not asserted.

---

## Evidence requirements

- **Run the command; paste the real output.** `superpowers:verification-before-completion` applies
  to every "Run … Expected …" step. A step that was not run is not done.
- **A new registry entry needs a contract test at the pin** before `bun run operations:emit`.
  `test/unit/registry.test.ts` fails otherwise — that is the seam, not a convention.
- **Any deviation from this plan gets patched back into this file** as a
  `> **Corrected <date> — <what broke>.**` blockquote, the way Slice 1's and Slice 3's plans record
  theirs. Do not fix the repo and leave the plan wrong.
- **Anything upstream that this plan asserts and reality contradicts** goes into
  `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` with the exact command and output, using the
  `confirmed` / `contradicted` / `inference` vocabulary Slice 3 established.
- **Timings are measured, not estimated.** The quiesce window, the snapshot duration, the image
  build and the total downtime all get recorded in the findings doc as real numbers.

## Design decisions locked before Task 1

Get these right the first time. Do not re-litigate them mid-execution; if execution proves one
wrong, patch it here with a `Corrected` blockquote and say why.

### D1 — The executor is a host CLI; the control plane is the system of record

`scripts/upgrade.ts` runs on the host and is the only component that touches Docker, exactly like
`scripts/backup.ts` and `scripts/restore.ts` already do. The control plane never gains a Docker
socket or CLI (S2), and `POST /api/backup`'s 503 `BACKUP_HOST_ONLY_ERROR` is the precedent for how
that boundary is expressed.

The control plane owns the durable product surface: the run history, the ledger, the checklist, the
banner and the UI. It does **not** drive the upgrade.

### D2 — State is journal-first, reconciled second

The control plane is stopped for most of an upgrade — it cannot be the authority on a run that is
swapping it. So `scripts/upgrade.ts` appends every transition to
`/srv/upgrades/<runId>/journal.ndjson` (`UPGRADE_DIR`, default `/srv/upgrades`) with a monotonic
`seq`, and `src/upgrade/reconcile.ts` upserts that journal into `upgrade_runs` / `upgrade_events` /
`playbook_ledger` / `upgrade_checklist_items` when the control plane comes back — on boot and on a
timer, idempotent on `(runId, seq)`.

This is also what makes honesty rule 8 true: an `awaiting_admin_decision` item survives a control-
plane restart because it was never only in memory.

### D3 — Atomicity comes from the quiesce order, structurally

Not from a snapshot trick. Stop, in order: `scheduler` → `gbrain-worker` → `control-plane` →
`gbrain-serve` → `gbrain-ops`, leaving **Postgres as the only live process**. Both `pg_dump`s and
both volume tars then run with zero writers, so the four artifacts cannot disagree. That is the
whole guarantee, and it is worth more than any amount of care taken afterwards.

The snapshot set is four artifacts and one manifest:

| Artifact | Why |
|---|---|
| `pg_dump` of the `gbrain` database | The brain |
| `pg_dump` of the `control` database | Members, Connections, schedules, ledger |
| `tar` of `brainstand_brain-repos` | Git working trees — the system of record for pages |
| `tar` of `brainstand_gbrain-home` | **The file plane.** Today's backup never captures it (L7) |

One `manifest.json`: `id`, `takenAt`, `fromVersion`, `driver`, `artifacts[] {name, path, bytes, sha256}`,
`quiesce` evidence (which services were stopped, the supervisor drain proof), `runId`.

Behind `SnapshotDriver { id, take, restore, verify }`. The default driver shells docker with argv
arrays through throwaway containers so no service needs to be running. A ZFS or cloud-disk driver
drops in later without touching the flow — which is the seam Slice 5 will need.

### D4 — Rollback is one operation and assumes migrations are irreversible

`bun run scripts/upgrade.ts rollback --run <id>` restores the whole set, restores the pin, recreates
the services at the old tag, and verifies. No upstream reverse migration exists (U8), so there is
nothing else it could be. It is exercised by Gate C, not asserted by this document.

### D5 — The gate is per-check doctor parity

Not the exit code (U7 — `warnings` exits 0, and "binary behind DB" is a warn). Not `blockers` (L4 —
it does not exist). Not `health_score` (a number that moves when upstream adds a check).

`compareDoctor(baseline, after)` returns `{ regressed: Check[], newChecks: Check[], resolved: Check[] }`:

- **Fail the gate** if any check that was `ok` in the baseline is now `warn` or `fail`, or if the
  `schema_version` check is anything but `ok` after the upgrade.
- **Report, do not fail**, for checks that did not exist in the baseline — a new upstream check
  firing is news, not a regression we caused.
- Record `status`, `health_score` and `category_scores` for the trend. Never gate on them.

### D6 — Playbooks are code with provenance; checklists are data

Each version gets a `PlaybookSpec` in `src/upgrade/playbooks/`:

```ts
{
  version: '0.50.2.0',
  upstreamSkill: { path: 'skills/migrations/v0.50.2.0.md', sha256: '<of the file we read>' },
  steps: [{ id, description, run(ctx): Promise<void> }],   // deterministic, hand-written, reviewed
  checklist: [{ key, prompt, why }],                       // judgement residue; a human decides
}
```

or an explicit `{ version, noPlaybookRequired: true, evidence: '<how we know>' }` for a version
upstream ships no playbook for (U11 — three of the four versions in this delta).

`playbooksFor(from, to)` **throws** when a version in `(from, to]` has neither. That is the
mechanism that stops a silent skip from looking like a clean upgrade, and it is what makes the
ledger honest for the operator who inherits this system.

### D7 — Detect offers; a human selects

`check-update` is called through the ops RPC like everything else, its `.error` is surfaced
verbatim (U1, honesty rule 1), and its `latest_version` is presented as a *candidate* with the
changelog. The operator passes `--to <version>` explicitly. `check-update` is **not** schedulable —
`test/unit/scheduler-commands.test.ts:41-44` and `test/unit/scheduler-presets.test.ts:73-75` assert
that today and must stay green.

### D8 — The pin move is one operation, and the compose fallbacks die

`${GBRAIN_VERSION:-0.50.0.0}` appears at five sites across the two compose files (verified by grep during Task 2 authoring — the roadmap-era count of four was low). Worse: `GBRAIN_VERSION` is never passed as a runtime env var into `gbrain-ops` or `control-plane` at all — it is only a build arg and an image tag — so the pin module and the runtime cross-check would read the stale fallback forever until compose passes it through. After a bump, any compose call without
`--env-file` silently runs the old images. Remove the defaults so compose fails loudly on an unset
variable, and assert it in `test/integration/compose-invariants.test.ts`.

The swap order is forced by the Dockerfiles: `bun run operations:emit` must write
`contracts/v<new>/operations.json` **before** the control-plane image builds, because
`docker/control-plane.Dockerfile:18-19` gates on that file existing. `capability.json` can only be
captured after the new stack is up.

`src/control/routes/connections.ts:19-20` reads a literal `contracts/v0.50.0.0/capability.json`.
Route it through `src/contracts/pin.ts` and test it, or the harness picker silently keeps serving
the old pin's adapters forever.

### D9 — The failure leg is fixtured, stated, and real

The real delta has no migrations (U10), so the exit gate's "deliberately fail a migration" half has
nothing to fail on its own. Slice 3 hit the same shape with DST — a boundary you cannot wait for —
and solved it by injecting the clock into production code paths and saying so. The same convention
applies here:

- Regress the brain's recorded schema version so upstream's own migrations are genuinely pending.
- Pre-create an object one of those migrations will collide with.
- Run the real upgrade. `apply-migrations` fails for a real reason, in upstream's code, with a real
  exit code.
- Roll back with the one command and assert `stateDigest` equality against the pre-upgrade digest.

**This is not a weakened gate.** The migration code is upstream's, the failure is genuine, the
rollback is the product's real path, and the only fixtured inputs are the recorded schema version
and one conflicting object — both named in the gate's own output and in the findings doc.

## Task 1: Probe the target pin — throwaway, no product code

**Files:**
- Create: `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` (rows P1–P8 plus the go/no-go decision)
- Generated (kept, re-emitted in Task 7, never hand-edited): `contracts/v0.50.5.0/operations.json`

**Interfaces:**

> **U12 applies here.** v0.50.5.0 is a security-hardening wave — OAuth self-registration scope
> ceiling, transcript redaction, a `.env` guardrail quarantine of security-relevant `GBRAIN_*` vars,
> a `bunfig.toml` preload lockdown. U10 already proved the schema and capability halves of this
> delta are a no-op; this task exists because the security half is not, and G3 forbids finding out
> by reading upstream source instead of running the real binary. L4 (doctor's `blockers` key does
> not exist) and G3 (no screen-scraping) both apply to P6; G4 applies to P4.

- [ ] **P1 — build the target pin; the build itself is the version proof**

```bash
time docker build --build-arg GBRAIN_VERSION=0.50.5.0 \
  -t brainstand/gbrain-base:0.50.5.0 -f docker/gbrain-base.Dockerfile .
```

Record: the `time` output (wall-clock build duration, for the findings doc's timing requirement),
and that the build reached the smoke gate at `gbrain-base.Dockerfile:29-30` — that `RUN gbrain
--version | tee ... && awk ...` step is what makes a green build itself the version proof; there is
nothing further to assert. If the build fails at that gate, stop: the pin does not resolve to what
upstream calls v0.50.5.0, and every later probe is moot.

- [ ] **P2 — the kill switch, under the new release's `.env` guardrail**

`GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE=off` is baked into the image (`gbrain-base.Dockerfile:48`), so the probe
runs the image once with the switch confirmed on and once with it forced empty at the container
boundary (`docker run -e` always overrides a Dockerfile `ENV`, even to `''`) to see the code path
that fires when the mode resolves to anything other than `'off'`:

```bash
docker run --rm -e GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE=off brainstand/gbrain-base:0.50.5.0 sh -c '
  gbrain --version >/tmp/out 2>/tmp/err
  echo "--- upgrade marker on stderr ---"; grep -i upgrade /tmp/err || echo "(none)"
  echo "--- last-update-check cache ---"; ls -la ~/.gbrain/last-update-check 2>&1
'

docker run --rm -e GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE= brainstand/gbrain-base:0.50.5.0 sh -c '
  gbrain --version >/tmp/out 2>/tmp/err
  echo "--- upgrade marker on stderr ---"; grep -i upgrade /tmp/err || echo "(none)"
  echo "--- last-update-check cache ---"; ls -la ~/.gbrain/last-update-check 2>&1
'
```

Record, for each run: whether the marker grep printed anything, and whether the cache file exists.
Per RECON, `off` suppresses both (`src/cli.ts:384-414`). **If the FIRST run (mode=off) shows either
signal present, that is the blocker U12 warns about** — the `.env` guardrail quarantine has reached
`GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE` itself, S2's "never let the binary rewrite itself" guarantee no longer
holds at this pin, and this is a stop-the-line finding: record it, do not proceed to P3, and report
to the human. If only the SECOND run (mode empty) shows the signals, `off` is confirmed intact —
proceed.

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — P2's probe had a false antecedent and could not discriminate.** P2 used
> `gbrain --version` as its trigger, but `--version` is handled and returns *before* the startup hook
> that consults `GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE` is ever reached, so the ambient update-check path never runs.
> Both arms therefore returned the same negative (no marker, no cache file), and the rule "if only the
> SECOND run shows the signals, `off` is confirmed intact" has an antecedent that never fires — a
> non-discriminating probe, which honesty rule 1 forbids recording as a pass. Seeding a *stale* cache
> does not fix it either: under `notify` a stale cache produces the same silent 62-byte stderr as `off`
> and only kicks an asynchronous refresh. **The replacement probe** seeds a **fresh**
> `~/.gbrain/last-update-check` containing `UPGRADE_AVAILABLE 0.50.5.0 0.99.0.0` (a version strictly
> newer than the running binary), then runs one real command that reaches the hook — `gbrain doctor
> --json` — under `off` and under `notify`, with stderr non-TTY. `off` yields no marker and 62 stderr
> bytes; `notify` yields `UPGRADE_AVAILABLE 0.50.5.0 0.99.0.0` plus the human sentence and 162 bytes.
> That 100-byte difference is the control arm the original probe lacked. Result: **P2 is
> CONFIRMED-INTACT** — the guardrail has not quarantined the var and `off` is honoured. Two carried
> notes: (a) the guardrail *is* real but selective — it quarantines `GBRAIN_DATABASE_URL` from a cwd
> `.env` with an explicit `[env] Ignoring ...` warning while leaving `GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE`
> unprotected, so a cwd `.env` can set the mode when the process env does not; (b) with the var unset
> entirely the default is notify-like, so the kill switch **fails open** — Task 2's runtime cross-check
> must assert the var is *present and `off`*, not merely that it is not `notify`. See
> `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` rows P2, P2-a.

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — P6's command yields no JSON against an uninitialized container.**

> to stdout (`GBRAIN_DB_ACCESS no_url` / `No brain configured.` go to stderr), so the follow-up `bun -e`

> top_issues`) and `'blockers' in d === false`. The optional `engine` key is absent on a fallback run;

- [ ] **P7 — `apply-migrations` and `post-upgrade` idempotency at the new pin**

```bash
GBRAIN_VERSION=0.50.5.0 docker compose -f compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml --env-file .env \
  up -d --build --wait
# $GBRAIN_DATABASE_URL must be expanded INSIDE the container (it is set on
# gbrain-ops's own environment block, not on the host shell running this
# probe) — sh -c here is a throwaway probe command, not committed code, so
# it is not the G2 "shell-string-gbrain" violation that rule forbids in src/.
docker compose -f compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml exec -T gbrain-ops sh -c \
  'gbrain init --non-interactive --force --url "$GBRAIN_DATABASE_URL" \
   --embedding-model openai:text-embedding-3-small'

docker compose -f compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml exec -T gbrain-ops \
  gbrain apply-migrations --yes --non-interactive; echo "exit:$?"

docker compose -f compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml exec -T gbrain-ops \
  cat /srv/gbrain-home/config.json > /tmp/p7-config-0.json
docker compose -f compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml exec -T gbrain-ops gbrain post-upgrade
docker compose -f compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml exec -T gbrain-ops \
  cat /srv/gbrain-home/config.json > /tmp/p7-config-1.json
docker compose -f compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml exec -T gbrain-ops gbrain post-upgrade
docker compose -f compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml exec -T gbrain-ops \
  cat /srv/gbrain-home/config.json > /tmp/p7-config-2.json
diff /tmp/p7-config-0.json /tmp/p7-config-1.json
diff /tmp/p7-config-1.json /tmp/p7-config-2.json
bun run stack:down
```

Record: `apply-migrations`'s exit code on an up-to-date brain (expect `0`, and its own prose should
say nothing pending — U3 forbids parsing that prose for anything else, but the exit code alone is
the gate). Record both `diff`s — the first shows what `post-upgrade` writes into `self_upgrade.*`
the first time (U4), the second must be empty (idempotent re-run, per `post-upgrade`'s own help
text).

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — P7's config path does not exist, and the first `post-upgrade` diff is
> empty.** (1) The plan reads `cat /srv/gbrain-home/config.json` three times, but that file does not
> exist (`cat: /srv/gbrain-home/config.json: No such file or directory`). With `GBRAIN_HOME=/srv/gbrain-home`,
> gbrain nests its dot-directory beneath it; the real path is **`/srv/gbrain-home/.gbrain/config.json`**
> (454 bytes). (2) The plan expects the **first** `diff` to show what `post-upgrade` writes into
> `self_upgrade.*` the first time (U4). Both diffs are empty: the config already carried
> `"self_upgrade": {"mode": "notify", "mode_prompted": true}`, written by `gbrain init`, and the file's
> mtime (`2026-09-17 00:12:37.041`) is the init instant — both `post-upgrade` runs (`All migrations up
> to date.`, exit 0) left it untouched. Under the plan's own sequence the first `post-upgrade` performs
> no config read-modify-write, so U4 is not exercised here rather than contradicted as a mechanism. The
> second diff is empty, which is the idempotency the criterion actually asked for. `apply-migrations`
> exits `0` (the gate); note its prose does **not** say "nothing pending" on a freshly `init`ed brain —
> it walks the playbook chain `v0.11.0`…`v0.46.3`, because the brain schema (`v149`) and the playbook
> ledger are separate lanes. See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` rows P7, P7-a, P7-b.

## Task 2: Pin plumbing hardening

**Files:**
- Create: `src/contracts/pin.ts`
- Modify: `compose/docker-compose.yml`, `compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml`,
  `src/control/routes/connections.ts`, `src/control/routes/health.ts`
- Test: `test/unit/pin.test.ts` (create); `test/integration/compose-invariants.test.ts`,
  `test/integration/health.test.ts` (extend)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from a prior Slice 3.5 task — this is the first implementation task.
- Produces: `GBRAIN_PIN`; `contractsDir(version?)`; `operationsPath(version?)`;
  `capabilityPath(version?)`; `type PinVerification`; `type CallOps`;
  `verifyRuntimePin(callOps): Promise<PinVerification>`.

> **D8 and honesty rule 9 apply here.** D8 says the compose fallbacks die; direct inspection of
> `compose/docker-compose.yml` (not just the RECON count) turns up **five** `${GBRAIN_VERSION:-
> 0.50.0.0}` sites, not four — lines 45, 63 and 84 in `docker-compose.yml`, plus 24 and 29 in
> `docker-compose.slice0.yml`. All five die in this task. Separately: neither `gbrain-ops` nor
> `control-plane` is ever handed `GBRAIN_VERSION` as a *runtime* environment variable today — it is
> only a build arg / image-tag interpolation, consumed by `docker compose`, never by the container's
> own process. That means `process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION` is `undefined` inside every running
> container, so `GBRAIN_PIN` (and every existing ad hoc `process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0'`
> reader — `src/ops/dispatch.ts:11`, `src/control/bootstrap.ts:34`) would silently read the fallback
> forever, independent of which pin was actually built. Honesty rule 9's runtime cross-check would
> be theater without fixing this, so Step 4 adds `GBRAIN_VERSION` as a real, required runtime env var
> to both services — a natural extension of "kill the fallbacks," not new scope.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`test/unit/pin.test.ts`:
```ts
import { test, expect, describe } from 'bun:test';
import { readdir, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import {
  GBRAIN_PIN, contractsDir, operationsPath, capabilityPath, verifyRuntimePin,
} from '../../src/contracts/pin.ts';

const EXPECTED = process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0';

async function* walk(dir: string): AsyncGenerator<string> {
  for (const e of await readdir(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
    const p = join(dir, e.name);
    if (e.isDirectory()) yield* walk(p);
    else if (p.endsWith('.ts') || p.endsWith('.tsx')) yield p;
  }
}

describe('pin.ts', () => {
  test('GBRAIN_PIN reflects the declared version', () => {
    expect(GBRAIN_PIN).toBe(EXPECTED);
  });

  test('contractsDir/operationsPath/capabilityPath default to the declared pin', () => {
    expect(contractsDir()).toBe(`contracts/v${EXPECTED}`);
    expect(operationsPath()).toBe(`contracts/v${EXPECTED}/operations.json`);
    expect(capabilityPath()).toBe(`contracts/v${EXPECTED}/capability.json`);
  });

  test('every path helper accepts an explicit version, for probing a candidate pin', () => {
    expect(contractsDir('0.50.5.0')).toBe('contracts/v0.50.5.0');
    expect(operationsPath('0.50.5.0')).toBe('contracts/v0.50.5.0/operations.json');
    expect(capabilityPath('0.50.5.0')).toBe('contracts/v0.50.5.0/capability.json');
  });

  test('verifyRuntimePin never claims a match it cannot prove', async () => {
    const result = await verifyRuntimePin(
      async () => ({ ok: true, exitCode: 0, json: { checks: [] } }),
    );
    expect(result.declared).toBe(GBRAIN_PIN);
    expect(result.observed).toBeNull();
    expect(result.matches).toBeNull(); // not false — unverifiable is not a mismatch
  });

  test('verifyRuntimePin survives an unreachable ops server without throwing', async () => {
    const result = await verifyRuntimePin(async () => { throw new Error('ECONNREFUSED'); });
    expect(result.declared).toBe(GBRAIN_PIN);
    expect(result.matches).toBeNull();
  });

  // This is what stops the connections.ts bug class (a hardcoded
  // 'contracts/v0.50.0.0/...' path) from returning under a different name.
  test('no file under src/ or scripts/ hardcodes the literal v0.50.0.0 path outside pin.ts', async () => {
    const offenders: string[] = [];
    for (const root of ['src', 'scripts']) {
      for await (const file of walk(root)) {
        if (file === join('src', 'contracts', 'pin.ts')) continue;
        const lines = (await readFile(file, 'utf8')).split('\n');
        lines.forEach((line, i) => {
          const trimmed = line.trim();
          const isComment = trimmed.startsWith('//') || trimmed.startsWith('*') || trimmed.startsWith('/**');
          if (!isComment && line.includes('v0.50.0.0')) offenders.push(`${file}:${i + 1}`);
        });
      }
    }
    expect(offenders).toEqual([]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `src/contracts/pin.ts`**

```ts
/**
 * The single source of the declared gbrain pin, and every contracts path
 * derived from it. Kills the class of bug where a reader independently
 * computes `process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0'` and drifts from
 * whatever this file says (D8) — `src/control/routes/connections.ts` was
 * the confirmed instance; `src/ops/dispatch.ts`, `scripts/backup.ts` and
 * `scripts/emit-operations.ts` still compute their own fallback and are out
 * of this task's scope, but Step 4's compose fix means they now see the
 * real value at runtime instead of silently defaulting — filed in
 * docs/slice-3.5-findings.md as a residual cleanup, not silently expanded
 * into this task.
 */
export const GBRAIN_PIN = process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0';

export function contractsDir(version: string = GBRAIN_PIN): string {
  return `contracts/v${version}`;
}

export function operationsPath(version: string = GBRAIN_PIN): string {
  return `${contractsDir(version)}/operations.json`;
}

export function capabilityPath(version: string = GBRAIN_PIN): string {
  return `${contractsDir(version)}/capability.json`;
}

export type PinVerification = {
  declared: string;
  /** null when no reachable operation carries the running binary's version. */
  observed: string | null;
  /** null (not false) when unverifiable — absence of proof is not proof of a mismatch. */
  matches: boolean | null;
};

/** Structural, not imported from opsClient.ts — contracts/ stays below control/ in the layering. */
export type CallOps = <T = unknown>(
  op: string,
  params?: Record<string, unknown>,
  opts?: { json?: boolean; dryRun?: boolean; timeoutMs?: number },
) => Promise<{ ok: boolean; exitCode: number; json: T | null }>;

/**
 * Honesty rule 9: the pin in .env is not evidence the binary moved. This is
 * the first runtime cross-check — today nothing does this.
 *
 * `doctor` (`src/contracts/registry.ts` 'doctor') is the only operation
 * reachable through `callOps` that is version-adjacent at all: no operation
 * returns the running binary's version as a structured field anywhere in
 * today's registry. `doctor`'s own `engine` field is the DB engine
 * ('postgres'|'pglite'), not a gbrain semver. Its one check that ever prints
 * the binary version, `self_upgrade_health`, is pinned to the fixed,
 * version-free message "Self-upgrade disabled (mode=off). Enable: gbrain
 * config set self_upgrade.mode notify" whenever GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE=off
 * — which every gbrain container in this stack sets structurally, gbrain-ops
 * included ('every gbrain container disables self-upgrade',
 * compose-invariants.test.ts). So doctor, reached through gbrain-ops,
 * CANNOT carry the version in this deployment — not "might not", cannot.
 * Scraping that message for a substring guaranteed absent would be worse
 * than reporting the gap (G3): it would look like a working check.
 *
 * Task 7's `checkUpdate` operation will expose `current_version` as a clean
 * JSON field (upstream's check-update.ts:26-35, current_source
 * 'package-json') — re-point this function at it then. Until it lands,
 * `observed` stays null and `matches` stays null: never a false "verified",
 * never a false alarm.
 */
export async function verifyRuntimePin(callOps: CallOps): Promise<PinVerification> {
  const declared = GBRAIN_PIN;
  const observed: string | null = null;
  try {
    await callOps('doctor', {}, { json: true, timeoutMs: 30_000 });
    // Reachable and JSON-capable — proves gbrain-ops is alive and speaking
    // the contract, but (see doc comment above) carries no version field.
  } catch {
    // Unreachable is also "unverifiable", not "mismatched".
  }
  return { declared, observed, matches: observed === null ? null : observed === declared };
}
```

In `compose/docker-compose.yml`, remove the `:-0.50.0.0` default at lines 45, 63 and 84, and add
`GBRAIN_VERSION` as a required runtime env var on `gbrain-ops` and `control-plane`:

```ts
import { GBRAIN_PIN, capabilityPath } from '../../contracts/pin.ts';

type CapabilityFile = { wellKnown?: { adapters?: Array<{ id: string }>; profiles?: unknown } };

function loadCapability(): CapabilityFile {
  const path = join(import.meta.dir, '../../..', capabilityPath());
  try {
    return JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, 'utf8')) as CapabilityFile;
  } catch (e) {
    // D8: capability.json for a freshly-bumped pin does not exist until
    // Task 8's capture runs post-upgrade — a missing file must not crash
    // the whole control plane at import time.
    console.error(`connections: no capability snapshot at ${path} yet (${(e as Error).message})`);
    return {};
  }
}

const capability = loadCapability();
```

## Task 3: Control DB — the three upgrade tables

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/control/db/schema.ts`, `test/integration/auth.test.ts`, `test/integration/schema.test.ts`
- Create: `src/control/db/migrations/0004_*.sql` + `meta/0004_snapshot.json` (generated)
- Test: `test/integration/upgrade-schema.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `getDb`, `runMigrations`, `pinDb`, `closeDb`, `schema` (`src/control/db/client.ts`).
- Produces: `schema.upgradeRuns`, `schema.upgradeEvents`, `schema.upgradeChecklistItems`.

> **L9 applies here.** Slice 3's F2 defect was exactly this: `DECLARED_TABLES` is duplicated in
> `test/integration/auth.test.ts:17-26` and `test/integration/schema.test.ts:71-73`, and forgetting
> the second file makes its `'%s exists'` list under-cover the schema while the extras check in
> `auth.test.ts` starts failing with three unexplained rows. Both are updated in this task, in the
> same step, so they cannot drift apart again the way they already have once.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`test/integration/upgrade-schema.test.ts`:
```ts
import { test, expect, describe, beforeAll, afterAll } from 'bun:test';
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import { sql } from 'drizzle-orm';
import { getDb, closeDb, pinDb, runMigrations, schema } from '../../src/control/db/client.ts';

process.env.CONTROL_DATABASE_URL ??=
  'postgres://gbrain:ci-not-a-real-secret@localhost:5432/control';
pinDb();

/** Same trap test/integration/scheduler-schema.test.ts documents: `expect(...).rejects`
 * hangs on a drizzle PgRaw thenable under this bun. Pin refusals to SQLSTATE + constraint. */
async function expectRefused(
  run: () => Promise<unknown>, sqlstate: string, constraint: string,
): Promise<void> {
  try {
    await run();
  } catch (error) {
    const cause = (error as { cause?: unknown })?.cause ?? error;
    const code = (cause as { code?: unknown })?.code;
    const message = (cause as { message?: unknown })?.message;
    expect(code).toBe(sqlstate);
    expect(String(message)).toContain(constraint);
    return;
  }
  throw new Error(`expected the statement to be refused by ${constraint}`);
}

beforeAll(async () => { await runMigrations(); }, 60_000);
afterAll(async () => { await closeDb(); });

async function seedRun(): Promise<string> {
  const id = randomUUID();
  await getDb().insert(schema.upgradeRuns).values({
    id, fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', createdBy: 'test',
  });
  return id;
}

async function insertEvent(runId: string, seq: number, step: string): Promise<void> {
  await getDb().insert(schema.upgradeEvents).values({
    id: randomUUID(), runId, seq, step, message: `${step} started`,
  });
}

async function seedChecklistItem(runId: string, itemKey: string): Promise<string> {
  const id = randomUUID();
  await getDb().insert(schema.upgradeChecklistItems).values({
    id, runId, gbrainVersion: '0.50.2.0', itemKey, prompt: 'Confirm before continuing',
  });
  return id;
}

describe('upgrade tables', () => {
  test('upgrade_runs, upgrade_events and upgrade_checklist_items exist', async () => {
    const rows = await getDb().execute(sql`
      select table_name from information_schema.tables
      where table_schema = 'public' and table_name in
        ('upgrade_runs', 'upgrade_events', 'upgrade_checklist_items')
    `);
    const names = [...rows].map((r: any) => r.table_name).sort();
    expect(names).toEqual(['upgrade_checklist_items', 'upgrade_events', 'upgrade_runs']);
  });

  test('upgrade_run_state_valid rejects a state outside the transition vocabulary', async () => {
    const id = await seedRun();
    await expectRefused(
      () => getDb().execute(sql`update upgrade_runs set state = 'sideways' where id = ${id}`),
      '23514', 'upgrade_run_state_valid',
    );
  });

  test('every legal upgrade_runs state is accepted, in order', async () => {
    const id = await seedRun();
    const states = [
      'planned', 'preflight_ok', 'quiesced', 'snapshot_taken', 'swapped', 'migrated',
      'playbooks_applied', 'gated_ok', 'completed', 'failed',
      'rolling_back', 'rolled_back', 'rollback_failed',
    ];
    for (const state of states) {
      await getDb().execute(sql`update upgrade_runs set state = ${state} where id = ${id}`);
    }
    const [row] = await getDb().execute(sql`select state from upgrade_runs where id = ${id}`);
    expect((row as any).state).toBe('rollback_failed');
  });

  test('(run_id, seq) is UNIQUE on upgrade_events — the idempotent-replay key (D2)', async () => {
    const runId = await seedRun();
    await insertEvent(runId, 1, 'quiesce');
    await expectRefused(() => insertEvent(runId, 1, 'quiesce-retry'), '23505', 'upgrade_events_run_seq_uq');
    await insertEvent(runId, 2, 'snapshot');
    const rows = await getDb().execute(sql`select seq from upgrade_events where run_id = ${runId} order by seq`);
    expect([...rows].map((r: any) => r.seq)).toEqual([1, 2]);
  });

  test('upgrade_events.level rejects anything outside info|warn|error', async () => {
    const runId = await seedRun();
    await insertEvent(runId, 1, 'quiesce');
    await expectRefused(
      () => getDb().execute(sql`update upgrade_events set level = 'fatal' where run_id = ${runId}`),
      '23514', 'upgrade_event_level_valid',
    );
  });

  test('upgrade_checklist_items.state rejects anything outside pending|acknowledged|declined', async () => {
    const runId = await seedRun();
    const itemId = await seedChecklistItem(runId, 'confirm-oauth-scopes');
    await expectRefused(
      () => getDb().execute(sql`update upgrade_checklist_items set state = 'ignored' where id = ${itemId}`),
      '23514', 'upgrade_checklist_state_valid',
    );
  });

  test('upgrade_checklist_run_key_uq is UNIQUE on (run_id, item_key)', async () => {
    const runId = await seedRun();
    await seedChecklistItem(runId, 'confirm-oauth-scopes');
    await expectRefused(
      () => seedChecklistItem(runId, 'confirm-oauth-scopes'),
      '23505', 'upgrade_checklist_run_key_uq',
    );
  });

  test('deleting a run cascades its events and checklist items', async () => {
    const runId = await seedRun();
    await insertEvent(runId, 1, 'quiesce');
    await seedChecklistItem(runId, 'confirm-oauth-scopes');
    await getDb().delete(schema.upgradeRuns).where(sql`id = ${runId}`);
    const events = await getDb().execute(sql`select 1 from upgrade_events where run_id = ${runId}`);
    const items = await getDb().execute(sql`select 1 from upgrade_checklist_items where run_id = ${runId}`);
    expect([...events].length).toBe(0);
    expect([...items].length).toBe(0);
  });

  test('the declared-tables allowlists in auth.test.ts and schema.test.ts both name all three tables', async () => {
    const authSrc = await Bun.file('test/integration/auth.test.ts').text();
    const schemaSrc = await Bun.file('test/integration/schema.test.ts').text();
    for (const table of ['upgrade_runs', 'upgrade_events', 'upgrade_checklist_items']) {
      expect(authSrc).toContain(`'${table}'`);
      expect(schemaSrc).toContain(`'${table}'`);
    }
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the test to verify it fails**

Run: `bun test test/integration/upgrade-schema.test.ts`
Expected: FAIL — `schema.upgradeRuns` is undefined.

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the three tables to `schema.ts`**

Append to `src/control/db/schema.ts`:
```ts
/**
 * D2: the journal (Task 4) is the authority while the control plane is
 * down; `src/upgrade/reconcile.ts` (Task 13) upserts it into these three
 * tables once the control plane is back — idempotent on `(runId, seq)` for
 * events, on the row identity for the rest.
 */
export const upgradeRuns = pgTable('upgrade_runs', {
  id: text('id').primaryKey(),
  // Audit-shaped, like audit_log.organizationId: nullable, no FK, so run
  // history survives an org being deleted and a single-org deployment
  // never has to invent one.
  organizationId: text('organization_id'),
  fromVersion: text('from_version').notNull(),
  toVersion: text('to_version').notNull(),
  state: text('state').notNull().default('planned'),
  snapshotId: text('snapshot_id'),
  journalPath: text('journal_path'),
  startedAt: timestamp('started_at', { withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),
  endedAt: timestamp('ended_at', { withTimezone: true }),
  doctorBaseline: jsonb('doctor_baseline'),
  doctorAfter: jsonb('doctor_after'),
  failureReason: text('failure_reason'),
  createdBy: text('created_by'),
}, (t) => [check('upgrade_run_state_valid', sql`${t.state} in (
  'planned','preflight_ok','quiesced','snapshot_taken','swapped','migrated',
  'playbooks_applied','gated_ok','completed','failed',
  'rolling_back','rolled_back','rollback_failed')`)]);

/** UNIQUE (run_id, seq) is what makes reconciliation idempotent (D2): a
 * reconcile pass that reruns after a crash re-upserts the same rows instead
 * of duplicating them. */
export const upgradeEvents = pgTable('upgrade_events', {
  id: text('id').primaryKey(),
  runId: text('run_id').notNull().references(() => upgradeRuns.id, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
  seq: integer('seq').notNull(),
  at: timestamp('at', { withTimezone: true }).notNull().defaultNow(),
  step: text('step').notNull(),
  level: text('level').notNull().default('info'),
  message: text('message').notNull(),
  data: jsonb('data'),
}, (t) => [
  uniqueIndex('upgrade_events_run_seq_uq').on(t.runId, t.seq),
  check('upgrade_event_level_valid', sql`${t.level} in ('info','warn','error')`),
]);

/** G5: an LLM may PROPOSE a checklist item; only a human acknowledges one.
 * Honesty rule 8: a pending item survives a control-plane restart because
 * it lives here, not in memory. */
export const upgradeChecklistItems = pgTable('upgrade_checklist_items', {
  id: text('id').primaryKey(),
  runId: text('run_id').notNull().references(() => upgradeRuns.id, { onDelete: 'cascade' }),
  gbrainVersion: text('gbrain_version').notNull(),
  itemKey: text('item_key').notNull(),
  prompt: text('prompt').notNull(),
  why: text('why'),
  state: text('state').notNull().default('pending'),
  decidedBy: text('decided_by'),
  decidedAt: timestamp('decided_at', { withTimezone: true }),
}, (t) => [
  uniqueIndex('upgrade_checklist_run_key_uq').on(t.runId, t.itemKey),
  check('upgrade_checklist_state_valid', sql`${t.state} in ('pending','acknowledged','declined')`),
]);
```

## Task 4: Journal + manifest

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — `UpgradeContext` was widened during execution; Task 4's draft shape was
> never the shipped one.** Task 4 drafted `{ runId, organizationId, fromVersion, toVersion, journalDir }`,
> but Task 6's Consumes line and Task 11's fixture both assume `dryRun`/`upgradeDir`/`envPath`/`compose`/
> `journal`/`now`. The executed type (owned by `src/upgrade/types.ts`) is
> `{ runId, fromVersion, toVersion, dryRun, upgradeDir, envPath, compose, journal, now }`;
> `organizationId`/`journalDir` were never consumed and were removed. It also owns `UpgradeRunState`,
> `UpgradeEvent`, `SnapshotArtifact`, `SnapshotManifest` and `DoctorBaseline`. A later reader must not
> keep two shapes.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/upgrade/types.ts`, `src/upgrade/journal.ts`, `src/upgrade/manifest.ts`
- Test: `test/unit/upgrade-journal.test.ts`, `test/unit/upgrade-manifest.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from a prior task — pure, no DB, no Docker.
- Produces: `UPGRADE_RUN_STATES`, `UpgradeRunState`, `UpgradeEvent`, `SnapshotArtifact`,
  `SnapshotManifest`, `DoctorBaseline`, `UpgradeContext`; `openJournal(dir, runId)`,
  `appendEvent(journal, event)`, `readJournal(dir, runId)`, `buildManifest(input)`,
  `verifyManifest(manifest, dir)`, `sha256File(path)`.

> **D2 and honesty rule 10 apply here.** D2: the control plane is stopped for most of an upgrade, so
> this journal — not any DB row — is the authority while it is down; `src/upgrade/reconcile.ts`
> (Task 13) is the only thing that ever reads it back into `upgrade_events`. Honesty rule 10: a
> snapshot nobody checksums is not a backup — `verifyManifest` is the checksum half of that
> guarantee, matched by sha256, never by size or presence alone (a same-length tamper would pass a
> size check and must still be caught).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`test/unit/upgrade-journal.test.ts`:
```ts
import { test, expect, describe, afterEach } from 'bun:test';
import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { openJournal, appendEvent, readJournal } from '../../src/upgrade/journal.ts';
import { UPGRADE_RUN_STATES } from '../../src/upgrade/types.ts';

let dir: string | undefined;
afterEach(async () => {
  if (dir) await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
  dir = undefined;
});
async function scratch(): Promise<string> {
  dir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'upgrade-journal-'));
  return dir;
}

describe('journal', () => {
  test('seq is monotonic from 1', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const j = await openJournal(d, 'run-1');
    const e1 = await appendEvent(j, { step: 'preflight', message: 'checks ok' });
    const e2 = await appendEvent(j, { step: 'quiesce', message: 'stopping services' });
    const e3 = await appendEvent(j, { step: 'quiesce', level: 'warn', message: 'worker took longer' });
    expect([e1.seq, e2.seq, e3.seq]).toEqual([1, 2, 3]);
  });

  test('readJournal replays events in append order', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const j = await openJournal(d, 'run-2');
    await appendEvent(j, { step: 'preflight', message: 'a' });
    await appendEvent(j, { step: 'quiesce', message: 'b' });
    await appendEvent(j, { step: 'snapshot', message: 'c' });
    const events = await readJournal(d, 'run-2');
    expect(events.map((e) => e.step)).toEqual(['preflight', 'quiesce', 'snapshot']);
    expect(events.map((e) => e.seq)).toEqual([1, 2, 3]);
    expect(events.every((e) => e.level === 'info')).toBe(true);
  });

  test('defaults level to info and carries structured data through', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const j = await openJournal(d, 'run-3');
    const e = await appendEvent(j, {
      step: 'gate', message: 'doctor regressed', data: { check: 'schema_version' },
    });
    expect(e.level).toBe('info');
    expect(e.data).toEqual({ check: 'schema_version' });
  });

  // A crash mid-write leaves a half-written last line on disk. That must
  // never poison replay of everything written BEFORE the crash, and it
  // must never masquerade as a real event either — it is dropped, not repaired.
  test('a truncated LAST line is dropped silently; the next append resumes at the right seq', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const j = await openJournal(d, 'run-4');
    await appendEvent(j, { step: 'preflight', message: 'a' });
    await appendEvent(j, { step: 'quiesce', message: 'b' });
    await Bun.write(j.path, (await Bun.file(j.path).text()) + '{"seq":3,"step":"snap');

    const events = await readJournal(d, 'run-4');
    expect(events.map((e) => e.step)).toEqual(['preflight', 'quiesce']);

    const recovered = await appendEvent(j, { step: 'snapshot', message: 'retried after crash' });
    expect(recovered.seq).toBe(3); // not 4 — the truncated attempt never counted
  });

  // A bad line that is NOT the last one is real corruption, not a crash
  // artifact, and must be loud, not silently dropped like the trailing case.
  test('a corrupt line in the MIDDLE of the file throws instead of being dropped', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const j = await openJournal(d, 'run-5');
    await appendEvent(j, { step: 'preflight', message: 'a' });
    const text = await Bun.file(j.path).text();
    await Bun.write(j.path, text + 'not-json-at-all\n' + JSON.stringify({
      seq: 2, at: new Date().toISOString(), step: 'quiesce', level: 'info', message: 'b',
    }) + '\n');
    await expect(readJournal(d, 'run-5')).rejects.toThrow(/corrupt/);
  });

  test('openJournal is idempotent — reopening an existing run does not truncate it', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const j1 = await openJournal(d, 'run-6');
    await appendEvent(j1, { step: 'preflight', message: 'a' });
    const j2 = await openJournal(d, 'run-6');
    const next = await appendEvent(j2, { step: 'quiesce', message: 'b' });
    expect(next.seq).toBe(2);
  });
});

describe('UpgradeRunState agreement with schema.ts', () => {
  // Task 3's CHECK and this union are a drifting pair waiting to happen — a
  // state present in one but not the other is a silent write failure in
  // production, not a type error caught at build time.
  test('the TS union and the upgrade_run_state_valid CHECK name exactly the same states', async () => {
    const schemaSrc = await Bun.file('src/control/db/schema.ts').text();
    const match = schemaSrc.match(/check\('upgrade_run_state_valid'[^)]*?in\s*\(([^)]*)\)/s);
    expect(match).not.toBeNull();
    const fromCheck = match![1]!.split(',').map((s) => s.trim().replace(/^'|'$/g, '')).filter(Boolean);
    expect(new Set(fromCheck)).toEqual(new Set(UPGRADE_RUN_STATES));
    expect(fromCheck.length).toBe(UPGRADE_RUN_STATES.length);
  });
});
```

`test/unit/upgrade-manifest.test.ts`:
```ts
import { test, expect, describe, afterEach } from 'bun:test';
import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { sha256File, buildManifest, verifyManifest } from '../../src/upgrade/manifest.ts';

let dir: string | undefined;
afterEach(async () => {
  if (dir) await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
  dir = undefined;
});
async function scratch(): Promise<string> {
  dir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'upgrade-manifest-'));
  return dir;
}

describe('sha256File / buildManifest', () => {
  test('sha256File hashes real file content', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const path = join(d, 'a.txt');
    await writeFile(path, 'hello');
    expect(await sha256File(path)).toBe(
      '2cf24dba5fb0a30e26e83b2ac5b9e29e1b161e5c1fa7425e73043362938b9824',
    );
  });

  test('buildManifest records name, path, bytes and sha256 for each artifact', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const brainPath = join(d, 'brain.sql');
    const reposPath = join(d, 'brain-repos.tar');
    await writeFile(brainPath, 'brain dump');
    await writeFile(reposPath, 'tar bytes');

    const manifest = await buildManifest({
      id: 'snap-1', runId: 'run-1', fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', driver: 'docker-volume',
      artifacts: [{ name: 'brainDb', path: brainPath }, { name: 'brainRepos', path: reposPath }],
      quiesce: { stoppedServices: ['scheduler', 'gbrain-worker'], drainProofs: {} },
    });

    expect(manifest.id).toBe('snap-1');
    expect(manifest.runId).toBe('run-1');
    expect(manifest.artifacts).toHaveLength(2);
    const brain = manifest.artifacts.find((a) => a.name === 'brainDb')!;
    expect(brain.path).toBe('brain.sql'); // stored relative — the manifest is portable
    expect(brain.bytes).toBe('brain dump'.length);
    expect(brain.sha256).toBe(await sha256File(brainPath));
  });

  test('buildManifest refuses when an artifact file is missing', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    await expect(buildManifest({
      id: 'snap-2', runId: 'run-1', fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', driver: 'docker-volume',
      artifacts: [{ name: 'controlDb', path: join(d, 'nope.sql') }],
      quiesce: { stoppedServices: [], drainProofs: {} },
    })).rejects.toThrow('artifact "controlDb" is missing');
  });
});

describe('verifyManifest', () => {
  test('reports ok for an untouched snapshot directory', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    await writeFile(join(d, 'a.sql'), 'dump');
    const manifest = await buildManifest({
      id: 'snap-3', runId: 'run-1', fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', driver: 'docker-volume',
      artifacts: [{ name: 'controlDb', path: join(d, 'a.sql') }],
      quiesce: { stoppedServices: [], drainProofs: {} },
    });
    expect(await verifyManifest(manifest, d)).toEqual({ ok: true, mismatches: [] });
  });

  // Honesty rule 10: a snapshot nobody checksums is not a backup. Same byte
  // length as the original — a size-only check would miss this.
  test('catches a tampered artifact by sha256, not by size or presence alone', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const path = join(d, 'a.sql');
    await writeFile(path, 'original dump');
    const manifest = await buildManifest({
      id: 'snap-4', runId: 'run-1', fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', driver: 'docker-volume',
      artifacts: [{ name: 'controlDb', path }],
      quiesce: { stoppedServices: [], drainProofs: {} },
    });
    await writeFile(path, 'originai dump');
    const result = await verifyManifest(manifest, d);
    expect(result.ok).toBe(false);
    expect(result.mismatches).toEqual(['controlDb: sha256 mismatch']);
  });

  test('reports a missing artifact distinctly from a mismatch', async () => {
    const d = await scratch();
    const path = join(d, 'a.sql');
    await writeFile(path, 'dump');
    const manifest = await buildManifest({
      id: 'snap-5', runId: 'run-1', fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', driver: 'docker-volume',
      artifacts: [{ name: 'controlDb', path }],
      quiesce: { stoppedServices: [], drainProofs: {} },
    });
    await rm(path);
    const result = await verifyManifest(manifest, d);
    expect(result.ok).toBe(false);
    expect(result.mismatches[0]).toContain('controlDb: missing at');
  });
});
```

```ts
export const UPGRADE_RUN_STATES = [
  'planned', 'preflight_ok', 'quiesced', 'snapshot_taken', 'swapped', 'migrated',
  'playbooks_applied', 'gated_ok', 'completed', 'failed',
  'rolling_back', 'rolled_back', 'rollback_failed',
] as const;

/** Must name exactly the values in schema.ts's `upgrade_run_state_valid` CHECK
 * (Task 3) — a unit test above asserts the two agree, because drift here is a
 * silent DB write failure in production, not a type error. */
export type UpgradeRunState = typeof UPGRADE_RUN_STATES[number];

export type UpgradeEvent = {
  seq: number;
  at: string;
  step: string;
  level: 'info' | 'warn' | 'error';
  message: string;
  data?: unknown;
};

export type SnapshotArtifact = { name: string; path: string; bytes: number; sha256: string };

export type SnapshotManifest = {
  id: string;
  runId: string;
  takenAt: string;
  fromVersion: string;
  driver: string;
  artifacts: SnapshotArtifact[];
  /** D3: which services were stopped, and the supervisor drain proof (Task 6). */
  quiesce: { stoppedServices: string[]; drainProofs: Record<string, unknown> };
};

export type DoctorCheckSummary = { name: string; status: 'ok' | 'warn' | 'fail'; category?: string };

/** D5's baseline half — captured before the swap, compared after by compareDoctor (Task 12). */
export type DoctorBaseline = {
  capturedAt: string;
  status: string;
  healthScore: number;
  checks: DoctorCheckSummary[];
};

/** Threaded through every playbook step and the host executor (Task 9, Task 11). */
export type UpgradeContext = {
  runId: string;
  organizationId: string | null;
  fromVersion: string;
  toVersion: string;
  journalDir: string;
};
```

`src/upgrade/journal.ts`:
```ts
import { mkdir, appendFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import type { UpgradeEvent } from './types.ts';

export type Journal = { dir: string; runId: string; path: string };

/**
 * D2: this is the authority while the control plane is down. One journal
 * per run, one writer (scripts/upgrade.ts, the host CLI, per D1) — no
 * advisory lock is needed the way S3's file-plane writes need one, because
 * concurrent writers were never possible here in the first place.
 */
export async function openJournal(dir: string, runId: string): Promise<Journal> {
  const runDir = join(dir, runId);
  await mkdir(runDir, { recursive: true });
  return { dir, runId, path: join(runDir, 'journal.ndjson') };
}

export async function appendEvent(
  journal: Journal,
  input: { step: string; level?: UpgradeEvent['level']; message: string; data?: unknown },
): Promise<UpgradeEvent> {
  const existing = await readJournal(journal.dir, journal.runId);
  const seq = existing.length > 0 ? existing[existing.length - 1]!.seq + 1 : 1;
  const event: UpgradeEvent = {
    seq, at: new Date().toISOString(), step: input.step,
    level: input.level ?? 'info', message: input.message,
    ...(input.data !== undefined ? { data: input.data } : {}),
  };
  await appendFile(journal.path, JSON.stringify(event) + '\n');
  return event;
}

/**
 * A truncated LAST line is a crash mid-write (the process died between the
 * write() and its trailing '\n') and is dropped silently — it was never a
 * committed event, so dropping it is not data loss. A malformed line
 * anywhere else in the file is corruption of an already-committed event and
 * throws loudly; silently skipping it would mean replay quietly disagreeing
 * with what a prior reconcile pass (Task 13) already persisted.
 */
export async function readJournal(dir: string, runId: string): Promise<UpgradeEvent[]> {
  const path = join(dir, runId, 'journal.ndjson');
  const file = Bun.file(path);
  if (!(await file.exists())) return [];
  const lines = (await file.text()).split('\n').filter((l) => l.length > 0);
  const events: UpgradeEvent[] = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
    const line = lines[i]!;
    try {
      events.push(JSON.parse(line) as UpgradeEvent);
    } catch {
      if (i !== lines.length - 1) {
        throw new Error(`journal ${path} is corrupt at line ${i + 1}: ${line.slice(0, 80)}`);
      }
      // else: a truncated trailing write — drop it, do not throw.
    }
  }
  return events;
}
```

`src/upgrade/manifest.ts`:
```ts
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { readFile, stat } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { basename, join } from 'node:path';
import type { SnapshotArtifact, SnapshotManifest } from './types.ts';

export async function sha256File(path: string): Promise<string> {
  return createHash('sha256').update(await readFile(path)).digest('hex');
}

export type BuildManifestInput = {
  id: string;
  runId: string;
  fromVersion: string;
  driver: string;
  /** `path` is where the artifact lives RIGHT NOW, on the machine building
   * the manifest — only the basename is stored, so the manifest is portable
   * to wherever the snapshot directory is restored. */
  artifacts: Array<{ name: string; path: string }>;
  quiesce: SnapshotManifest['quiesce'];
};

/** Honesty rule 10: a manifest that names an artifact it never hashed is
 * worse than no manifest — it looks like a verified backup and is not. */
export async function buildManifest(input: BuildManifestInput): Promise<SnapshotManifest> {
  const artifacts: SnapshotArtifact[] = [];
  for (const a of input.artifacts) {
    let bytes: number;
    try {
      bytes = (await stat(a.path)).size;
    } catch {
      throw new Error(`buildManifest: artifact "${a.name}" is missing at ${a.path}`);
    }
    artifacts.push({ name: a.name, path: basename(a.path), bytes, sha256: await sha256File(a.path) });
  }
  return {
    id: input.id, runId: input.runId, takenAt: new Date().toISOString(),
    fromVersion: input.fromVersion, driver: input.driver, artifacts, quiesce: input.quiesce,
  };
}

/** The checksum half of honesty rule 10 — Gate C exercises the restore half. */
export async function verifyManifest(
  manifest: SnapshotManifest, dir: string,
): Promise<{ ok: boolean; mismatches: string[] }> {
  const mismatches: string[] = [];
  for (const artifact of manifest.artifacts) {
    const path = join(dir, artifact.path);
    let actual: string;
    try {
      actual = await sha256File(path);
    } catch {
      mismatches.push(`${artifact.name}: missing at ${path}`);
      continue;
    }
    if (actual !== artifact.sha256) mismatches.push(`${artifact.name}: sha256 mismatch`);
  }
  return { ok: mismatches.length === 0, mismatches };
}
```

## Task 5: Snapshot driver seam + the docker-volume driver

**Files:**
- Create: `src/upgrade/snapshot.ts`
- Test: `test/unit/snapshot.test.ts`
- Test: `test/integration/snapshot.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `SnapshotManifest`, `SnapshotArtifact` (types, Task 4, `src/upgrade/types.ts`);
  `buildManifest`, `sha256File`, `verifyManifest` (Task 4, `src/upgrade/manifest.ts`).
- Produces: `interface SnapshotDriver`; `type SnapshotContext`; `type Runner`; `dockerVolumeDriver`;
  `SNAPSHOT_VOLUMES`; `SNAPSHOT_DATABASES`; `dumpDatabase`; `tarVolumeToFile`; `untarFileIntoVolume`;
  `restoreDatabase`.

> **L7 and D3 apply here.** `scripts/backup.ts:87-95` admits its own atomicity is "the portable
> minimum" and `:209-218` (RECON) confirms `gbrain-home` — the file plane — is never captured at
> all, which is why `scripts/restore.ts:202-246` has to re-derive `config.json` from the control DB
> instead of restoring it. This driver captures all four of D3's artifacts, `gbrain-home` included,
> so rollback restores the file plane directly.

**A note on `SnapshotContext` vs the plan's own `UpgradeContext`.** The driver does not take the
whole `UpgradeContext` Task 4/11 build (`runId, fromVersion, toVersion, dryRun, upgradeDir, envPath,
compose, journal, now`). It takes a narrower, driver-specific `SnapshotContext` — just the fields a
snapshot actually needs — so Task 11's `RunDeps.snapshot(ctx, drain)` wrapper projects the big
context down before calling `dockerVolumeDriver.take(...)`, and so Gate A (Task 15) can call
`dockerVolumeDriver.take({ runId, volumes: [vol], databases: [], outDir })` directly against a
throwaway scratch volume with no compose stack at all. `fromVersion` is **not** a `SnapshotContext`
field — `take()` reads `process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0'` itself, the same convention every
other runtime reader in this repo already uses (RECON, "Version pinning plumbing").

> **Corrected at assembly — one signature for `verifyManifest`.** Three blocks of this plan were
> drafted in parallel and reached for three different shapes: `verifyManifest(manifest, dir)`,
> `verifyManifest(ctx.upgradeDir, manifest)`, and a one-argument `verifyManifest(manifest)`. The
> canonical signature, defined in Task 4 and used everywhere, is
> **`verifyManifest(manifest, dir?)` returning `{ ok, mismatches }`** — `dir` defaults to the
> manifest's own recorded directory, so the one-argument call remains valid wherever artifact paths
> are absolute (the docker-volume driver and both gates). Any call site that reads `failed` instead
> of `mismatches`, or passes the directory first, is stale and must be fixed rather than accommodated.

```ts
import { test, expect, describe, beforeEach, afterEach } from 'bun:test';
import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import {
  dockerVolumeDriver, dumpDatabase, tarVolumeToFile,
  SNAPSHOT_DATABASES, SNAPSHOT_VOLUMES,
} from '../../src/upgrade/snapshot.ts';
import type { Runner, RunResult } from '../../src/upgrade/snapshot.ts';

/** Records every argv it is asked to run — no Docker, no Postgres. */
class FakeRunner implements Runner {
  calls: string[][] = [];
  async run(argv: string[]): Promise<RunResult> {
    this.calls.push(argv);
    return { exitCode: 0, stdout: '', stderr: '' };
  }
  async captureToFile(argv: string[], outPath: string): Promise<void> {
    this.calls.push(argv);
    await writeFile(outPath, `fake output for: ${argv.join(' ')}`);
  }
  async feedFile(argv: string[]): Promise<RunResult> {
    this.calls.push(argv);
    return { exitCode: 0, stdout: '', stderr: '' };
  }
}

const COMPOSE = ['docker', 'compose', '-f', 'compose/docker-compose.yml'];

let dir: string;
beforeEach(async () => { dir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'bs-snap-')); });
afterEach(async () => { await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }); });

describe('SNAPSHOT_DATABASES / SNAPSHOT_VOLUMES', () => {
  test('name exactly what D3 promises', () => {
    expect(SNAPSHOT_DATABASES).toEqual(['gbrain', 'control']);
    expect(SNAPSHOT_VOLUMES).toEqual(['brainstand_brain-repos', 'brainstand_gbrain-home']);
  });
});

describe('the exact argv for each artifact', () => {
  test('dumpDatabase runs pg_dump through compose exec postgres, never a shell string', async () => {
    const runner = new FakeRunner();
    await dumpDatabase(runner, COMPOSE, 'gbrain', join(dir, 'brain.sql'));
    expect(runner.calls).toEqual([
      [...COMPOSE, 'exec', '-T', 'postgres', 'pg_dump', '-U', 'gbrain', '-d', 'gbrain'],
    ]);
  });

  test('tarVolumeToFile uses a throwaway busybox container, never `compose exec` into a service', async () => {
    const runner = new FakeRunner();
    await tarVolumeToFile(runner, 'brainstand_gbrain-home', dir, 'gbrain-home.tar');
    expect(runner.calls).toEqual([[
      'docker', 'run', '--rm',
      '-v', 'brainstand_gbrain-home:/src:ro', '-v', `${dir}:/out`,
      'busybox', 'tar', 'cf', '/out/gbrain-home.tar', '-C', '/src', '.',
    ]]);
    expect(runner.calls[0]!.join(' ')).not.toMatch(/gbrain-serve|gbrain-worker|gbrain-ops/);
  });

  test('take() produces all four named artifacts through those two primitives only', async () => {
    const runner = new FakeRunner();
    const manifest = await dockerVolumeDriver.take(
      { runId: 'run-1', volumes: SNAPSHOT_VOLUMES, databases: SNAPSHOT_DATABASES, outDir: dir, compose: COMPOSE },
      runner,

    expect(manifest.runId).toBe('run-1');

    const tarCalls = runner.calls.filter((c) => c[0] === 'docker' && c[1] === 'run');
    expect(tarCalls).toHaveLength(2);

describe('verify and restore', () => {

    const runner = new FakeRunner();
    const manifest = await dockerVolumeDriver.take(

      runner,
    );
    const corrupted = {
      ...manifest,
      artifacts: manifest.artifacts.map((a) =>
        a.name === 'brain-db' ? { ...a, sha256: '0'.repeat(64) } : a),
    };

  test('restore refuses a manifest that does not verify, before it runs a single command', async () => {
    const runner = new FakeRunner();
    const ctx = { runId: 'run-3', volumes: SNAPSHOT_VOLUMES, databases: SNAPSHOT_DATABASES, outDir: dir, compose: COMPOSE };
    const manifest = await dockerVolumeDriver.take(ctx, runner);
    const corrupted = {
      ...manifest,
      artifacts: manifest.artifacts.map((a) =>
        a.name === 'control-db' ? { ...a, sha256: '0'.repeat(64) } : a),
    };
    const restoreRunner = new FakeRunner();
    await expect(dockerVolumeDriver.restore(ctx, corrupted, restoreRunner))
      .rejects.toThrow(/verification failed/);
    expect(restoreRunner.calls).toHaveLength(0);
  });

  test('describe() lists the four production artifact names with zero I/O', () => {
    expect(dockerVolumeDriver.describe().map((a) => a.name).sort()).toEqual(
      ['brain-db', 'brain-repos', 'control-db', 'gbrain-home'],
    );
  });
});
```
