- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

`src/upgrade/snapshot.ts`:
```ts
import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
import { mkdir, stat, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { basename, dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import type { SnapshotArtifact, SnapshotManifest } from './types.ts';
import { buildManifest, sha256File, verifyManifest } from './manifest.ts';

/**
 * The four artifacts D3 defines. `SnapshotContext.databases`/`.volumes`
 * default to these in production (Task 11); Gate A (Task 15) overrides both
 * with a single throwaway volume so the driver's own argv path is exercised
 * against a scratch volume, never a product service.
 */
export const SNAPSHOT_DATABASES = ['gbrain', 'control'] as const;
export const SNAPSHOT_VOLUMES = ['brainstand_brain-repos', 'brainstand_gbrain-home'] as const;

const DB_ARTIFACT_NAME: Record<string, string> = { gbrain: 'brain-db', control: 'control-db' };
const VOLUME_ARTIFACT_NAME: Record<string, string> = {
  'brainstand_brain-repos': 'brain-repos',
  'brainstand_gbrain-home': 'gbrain-home',
};
const artifactNameForDb = (db: string): string => DB_ARTIFACT_NAME[db] ?? db;
const artifactNameForVolume = (volume: string): string => VOLUME_ARTIFACT_NAME[volume] ?? volume;
const sanitize = (s: string): string => s.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9._-]/g, '_');

// Mirrors scripts/backup.ts:21-25 exactly — this is that file's successor.
const DEFAULT_COMPOSE = [
  'docker', 'compose',
  ...(existsSync('.env') ? ['--env-file', '.env'] : []),
  '-f', 'compose/docker-compose.yml',
];

/**
 * What a snapshot needs to know — deliberately NOT the full `UpgradeContext`
 * (Task 4/11: runId, fromVersion, toVersion, dryRun, upgradeDir, envPath,
 * compose, journal, now). Task 11's own `RunDeps.snapshot(ctx, drain)`
 * wrapper projects the big context down to this one before calling take().
 */
export type SnapshotContext = {
  runId: string;
  volumes: readonly string[];
  databases: readonly string[];
  outDir: string;
  /** docker compose argv prefix. Defaults to the production stack. */
  compose?: readonly string[];
};

export type RunResult = { exitCode: number; stdout: string; stderr: string };

/** The seam a fake occupies in tests; production wires real Bun.spawn calls. */
export type Runner = {
  run(argv: string[], timeoutMs?: number): Promise<RunResult>;
  captureToFile(argv: string[], outPath: string, timeoutMs?: number): Promise<void>;
  feedFile(argv: string[], inputPath: string, timeoutMs?: number): Promise<RunResult>;
};

async function bunRun(argv: string[], timeoutMs = 900_000): Promise<RunResult> {
  const p = Bun.spawn(argv, { stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const timer = setTimeout(() => p.kill(), timeoutMs);
  try {
    const [stdout, stderr, exitCode] = await Promise.all([
      new Response(p.stdout).text(), new Response(p.stderr).text(), p.exited,
    ]);
    return { exitCode, stdout, stderr };
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}

async function bunCaptureToFile(argv: string[], outPath: string, timeoutMs = 900_000): Promise<void> {
  const p = Bun.spawn(argv, { stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const timer = setTimeout(() => p.kill(), timeoutMs);
  try {
    const [stdout, stderr, exitCode] = await Promise.all([
      new Response(p.stdout).arrayBuffer(), new Response(p.stderr).text(), p.exited,
    ]);
    if (exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`${argv.slice(0, 6).join(' ')}… failed (${exitCode}): ${stderr}`);
    await writeFile(outPath, new Uint8Array(stdout));
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}

async function bunFeedFile(argv: string[], inputPath: string, timeoutMs = 900_000): Promise<RunResult> {
  const p = Bun.spawn(argv, { stdin: Bun.file(inputPath), stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const timer = setTimeout(() => p.kill(), timeoutMs);
  try {
    const [stdout, stderr, exitCode] = await Promise.all([
      new Response(p.stdout).text(), new Response(p.stderr).text(), p.exited,
    ]);
    return { exitCode, stdout, stderr };
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}

const defaultRunner: Runner = { run: bunRun, captureToFile: bunCaptureToFile, feedFile: bunFeedFile };

/** `pg_dump` of one database, through the ONE process quiesce() leaves alive. */
export async function dumpDatabase(
  runner: Runner, compose: readonly string[], db: string, outPath: string,
): Promise<void> {
  await runner.captureToFile(
    [...compose, 'exec', '-T', 'postgres', 'pg_dump', '-U', 'gbrain', '-d', db], outPath,
  );
}

/** `psql` drop+create+load, same shape as scripts/restore.ts:265-286. */
export async function restoreDatabase(
  runner: Runner, compose: readonly string[], db: string, dumpPath: string,
): Promise<void> {
  const psql = (sql: string) => runner.run([
    ...compose, 'exec', '-T', 'postgres', 'psql', '-U', 'gbrain', '-d', 'postgres',
    '-v', 'ON_ERROR_STOP=1', '-c', sql,
  ]);
  const drop = await psql(`DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS ${db} WITH (FORCE)`);
  if (drop.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`drop ${db} failed: ${drop.stderr}`);
  const create = await psql(`CREATE DATABASE ${db}`);
  if (create.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`create ${db} failed: ${create.stderr}`);
  const load = await runner.feedFile(
    [...compose, 'exec', '-T', 'postgres', 'psql', '-U', 'gbrain', '-d', db, '-v', 'ON_ERROR_STOP=1'],
    dumpPath,
  );
  if (load.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`restore ${db} failed: ${load.stderr}`);
}

/**
 * A throwaway `busybox` container mounts the named volume directly (D3) — no
 * service needs to be running. This is the seam Slice 5's VM/ZFS driver
 * replaces; it must not grow a second gbrain-facing implementation now.
 */
export async function tarVolumeToFile(
  runner: Runner, volume: string, outDir: string, fileName: string,
): Promise<void> {
  const r = await runner.run([
    'docker', 'run', '--rm',
    '-v', `${volume}:/src:ro`, '-v', `${outDir}:/out`,
    'busybox', 'tar', 'cf', `/out/${fileName}`, '-C', '/src', '.',
  ], 900_000);
  if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`tar of ${volume} failed (${r.exitCode}): ${r.stderr}`);
}

export async function untarFileIntoVolume(
  runner: Runner, volume: string, inDir: string, fileName: string,
): Promise<void> {
  const r = await runner.run([
    'docker', 'run', '--rm',
    '-v', `${volume}:/dst`, '-v', `${inDir}:/in:ro`,
    'busybox', 'tar', 'xf', `/in/${fileName}`, '-C', '/dst',
  ], 900_000);
  if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`untar into ${volume} failed (${r.exitCode}): ${r.stderr}`);
}

export interface SnapshotDriver {
  id: string;
  /** The production artifact list, with zero I/O — used by `plan`'s dry run. */
  describe(): { name: string }[];
  take(ctx: SnapshotContext, runner?: Runner): Promise<SnapshotManifest>;
  restore(ctx: SnapshotContext, manifest: SnapshotManifest, runner?: Runner): Promise<void>;
  verify(manifest: SnapshotManifest): Promise<boolean>;
}

/**
 * The default driver. Shells docker with argv arrays through throwaway
 * containers so no service needs to be running (D3) — a ZFS or cloud-disk
 * driver drops in later behind the same `SnapshotDriver` interface without
 * touching `runUpgrade`. That seam is what Slice 5 needs; it is not a
 * license to start a second implementation now (YAGNI applies to drivers,
 * never to the interface).
 */
export const dockerVolumeDriver: SnapshotDriver = {
  id: 'docker-volume',

  describe(): { name: string }[] {
    return [
      ...SNAPSHOT_DATABASES.map((db) => ({ name: artifactNameForDb(db) })),
      ...SNAPSHOT_VOLUMES.map((v) => ({ name: artifactNameForVolume(v) })),
    ];
  },

  async take(ctx: SnapshotContext, runner: Runner = defaultRunner): Promise<SnapshotManifest> {
    await mkdir(ctx.outDir, { recursive: true });
    const compose = ctx.compose ?? DEFAULT_COMPOSE;
    const artifacts: SnapshotArtifact[] = [];

    for (const db of ctx.databases) {
      const name = artifactNameForDb(db);
      const path = join(ctx.outDir, `${sanitize(name)}.sql`);
      await dumpDatabase(runner, compose, db, path);
      artifacts.push({ name, path, bytes: (await stat(path)).size, sha256: await sha256File(path) });
    }
    for (const volume of ctx.volumes) {
      const name = artifactNameForVolume(volume);
      const fileName = `${sanitize(name)}.tar`;
      await tarVolumeToFile(runner, volume, ctx.outDir, fileName);
      const path = join(ctx.outDir, fileName);
      artifacts.push({ name, path, bytes: (await stat(path)).size, sha256: await sha256File(path) });
    }

    const manifest = buildManifest({
      id: randomUUID(),
      takenAt: new Date().toISOString(),
      fromVersion: process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0',
      driver: 'docker-volume',
      artifacts,
      // Attached by the caller: Task 11's RunDeps.snapshot(ctx, drain) merges
      // the real quiesce evidence in after take() returns. This driver only
      // knows the artifacts it captured, not why the stack was quiet when it
      // captured them.
      quiesce: null,
      runId: ctx.runId,
    });
    await writeFile(join(ctx.outDir, 'manifest.json'), `${JSON.stringify(manifest, null, 2)}\n`);
    return manifest;
  },

  async restore(
    ctx: SnapshotContext, manifest: SnapshotManifest, runner: Runner = defaultRunner,
  ): Promise<void> {
    const { ok, mismatches } = await verifyManifest(manifest);
    if (!ok) {
      throw new Error(
        `refusing to restore manifest ${manifest.id}: verification failed for [${failed.join(', ')}] ` +
        '(honesty rule 10 — a snapshot nobody can verify is not a backup).',
      );
    }
    const compose = ctx.compose ?? DEFAULT_COMPOSE;
    const byName = new Map(manifest.artifacts.map((a) => [a.name, a]));

    for (const db of ctx.databases) {
      const artifact = byName.get(artifactNameForDb(db));
      if (!artifact) throw new Error(`manifest ${manifest.id} has no artifact for database '${db}'`);
      await restoreDatabase(runner, compose, db, artifact.path);
    }
    for (const volume of ctx.volumes) {
      const artifact = byName.get(artifactNameForVolume(volume));
      if (!artifact) throw new Error(`manifest ${manifest.id} has no artifact for volume '${volume}'`);
      await untarFileIntoVolume(runner, volume, dirname(artifact.path), basename(artifact.path));
    }
  },

  async verify(manifest: SnapshotManifest): Promise<boolean> {
    return (await verifyManifest(manifest)).ok;
  },
};
```

The assertion that matters is `take() produces all four named artifacts through those two primitives only` — specifically the two `not.toMatch(/exec -T gbrain/)` checks on the tar calls. If either ever matches, the driver has started reaching into a running gbrain service for the file plane instead of a throwaway container, which quietly reintroduces the exact race D3 exists to rule out (a service writing to `gbrain-home` while the tar runs). Do not relax that assertion — fix the call site instead.

`test/integration/snapshot.test.ts`:

```ts
import { test, expect, describe } from 'bun:test';
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { tarVolumeToFile, untarFileIntoVolume } from '../../src/upgrade/snapshot.ts';
import type { Runner, RunResult } from '../../src/upgrade/snapshot.ts';

const dockerAvailable = Bun.which('docker') !== null;
const SKIP_SNAPSHOT_DOCKER_CHECK_ENV = 'BRAINSTAND_ALLOW_SKIP_SNAPSHOT_DOCKER_CHECK';

async function run(argv: string[]): Promise<RunResult> {
  const p = Bun.spawn(argv, { stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const [stdout, stderr, exitCode] = await Promise.all([
    new Response(p.stdout).text(), new Response(p.stderr).text(), p.exited,
  ]);
  return { exitCode, stdout, stderr };
}
const realRunner: Runner = {
  run: (argv) => run(argv),
  captureToFile: async () => { throw new Error('not exercised by this test'); },
  feedFile: async () => { throw new Error('not exercised by this test'); },
};

describe('docker-volume driver: real round trip', () => {
  test.skipIf(!dockerAvailable)(
    'a tar out of a scratch volume, followed by an untar into a fresh one, round-trips the content',
    async () => {
      const volume = `bs-snap-scratch-${randomUUID().slice(0, 8)}`;
      const restoreVolume = `${volume}-restore`;
      const hostDir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'bs-snap-it-'));
      try {
        expect((await run(['docker', 'volume', 'create', volume])).exitCode).toBe(0);
        expect((await run(['docker', 'volume', 'create', restoreVolume])).exitCode).toBe(0);

        const seed = await run([
          'docker', 'run', '--rm', '-v', `${volume}:/src`, 'busybox',
          'sh', '-c', 'echo "snapshot-round-trip" > /src/marker.txt',
        ]);
        expect(seed.exitCode).toBe(0);

        await tarVolumeToFile(realRunner, volume, hostDir, 'scratch.tar');
        await untarFileIntoVolume(realRunner, restoreVolume, hostDir, 'scratch.tar');

        const read = await run([
          'docker', 'run', '--rm', '-v', `${restoreVolume}:/dst:ro`, 'busybox', 'cat', '/dst/marker.txt',
        ]);
        expect(read.exitCode).toBe(0);
        expect(read.stdout.trim()).toBe('snapshot-round-trip');
      } finally {
        await run(['docker', 'volume', 'rm', '-f', volume]);
        await run(['docker', 'volume', 'rm', '-f', restoreVolume]);
        await rm(hostDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
      }
    },
    120_000,
  );

  // Same convention as test/integration/compose-invariants.test.ts: a skipped
  // Docker check must be loud, not silent.
  test('the Docker-dependent round trip above is not silently skipped', () => {
    if (dockerAvailable) return;
    if (process.env[SKIP_SNAPSHOT_DOCKER_CHECK_ENV] === '1') return;
    throw new Error(
      'Docker is unavailable, so the scratch-volume snapshot round trip was skipped. If that is ' +
      `genuinely intended for this run, set ${SKIP_SNAPSHOT_DOCKER_CHECK_ENV}=1 to acknowledge it.`,
    );
  });
});
```

## Task 6: Quiesce and the drain proof

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — U9 is CONTRADICTED at this pin: a clean drain does not emit `stopped`.**
> A clean SIGTERM stop at `0.50.0.0` writes a `shutting_down` audit event with `exit_code: 0` and
> **no** `stopped` event; `stopped` was observed only on the `supervisor_lock_lost` path
> (`shutting_down` then `stopped`, both `exit_code: 4`). Consequence: `reason:'drained'` was never a
> clean-drain proof (U9 stands), and a `stopped`-only reader would report `unproven` — or, worse,
> inherit a stale `stopped` from an earlier lock-lost episode and misclassify a later clean drain. The
> executed fix scopes the verdict to the quiesce **window** (only events at or after the stop request
> may decide it) and accepts the pin's real vocabulary: in-window `shutting_down{exit_code:0}`, or
> `stopped{exit_code:0, reason in SIGTERM|SIGINT}`, **and** `jobs supervisor status --json` agreeing
> (`running === false`, no live workers). Absence of evidence is `unproven`, never `clean`. Measured on
> the live stack: a clean quiesce window of 764 ms in Gate C (F1's controlled run measured 1150 ms).
> See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` F1 and U9.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/upgrade/quiesce.ts`
- Test: `test/unit/quiesce.test.ts`
- Test: `test/integration/quiesce.test.ts`
- Modify: `scripts/backup.ts`
- Modify: `scripts/restore.ts`
- Modify: `src/ops/fileplane.ts`
- Modify: `src/ops/server.ts`
- Modify: `src/control/opsClient.ts`
- Modify: `compose/docker-compose.yml`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `UpgradeContext` (type, Task 4, `src/upgrade/types.ts` — `{runId, fromVersion,
  toVersion, dryRun, upgradeDir, envPath, compose, journal, now}`, confirmed by Task 11's own test
  fixture); `callOps` (existing, `src/control/opsClient.ts`).
- Produces: `QUIESCE_ORDER`; `quiesce(ctx): Promise<QuiesceEvidence>`; `resume(ctx, services?):
  Promise<void>`; `verifySupervisorDrained(opts?): Promise<SupervisorDrainProof>`; `type OpsCaller`;
  `type SupervisorDrainProof`; `type QuiesceEvidence`; the `/fileplane/audit-tail` ops route;
  `auditTail` on `src/control/opsClient.ts`.

> **U9 and honesty rule 3 apply here, and they are the whole point of this task.** `jobs supervisor
> stop` (`src/commands/jobs.ts:1900-1950`, RECON) reports `reason:'drained'` on nothing stronger than
> "the PID vanished within 40s" — it cannot distinguish a clean `SIGTERM` exit from a `MAX_CRASHES`
> exit in the same window. The proof has to come from somewhere the supervisor itself wrote it: the
> audit log's own `stopped` event, cross-checked against `jobs supervisor status --json`. Exit code
> `4` (`LOCK_LOST`, L3) gets its own classification — "someone else owns this" — never folded into
> "crashed".

**Reaching the audit log.** `${GBRAIN_AUDIT_DIR:-~/.gbrain/audit}/supervisor-YYYY-Www.jsonl` (RECON)
is a file-plane read. There is **no existing ops route that can serve it** — every route in
`src/ops/server.ts:119-204` is either the allowlisted `/rpc` (bound to gbrain's own CLI flags) or a
handful of dedicated, narrowly-scoped file routes, none of which touch the audit directory. This
task adds exactly one more: `POST /fileplane/audit-tail { lines }` → `{ lines: string[] }`. It is a
**read**, so it takes no advisory lock (S3 gates writes); its only input is an integer line count —
the path itself is computed server-side from `GBRAIN_AUDIT_DIR`/`GBRAIN_HOME`, never accepted from
the caller, so unlike `takeGrantCredentialsFile` there is no traversal surface to defend at all. To
make the path itself deterministic rather than depending on how `~` resolves inside each container,
this task also pins `GBRAIN_AUDIT_DIR: /srv/gbrain-home/.gbrain/audit` in
`compose/docker-compose.yml`'s `x-gbrain-env` anchor — the same volume `gbrain-ops` and
`gbrain-worker` already share, so ops can read what the worker's supervisor wrote without gaining
any new mount (S2 is unchanged: ops already holds RW `GBRAIN_HOME`).

**Reaching ops from `scripts/upgrade.ts` in production.** `quiesce(ctx)` runs on the host (D1), and
production publishes **no** ops host port — only `compose/docker-compose.dev.yml` does, for
`test/e2e` and Gate A's own convenience (`test/e2e/host-ops-env.ts`, and Task 15's harness-rule
blockquote). So `quiesce()` cannot just `fetch(OPS_RPC_URL)` from the host the way a dev/test caller
can. It builds its own `OpsCaller` via the **same relay `scripts/restore.ts:170-182` already uses**:
a `compose exec -T gbrain-ops bun -e <program>` that reads a JSON body off stdin, POSTs it to
`127.0.0.1:3140` from *inside* the container, and prints only the parsed result back to the host —
never the request/response bodies verbatim (S3c). `verifySupervisorDrained` itself stays agnostic to
*how* it reaches ops: it takes an `OpsCaller` (or, by default, a direct HTTP one built from
`OPS_RPC_URL`/`OPS_RPC_TOKEN`, which is what Gate A's own calls — `verifySupervisorDrained()` with no
args — rely on in its dev/test environment). `quiesce()` is the one caller that must supply the
relay-based `OpsCaller`, because it is the one caller required to work in production.

**The sequencing constraint later tasks depend on.** Task 11 recreates only `gbrain-ops` after the
image swap, runs schema migrations and playbooks while it is the sole live gbrain process, and only
afterward recreates `gbrain-serve`, `gbrain-worker` and `control-plane` — then calls `resume(ctx)`
with no arguments, which is a no-op for everything `deps.recreate` already started and is what
actually brings `scheduler` back (the one service that call never named). `resume(ctx, services)`
supports an explicit subset for exactly this reason: a partial resume is a first-class operation,
not a special case bolted on for one caller.

`test/unit/quiesce.test.ts`:
```ts
import { test, expect, describe } from 'bun:test';
import { verifySupervisorDrained } from '../../src/upgrade/quiesce.ts';
import type { OpsCaller } from '../../src/upgrade/quiesce.ts';

function fakeOps(lines: string[], status: { running: boolean; workers: unknown[] } | null): OpsCaller {
  return {
    async auditTail(): Promise<string[]> { return lines; },
    async supervisorStatus() { return status; },
  };
}

describe('verifySupervisorDrained', () => {
  test('a clean SIGTERM exit with an empty worker list is a real drain', async () => {
    const ops = fakeOps(
      [JSON.stringify({ event: 'stopped', exit_code: 0, reason: 'SIGTERM' })],
      { running: false, workers: [] },
    );
    const proof = await verifySupervisorDrained({ ops });
    expect(proof.clean).toBe(true);
    expect(proof.exitCode).toBe(0);
    expect(proof.classification).toBe('clean');
  });

  // U9 / honesty rule 3: the assertion that matters. The PID vanishing inside
  // the poll window is NOT proof of a clean exit on its own.
  test('a MAX_CRASHES exit in the same window is never reported as clean', async () => {
    const ops = fakeOps(
      [JSON.stringify({ event: 'stopped', exit_code: 1, reason: 'MAX_CRASHES' })],
      { running: false, workers: [] },
    );
    const proof = await verifySupervisorDrained({ ops });
    expect(proof.clean).toBe(false);
    expect(proof.classification).toBe('crashed');
  });

  test('exit code 4 (LOCK_LOST) is its own classification, not folded into "crashed"', async () => {
    const ops = fakeOps(
      [JSON.stringify({ event: 'stopped', exit_code: 4, reason: 'SIGTERM' })],
      { running: false, workers: [] },
    );
    const proof = await verifySupervisorDrained({ ops });
    expect(proof.clean).toBe(false);
    expect(proof.classification).toBe('lock_lost');
  });

  test('no stopped event at all is "unproven", not silently clean', async () => {
    const ops = fakeOps([], { running: false, workers: [] });
    const proof = await verifySupervisorDrained({ ops });
    expect(proof.clean).toBe(false);
    expect(proof.classification).toBe('unproven');
    expect(proof.exitCode).toBeNull();
  });

  test('a clean audit event contradicted by a still-running status is not clean', async () => {
    const ops = fakeOps(
      [JSON.stringify({ event: 'stopped', exit_code: 0, reason: 'SIGTERM' })],
      { running: true, workers: [{ pid: 999 }] },
    );
    const proof = await verifySupervisorDrained({ ops });
    expect(proof.clean).toBe(false);
  });

  test('only the LAST stopped event counts, not an earlier one from a prior cycle', async () => {
    const ops = fakeOps(
      [
        JSON.stringify({ event: 'stopped', exit_code: 1, reason: 'MAX_CRASHES' }),
        JSON.stringify({ event: 'started' }),
        JSON.stringify({ event: 'stopped', exit_code: 0, reason: 'SIGTERM' }),
      ],
      { running: false, workers: [] },
    );
    const proof = await verifySupervisorDrained({ ops });
    expect(proof.clean).toBe(true);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

`src/upgrade/quiesce.ts`:
```ts
import { join } from 'node:path';
import type { UpgradeContext } from './types.ts';
import { callOps, auditTail as httpAuditTail } from '../control/opsClient.ts';

/** Stop order (D3): every writer drains before the last one — gbrain-ops —
 *  goes down, leaving Postgres as the only live process. resume() runs the
 *  reverse. */
export const QUIESCE_ORDER = [
  'scheduler', 'gbrain-worker', 'control-plane', 'gbrain-serve', 'gbrain-ops',
] as const;

/**
 * Generous, MEASURED bounds (L1), not the compose file's redeploy-tuned
 * defaults. `scheduler`'s compose `stop_grace_period` is 20s
 * (compose/docker-compose.yml:136) — right for a routine redeploy, wrong for
 * an upgrade quiesce: `executeRun` is always awaited to completion
 * (src/scheduler/main.ts, RECON) and a `dream` run can run up to 7200s. This
 * task does not make quiesce wait out a live run — that is Task 11
 * preflight's job (refuse the upgrade while a `schedule_runs` row is
 * `running`). This bound exists only so a run preflight already accepted as
 * short is never truncated by a race against quiesce's own SIGTERM.
 */
const STOP_GRACE_S: Record<(typeof QUIESCE_ORDER)[number], number> = {
  scheduler: 90,
  'gbrain-worker': 90, // matches scripts/backup.ts's existing precedent exactly
  'control-plane': 30,
  // U12: AT THIS PIN, serve --http's server.close() has no timeout bound —
  // v0.50.5.0 adds a hard 5s one. Give margin in case it never fires cleanly.
  'gbrain-serve': 30,
  'gbrain-ops': 30,
};

async function sh(argv: string[], timeoutMs: number): Promise<{ exitCode: number; stdout: string; stderr: string }> {
  const p = Bun.spawn(argv, { stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const timer = setTimeout(() => p.kill(), timeoutMs);
  try {
    const [stdout, stderr, exitCode] = await Promise.all([
      new Response(p.stdout).text(), new Response(p.stderr).text(), p.exited,
    ]);
    return { exitCode, stdout, stderr };
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}

/** Feed text to a command's stdin — argv array only; the text never reaches
 *  argv, so a credential in it is never a credential in `ps` (S3c). */
async function feedString(argv: string[], input: string, timeoutMs = 60_000) {
  const p = Bun.spawn(argv, { stdin: 'pipe', stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const timer = setTimeout(() => p.kill(), timeoutMs);
  try {
    await p.stdin.write(input);
    await p.stdin.end();
    const [stdout, stderr, exitCode] = await Promise.all([
      new Response(p.stdout).text(), new Response(p.stderr).text(), p.exited,
    ]);
    return { exitCode, stdout, stderr };
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}

export type OpsCaller = {
  auditTail(lines: number): Promise<string[]>;
  supervisorStatus(): Promise<{ running: boolean; workers: unknown[] } | null>;
};

/** Direct HTTP, opsClient.ts's own convention. Works wherever ops publishes
 *  a reachable port — compose/docker-compose.dev.yml does, for Gate A and
 *  test/e2e. This is the default `verifySupervisorDrained` falls back to. */
function httpOpsCaller(): OpsCaller {
  return {
    auditTail: (lines) => httpAuditTail(lines),
    async supervisorStatus() {
      const r = await callOps<{ running?: boolean; workers?: unknown[] }>(
        'jobs.supervisorStatus', {}, { json: true, timeoutMs: 30_000 },
      );
      return r.json ? { running: !!r.json.running, workers: r.json.workers ?? [] } : null;
    },
  };
}

/**
 * Production-safe: reach ops through `compose exec -T gbrain-ops bun -e`,
 * the same relay scripts/restore.ts:170-182 already uses. Production
 * publishes no ops host port (S2), so a bare host-side fetch to
 * OPS_RPC_URL only works in dev/test, never here.
 */
function relayOpsCaller(compose: readonly string[]): OpsCaller {
  const relay = async <T>(path: string, body: unknown): Promise<T> => {
    const program = `
      let input = '';
      for await (const chunk of process.stdin) input += chunk;
      const base = process.env.OPS_RPC_URL ?? 'http://127.0.0.1:3140';
      const res = await fetch(base + ${JSON.stringify(path)}, {
        method: 'POST',
        headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json', authorization: 'Bearer ' + process.env.OPS_RPC_TOKEN },
        body: input,
      });
      process.stdout.write(await res.text());
    `;
    const r = await feedString(
      [...compose, 'exec', '-T', 'gbrain-ops', 'bun', '-e', program], JSON.stringify(body),
    );
    if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`ops relay ${path} failed: ${r.stderr}`);
    return JSON.parse(r.stdout) as T;
  };
  return {
    auditTail: async (lines) => {
      const r = await relay<{ lines?: string[] }>('/fileplane/audit-tail', { lines });
      return Array.isArray(r.lines) ? r.lines : [];
    },
    supervisorStatus: async () => {
      const r = await relay<{ json: { running?: boolean; workers?: unknown[] } | null }>(
        '/rpc', { op: 'jobs.supervisorStatus', params: {}, json: true, timeoutMs: 30_000 },
      );
      return r.json ? { running: !!r.json.running, workers: r.json.workers ?? [] } : null;
    },
  };
}

export type SupervisorDrainProof = {
  clean: boolean;
  exitCode: number | null;
  reason: string | null;
  running: boolean | null;
  workers: unknown[];
  classification: 'clean' | 'crashed' | 'lock_lost' | 'unproven';
};

export type VerifyDrainedOptions = {
  /** Test-only: read audit lines from this LOCAL directory instead of
   *  fetching them through ops — Gate A's negative fixtures use this rather
   *  than inducing a real MAX_CRASHES in a live supervisor. */
  auditDir?: string;
  ops?: OpsCaller;
};

async function readLocalAuditDir(dir: string): Promise<string[]> {
  const { readdir, readFile } = await import('node:fs/promises');
  const names = (await readdir(dir)).filter((n) => n.endsWith('.jsonl')).sort();
  const lines: string[] = [];
  for (const name of names) {
    lines.push(...(await readFile(join(dir, name), 'utf8')).split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim() !== ''));
  }
  return lines;
}

/**
 * The load-bearing check (U9, honesty rule 3). The audit log's own `stopped`
 * event, PLUS a status check that agrees with it, is the proof — never the
 * stop report alone. Exit code 4 (LOCK_LOST) gets its own classification
 * (L3): "someone else owns this," never an undifferentiated crash.
 */
export async function verifySupervisorDrained(
  opts: VerifyDrainedOptions = {},
): Promise<SupervisorDrainProof> {
  const ops = opts.ops ?? httpOpsCaller();
  const lines = opts.auditDir ? await readLocalAuditDir(opts.auditDir) : await ops.auditTail(50);
  const events = lines
    .map((l) => { try { return JSON.parse(l) as Record<string, unknown>; } catch { return null; } })
    .filter((e): e is Record<string, unknown> => e !== null);
  const last = [...events].reverse().find((e) => e.event === 'stopped') ?? null;

  const status = await ops.supervisorStatus();
  const running = status?.running ?? null;
  const workers = Array.isArray(status?.workers) ? status!.workers : [];

  if (!last) {
    return { clean: false, exitCode: null, reason: null, running, workers, classification: 'unproven' };
  }

  const exitCode = typeof last.exit_code === 'number' ? last.exit_code : null;
  const reason = typeof last.reason === 'string' ? last.reason : null;

  if (exitCode === 4) {
    return { clean: false, exitCode, reason, running, workers, classification: 'lock_lost' };
  }

  const cleanExit = exitCode === 0 && (reason === 'SIGTERM' || reason === 'SIGINT');
  const clean = cleanExit && running === false && workers.length === 0;
  return { clean, exitCode, reason, running, workers, classification: clean ? 'clean' : 'crashed' };
}

export type QuiesceEvidence = { stopped: string[]; proof: SupervisorDrainProof; elapsedMs: number };

/** Stop every writer, in order, leaving Postgres as the only live process (D3). */
export async function quiesce(ctx: UpgradeContext): Promise<QuiesceEvidence> {
  const t0 = Date.now();
  const stopped: string[] = [];
  let proof: SupervisorDrainProof | null = null;
  const ops = relayOpsCaller(ctx.compose);

  for (const svc of QUIESCE_ORDER) {
    const grace = STOP_GRACE_S[svc];
    const r = await sh([...ctx.compose, 'stop', '-t', String(grace), svc], (grace + 30) * 1000);
    if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`quiesce: stopping ${svc} failed: ${r.stderr}`);
    stopped.push(svc);
    // gbrain-ops and gbrain-worker share the gbrain-home volume, and ops
    // itself is not stopped until the LAST iteration of this loop — so this
    // read is still reachable right after the worker (the previous entry)
    // goes down.
    if (svc === 'gbrain-worker') proof = await verifySupervisorDrained({ ops });
  }

  if (!proof) throw new Error('quiesce finished without ever verifying the supervisor drain');
  return { stopped, proof, elapsedMs: Date.now() - t0 };
}

/**
 * Bring services back up. `services` defaults to everything, but a caller
 * may resume exactly one — a first-class operation, not a special case.
 * `up -d`, not `start`: a pin swap changes the image tag a stopped
 * container would otherwise keep ignoring.
 */
export async function resume(
  ctx: UpgradeContext, services: readonly string[] = QUIESCE_ORDER,
): Promise<void> {
  const order = [...QUIESCE_ORDER].reverse().filter((svc) => services.includes(svc));
  for (const svc of order) {
    const r = await sh([...ctx.compose, 'up', '-d', '--no-deps', svc], 120_000);
    if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`resume: starting ${svc} failed: ${r.stderr}`);
  }
}
```

`src/ops/fileplane.ts` — add the audit reader (insert after `resetBrainSchema`'s neighbors, near the
other read helpers):
```ts
const AUDIT_DIR = process.env.GBRAIN_AUDIT_DIR ?? join(GBRAIN_HOME, '.gbrain', 'audit');

/** ISO-8601 week file name, matching gbrain's own `supervisor-YYYY-Www.jsonl`
 *  naming (RECON, U9). */
function isoWeekAuditFile(date: Date): string {
  const d = new Date(Date.UTC(date.getFullYear(), date.getMonth(), date.getDate()));
  const dayNum = (d.getUTCDay() + 6) % 7;
  d.setUTCDate(d.getUTCDate() - dayNum + 3);
  const firstThursday = new Date(Date.UTC(d.getUTCFullYear(), 0, 4));
  const week = 1 + Math.round(
    ((d.getTime() - firstThursday.getTime()) / 86_400_000 - 3 + ((firstThursday.getUTCDay() + 6) % 7)) / 7,
  );
  return `supervisor-${d.getUTCFullYear()}-W${String(week).padStart(2, '0')}.jsonl`;
}

/**
 * Tail the CURRENT ISO week's supervisor audit log. A read, not a write —
 * S3 gates file-plane WRITES behind the advisory lock; this takes none. The
 * path is computed here, never accepted from the caller — no traversal
 * surface at all, unlike `takeGrantCredentialsFile`.
 */
export async function readSupervisorAuditTail(lines: number): Promise<string[]> {
  const path = join(AUDIT_DIR, isoWeekAuditFile(new Date()));
  const raw = await readIfExists(path);
  if (raw === null) return [];
  return raw.split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim() !== '').slice(-Math.max(1, lines));
}
```

```ts
/** Tail the supervisor's own audit log (Task 6) — the only way to tell a
 *  clean SIGTERM exit from a MAX_CRASHES exit inside the same drain window
 *  (U9). A dedicated route, not an allowlisted operation: it takes no argv,
 *  just a line count, and the path it reads is fixed server-side. */
export async function auditTail(lines = 50): Promise<string[]> {
  const payload = await post<{ lines?: string[] }>('/fileplane/audit-tail', { lines }, 30_000);
  return Array.isArray(payload.lines) ? payload.lines : [];
}
```

```ts
import { test, expect, describe } from 'bun:test';
import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
import { QUIESCE_ORDER, quiesce, resume } from '../../src/upgrade/quiesce.ts';
import type { UpgradeContext } from '../../src/upgrade/types.ts';

const COMPOSE = [
  'docker', 'compose',
  ...(existsSync('.env') ? ['--env-file', '.env'] as const : []),
  '-f', 'compose/docker-compose.yml',
];
const SKIP_QUIESCE_INTEGRATION_ENV = 'BRAINSTAND_ALLOW_SKIP_QUIESCE_INTEGRATION';

/** Same shape as test/integration/backup.test.ts's probeBackupStack —
 *  quiesce needs the real stack up, not merely the Docker CLI present. */
async function probeStack(): Promise<boolean> {
  const p = Bun.spawn(
    [...COMPOSE, 'ps', '--status', 'running', '-q', 'postgres', 'gbrain-ops', 'scheduler'],
    { stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' },
  );
  const timer = setTimeout(() => p.kill(), 5_000);
  try {
    const [stdout, exitCode] = await Promise.all([new Response(p.stdout).text(), p.exited]);
    if (exitCode !== 0) return false;
    return stdout.trim().split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean).length >= 3;
  } catch { return false; } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}
const stackAvailable = await probeStack();

async function runningServices(): Promise<string[]> {
  const p = Bun.spawn([...COMPOSE, 'ps', '--services', '--filter', 'status=running'],
    { stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const stdout = await new Response(p.stdout).text();
  await p.exited;
  return stdout.split('\n').map((s) => s.trim()).filter(Boolean);
}

describe('quiesce: real stack', () => {
  test('quiesce integration tests are not silently skipped', () => {
    if (stackAvailable) return;
    if (process.env[SKIP_QUIESCE_INTEGRATION_ENV] === '1') return;
    throw new Error(
      'The full compose stack is not up, so the real quiesce/resume round trip was skipped. Bring ' +
      'it up with docker compose --env-file .env -f compose/docker-compose.yml up -d --wait, or set ' +
      `${SKIP_QUIESCE_INTEGRATION_ENV}=1 to acknowledge skipping it for this run.`,
    );
  });

  test.skipIf(!stackAvailable)(
    'stops every service but postgres, in QUIESCE_ORDER, and resume brings them all back',
    async () => {
      const ctx = {
        runId: 'quiesce-it', fromVersion: process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0',
        toVersion: process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0', dryRun: false,
        upgradeDir: '/tmp/quiesce-it', envPath: '.env', compose: COMPOSE,
        journal: null, now: () => new Date(),
      } as unknown as UpgradeContext;

      const evidence = await quiesce(ctx);
      expect(evidence.stopped).toEqual([...QUIESCE_ORDER]);
      expect(await runningServices()).toEqual(['postgres']);

      await resume(ctx);
      const backUp = await runningServices();
      for (const svc of QUIESCE_ORDER) expect(backUp).toContain(svc);
    },
    600_000,
  );
});
```

## Task 7: Detect — the `checkUpdate` and `postUpgrade` operations

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — `verifyRuntimePin` was not re-pointed in Task 7; the exit-gate dispatch
> completed it.** Task 2 shipped `verifyRuntimePin` with `observed: null` by design and deferred
> populating it to Task 7's `checkUpdate.current_version`. Task 7 added the operation but left the
> function returning null; the Task 17 dispatch re-pointed it. Final shape: `declared` from `GBRAIN_PIN`,
> `observed` from `checkUpdate`'s `current_version` (only when the body has no `.error`), and
> `matches = observed === declared` — **null/null when ops is unreachable or `checkUpdate` returns
> `.error`**, so it never reports a false match (honesty rule 9's runtime cross-check is now real).
> `/api/health` exposes the same object as `pin`. See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` U1, U2.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/contracts/types.ts`
- Modify: `src/contracts/registry.ts`
- Modify: `src/ops/server.ts`
- Regenerate: `contracts/v0.50.0.0/operations.json`
- Create: `contracts/v0.50.5.0/operations.json`
- Create: `src/upgrade/detect.ts`
- Test: `test/unit/detect.test.ts`
- Create: `test/contract/upgrade.contract.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `OperationSpec`, `OPERATIONS` (existing, `src/contracts/types.ts`,
  `src/contracts/registry.ts`); `withAdvisoryLock` (existing, `src/ops/lock.ts`).
- Produces: `checkUpdate`, `postUpgrade` registry entries; `requiresLock?: boolean` on
  `OperationSpec`; `CheckUpdateResult`; `type DetectResult`; `detectUpdate(body)`.

> **U1 applies here, and it is the single most dangerous upstream fact in this slice.** `check-update`
> never sets a non-zero exit code — not even on a network failure (RECON, `check-update.ts:1-273`).
> `okExitCodes: [0]` below is correct because 0 is the **only** code this command ever emits, not
> because 0 means the check succeeded — a comment claiming the latter would be exactly the "hopeful
> `[0]`" this task exists to avoid. `detectUpdate` never reads the exit code at all.

**Gate A (Task 15) extends `test/contract/upgrade.contract.test.ts`.** This task creates the file
with the tests below; Task 15 appends `describe('Slice 3.5 seam gate A')`, which crosses the RPC
boundary at `gbrain-ops`'s published dev port (`compose/docker-compose.dev.yml`) rather than
`execOperation`'s slice0 harness, because the latter is never rebuilt from the working tree and so
never carries a regenerated `operations.json`. This task's own tests only need the pinned binary, so
`execOperation` is the right tool here; do not migrate them to the RPC boundary preemptively.

**`detectUpdate` is pure and synchronous on purpose.** It classifies an *already-fetched*
`check-update` body — it never calls the RPC itself. The caller (an admin route, `scripts/upgrade.ts`,
or a test) does `const r = await callOps('checkUpdate', {}, {json:true}); const d = detectUpdate(r.json);`
That split keeps `detectUpdate` trivially unit-testable with plain fixture objects and keeps the one
network/RPC call in exactly one place per caller.

**Registry work.**
- `checkUpdate` → argv `['check-update']`, `supportsJson: true`, `privilege: 'read-only'` (it needs
  no brain `DATABASE_URL`), `okExitCodes: [0]` per U1.
- `postUpgrade` → argv `['post-upgrade']`, no `--json` (it has none, U3's sibling fact for this
  command), `privilege: 'brain-db'` (it runs `apply-migrations` in-process).
- **Both** need `requiresLock: true` — check-update writes its own cache file
  (`~/.gbrain/last-update-check`) and post-upgrade does a lock-free read-modify-write of
  `config.json`'s `self_upgrade.*` subtree (U4) — so both are file-plane writers under S3. This is
  enforced structurally, not by caller discipline: `src/ops/server.ts`'s `/rpc` handler wraps the
  whole `runGbrain` invocation in `withAdvisoryLock` whenever `spec.requiresLock` is set, the exact
  lock every other file-plane write already serializes through (`src/ops/lock.ts`).

**Guard rail — do not add this to the scheduler.** `test/unit/scheduler-commands.test.ts:41-44` and
`test/unit/scheduler-presets.test.ts:73-75` already assert `check-update`/`checkUpdate` is not
schedulable. Adding the operation to the registry must **not** touch
`src/scheduler/commands.ts` or `src/scheduler/presets.ts` at all — those two tests stay green because
nothing in this task's diff comes near them. Run them explicitly in Step 4 to prove it.

```ts
export type OperationSpec = {
  id: string;
  argv: readonly string[];
  params: readonly ParamSpec[];
  supportsJson: boolean;
  supportsDryRun: boolean;
  privilege: Privilege;
  okExitCodes: readonly number[];
  /**
   * S3: some operations write the file plane even though they run through
   * the same `gbrain <argv>` spawn as everything else — post-upgrade
   * patches config.json with no lock of its own (U4), and check-update
   * writes its own cache file. When true, gbrain-ops wraps the whole
   * invocation in the CONTROL database's advisory lock (src/ops/lock.ts),
   * the same one every other file-plane write already serializes through.
   */
  requiresLock?: boolean;
};
```

`src/contracts/registry.ts` — append two entries to `OPERATIONS`:
```ts
  /**
   * U1: NEVER sets a non-zero exit code — not even on a network failure.
   * okExitCodes: [0] here means "0 is the only code this ever emits," not
   * "0 means success." Branch on `.json.error` (src/upgrade/detect.ts),
   * never on exitCode.
   *
   * S3: also a file-plane writer (its `~/.gbrain/last-update-check` cache),
   * so it runs under the ops advisory lock like post-upgrade below.
   */
  checkUpdate: {
    id: 'checkUpdate',
    argv: ['check-update'],
    params: [
      { name: 'refreshCache', flag: '--refresh-cache', kind: 'flag' },
    ],
    supportsJson: true,
    supportsDryRun: false,
    privilege: 'read-only',
    okExitCodes: [0],
    requiresLock: true,

   * enforced at the RPC boundary below, not left to caller discipline.

   * No --json: post-upgrade has none. Text output only — G3 forbids
   * parsing it; the caller reads the exit code alone.

  postUpgrade: {
    id: 'postUpgrade',
    argv: ['post-upgrade'],
    params: [],
    supportsJson: false,
```

`src/upgrade/detect.ts`:
```ts
export type CheckUpdateResult = {
  current_version: string;
  current_source: 'package-json';
  latest_version: string;
  update_available: boolean;
  upgrade_command: string;
  release_url: string;
  changelog_diff: string;
  /** ALWAYS '' at this pin (U2) — the source has no timestamp. Never render it. */
  published_at: string;
  error?: 'network_error' | 'no_releases';
};

export type DetectStatus = 'up_to_date' | 'available' | 'unknown';

export type DetectResult = {
  status: DetectStatus;
  error: 'network_error' | 'no_releases' | 'malformed_response' | null;
  currentVersion: string | null;
  /** Upstream's master VERSION file — a candidate, never a vetted release
   *  (U2, D7). An operator selects it explicitly; nothing here auto-upgrades. */
  candidateVersion: string | null;
  changelog: string | null;
  releaseUrl: string | null;
};

/**
 * Pure and synchronous: classifies an ALREADY-FETCHED check-update body. It
 * never calls the RPC itself and it never branches on an exit code (U1) —
 * check-update always exits 0, even on failure, so `.error` is the only
 * trustworthy signal.
 */
export function detectUpdate(body: unknown): DetectResult {
  const b = body as Partial<CheckUpdateResult> | null | undefined;
  if (!b || typeof b.current_version !== 'string') {
    return {
      status: 'unknown', error: 'malformed_response', currentVersion: null,
      candidateVersion: null, changelog: null, releaseUrl: null,
    };
  }
  if (b.error) {
    // Honesty rule 1: a check that never ran is not a pass. Never fall
    // through to "up to date" just because update_available reads false.
    return {
      status: 'unknown', error: b.error, currentVersion: b.current_version,
      candidateVersion: null, changelog: null, releaseUrl: null,
    };
  }
  if (!b.update_available) {
    return {
      status: 'up_to_date', error: null, currentVersion: b.current_version,
      candidateVersion: null, changelog: null, releaseUrl: null,
    };
  }
  return {
    status: 'available', error: null, currentVersion: b.current_version,
    candidateVersion: b.latest_version ?? null,
    changelog: b.changelog_diff ?? null, releaseUrl: b.release_url ?? null,
  };
}
```

Regenerate both contract artifacts (D8 — `operations.json` must exist at the target pin before the
control-plane image ever builds):
```bash
GBRAIN_VERSION=0.50.0.0 bun run operations:emit
GBRAIN_VERSION=0.50.5.0 bun run operations:emit
```

## Task 8: Capability snapshots and drift

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — `recordCapabilitySnapshot` shipped with no product caller; the drift panel is test-fed.** `src/upgrade/capability.ts` implements `recordCapabilitySnapshot` / `latestSnapshotFor` / `driftBetween`, and `src/control/routes/upgrades.ts`'s drift route reads them, but **no code under `src/` or `scripts/` calls `recordCapabilitySnapshot`** — the only writer is `test/integration/capability.test.ts`. The upgrade run therefore records no snapshots, so `capability_snapshots` is empty on a real deployment and `GET /api/upgrades/drift` returns **400** once `restore.e2e`'s volume cycle has cleared the control database; Gate C's B9/C10 pass only on a database a test or a prior run seeded. Wiring the recorder into the run (or stating that the panel is test-fed) is carried into Slice 4; it was not invented here. See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` F7.

> **Corrected 2026-09-17 — the recorder is now wired, and it was always meant to be.** Task 8 is not a test-fed panel: `scripts/upgrade.ts`'s `runUpgrade` captures each pin's capability into the run directory (`captureCapabilityForRun` → `capability-from.json` before the pre-upgrade digest, `capability-to.json` after the resume) and journals a `capability` event; `reconcileJournals` (`src/upgrade/reconcile.ts`) imports those files into `capability_snapshots`, exactly once per `(runId, seq)`, on the same journal-first path as the ledger and the checklist. `captureCapability` gained an injectable `exec` so the one capturer serves both the slice0 harness and the main stack. Proven on an empty table: one real run → two rows (`0.50.0.0`, `0.50.5.0`) → `GET /api/upgrades/drift` **200** with `{versionChanged:true,...profilesChanged:false}`. See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` F7's fix subsection.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/upgrade/capability.ts`
- Test: `test/integration/capability.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `diffCapability`, `CapabilitySnapshot`, `CapabilityDrift`, `HarnessAdapter` (existing,
  `src/contracts/capability.ts:94-154` — **reused, never reimplemented**); `getDb`, `schema`
  (existing, `src/control/db/client.ts`, `src/control/db/schema.ts:135-142`).
- Produces: `recordCapabilitySnapshot(db, version, snapshot)`; `latestSnapshotFor(db, version)`;
  `driftBetween(db, fromVersion, toVersion)` (also callable as `driftBetween(fromVersion, toVersion)`
  — see below); `class SingleSnapshotError`.

> **This fills a table that has shipped since Slice 1 and been written to zero times.** RECON: "Both
> tables are declared, migrated, allowlisted — and NEVER written. No `.insert(schema.capabilitySnapshots`
> … exists anywhere." `diffCapability` (`src/contracts/capability.ts:94-109`) already computes
> `versionChanged`/`toolsAdded`/`toolsRemoved`/`adaptersAdded`/`adaptersRemoved`/`profilesChanged` —
> that is the drift primitive to build on. This task's own job is only getting two rows into the
> table and comparing them; a second differ would be duplicate, untested code sitting next to a
> reviewed one.

**`driftBetween`'s two call shapes.** The coordinating spec for this task gives the signature
`driftBetween(db, fromVersion, toVersion)`. Gate C (Task 17, already drafted) calls it as
`driftBetween('0.50.0.0', '0.50.5.0')` — two strings, no `db`. Both are honored: `driftBetween` is
overloaded so the leading argument is either an explicit `Db` or (when it is a string) is treated as
`fromVersion`, with `db` defaulting to `getDb()` — the same module-level access pattern every route
file in `src/control/routes/*.ts` already uses. Neither call site is wrong; this is one function
serving both.

**The count discrepancy (RECON) — do not "fix" it.** Our own `captureCapability()` reports 27
operations / 136 tools / 13 adapters / 6 profiles at the pin; upstream's generated
`docs/TOOL_CATALOG.md` says "122 tools across 23 areas" and `HARNESS_ADAPTERS` lists 9 entries. These
count **different things** — our capture walks `.well-known/gbrain` and `--tools-json` against a live
server; upstream's doc is generated from its own source tree. What matters, and what U10 predicts, is
that **our own capture is identical across the two pins**, because every underlying upstream registry
file (`src/core/operations.ts`, `src/core/harness/registry.ts`, the 7 schema-pack YAMLs) is
byte-identical between v0.50.0.0 and v0.50.5.0. A non-empty `toolsAdded`/`toolsRemoved`/
`adaptersAdded`/`adaptersRemoved` on this delta is a **finding to record** in
`docs/slice-3.5-findings.md`, never a number this code silently adjusts to match.

**The single-snapshot trap.** With only one version's snapshot recorded, an empty `CapabilityDrift`
and a genuine "nothing changed" are structurally indistinguishable if the function just returns one.
`driftBetween` closes this by **throwing** `SingleSnapshotError` instead of returning a sentinel: a
caller cannot receive a drift-shaped value by accident when the comparison never actually happened —
which is a stronger guarantee than a `{comparable: false}` flag a caller could forget to check. The
future `GET /api/upgrades/drift` route (Task 13) is expected to catch this specific error and render
`{comparable: false, reason: 'single_snapshot', available}`; the comparable case wraps the return of
`driftBetween` in `{comparable: true, from, to, drift}` at the route layer — this task's own function
returns the bare `CapabilityDrift`, unwrapped, exactly matching `diffCapability`'s own shape.

**Append, not overwrite.** `id` is a fresh `randomUUID()` per call, and `gbrainVersion` carries **no**
uniqueness constraint on `capability_snapshots` (`schema.ts:135-142`) — unlike `playbook_ledger`,
which is `.unique()` on `gbrainVersion` (`schema.ts:146`) because it holds current state, not
history. `capability_snapshots` is a history of captures; `capturedAt` plus the existing
`capability_snapshots_version_idx` is how a reader finds "the latest one" without ever losing an
earlier capture. `recordCapabilitySnapshot` never updates an existing row — it only inserts.

```ts
  await getDb().delete(schema.capabilitySnapshots).where(eq(schema.capabilitySnapshots.gbrainVersion, TO));
  await closeDb();
});

    await recordCapabilitySnapshot(getDb(), FROM, snap(FROM, ['search', 'whoami']));
    await expect(driftBetween(getDb(), FROM, TO)).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(SingleSnapshotError);
    try {
      await driftBetween(getDb(), FROM, TO);
      throw new Error('unreachable: the line above must throw');
    } catch (e) {
      expect(e).toBeInstanceOf(SingleSnapshotError);
      expect((e as SingleSnapshotError).available).toEqual([FROM]);
    }
  });

  // U10: this delta ships zero capability drift. The assertion that matters
  // is that driftBetween reports EXACTLY versionChanged and nothing else —
  // a non-empty toolsAdded/toolsRemoved here would be a finding to record,
  // never a number to silently adjust.
  test('two identical-catalog snapshots at different versions report versionChanged and nothing else', async () => {
    await recordCapabilitySnapshot(getDb(), TO, snap(TO, ['search', 'whoami']));
    const drift = await driftBetween(getDb(), FROM, TO);
    expect(drift).toEqual({
      versionChanged: true, toolsAdded: [], toolsRemoved: [],
      adaptersAdded: [], adaptersRemoved: [], profilesChanged: false,
    });
    // The two-argument overload — no db — the shape Gate C's own C10 calls.
    expect(await driftBetween(FROM, TO)).toEqual(drift);
  });

  test('a second capture at the SAME version appends — it does not overwrite the first', async () => {
    await recordCapabilitySnapshot(getDb(), FROM, snap(FROM, ['search', 'whoami', 'dream']));
    const rows = await getDb().select().from(schema.capabilitySnapshots)
      .where(eq(schema.capabilitySnapshots.gbrainVersion, FROM));
    expect(rows.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);

    // latestSnapshotFor reads the newest one, not the first.
    const latest = await latestSnapshotFor(getDb(), FROM);
    expect((latest!.tools as { name: string }[]).map((t) => t.name)).toContain('dream');
  });
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

`src/upgrade/capability.ts`:
```ts
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import { desc, eq } from 'drizzle-orm';
import { getDb, schema } from '../control/db/client.ts';
import { diffCapability } from '../contracts/capability.ts';
import type { CapabilitySnapshot, CapabilityDrift, HarnessAdapter } from '../contracts/capability.ts';

type Db = ReturnType<typeof getDb>;

/**
 * A fresh row every call — never an update, never an upsert.
 * `capability_snapshots.gbrainVersion` carries NO uniqueness constraint
 * (schema.ts:135-142), unlike `playbook_ledger`'s `.unique()`
 * (schema.ts:146): playbook_ledger is CURRENT STATE per version;
 * capability_snapshots is a HISTORY of captures. `capturedAt` plus the
 * existing version index is how a caller reads "the latest one" without
 * ever losing an earlier capture.
 */
export async function recordCapabilitySnapshot(
  db: Db, version: string, snapshot: CapabilitySnapshot,
): Promise<{ id: string }> {
  const id = randomUUID();
  await db.insert(schema.capabilitySnapshots).values({
    id,
    gbrainVersion: version,
    toolsJson: snapshot.tools as any,
    harnesses: (snapshot.wellKnown?.adapters ?? null) as any,
    profiles: (snapshot.wellKnown?.profiles ?? null) as any,
    capturedAt: new Date(snapshot.capturedAt),
  });
  return { id };
}

export async function latestSnapshotFor(db: Db, version: string): Promise<CapabilitySnapshot | null> {
  const rows = await db.select().from(schema.capabilitySnapshots)
    .where(eq(schema.capabilitySnapshots.gbrainVersion, version))
    .orderBy(desc(schema.capabilitySnapshots.capturedAt))
    .limit(1);
  const row = rows[0];
  if (!row) return null;
  return {
    gbrainVersion: row.gbrainVersion,
    capturedAt: row.capturedAt.toISOString(),
    tools: row.toolsJson,
    wellKnown: { profiles: row.profiles ?? null, adapters: (row.harnesses as HarnessAdapter[] | null) ?? [] },
    // Not persisted here — the table has no schemaPacks column, and
    // diffCapability never reads it. The full snapshot (schemaPacks
    // included) still lands in contracts/v<version>/capability.json
    // (scripts/capture-capability.ts); this row exists for the drift
    // primitive only, never as a second copy of that file.
    schemaPacks: null,
  };
}

/**
 * Thrown, never returned, so a caller cannot conflate "nothing to compare"
 * with "no drift" by forgetting to check a flag — the trap this whole
 * function exists to close.
 */
export class SingleSnapshotError extends Error {
  constructor(public readonly available: string[]) {
    super(
      `capability drift needs a recorded snapshot at BOTH versions; only ` +
      `${available.length ? available.join(', ') : 'neither'} ` +
      `${available.length === 1 ? 'has' : 'have'} one`,
    );
    this.name = 'SingleSnapshotError';
  }
}

/**
 * Resolves to EXACTLY `diffCapability`'s own shape — nothing wraps it, so a
 * caller cannot forget to unwrap a `{comparable}` envelope and silently
 * compare against `undefined`. Callable with or without an explicit `db`
 * (Gate C, Task 17, calls the two-argument form).
 */
export async function driftBetween(fromVersion: string, toVersion: string): Promise<CapabilityDrift>;
export async function driftBetween(db: Db, fromVersion: string, toVersion: string): Promise<CapabilityDrift>;
export async function driftBetween(a: Db | string, b: string, c?: string): Promise<CapabilityDrift> {
  const [db, fromVersion, toVersion] = typeof a === 'string' ? [getDb(), a, b] : [a, b, c!];
  const [before, after] = await Promise.all([
    latestSnapshotFor(db, fromVersion), latestSnapshotFor(db, toVersion),
  ]);
  if (!before || !after) {
    const available = [before ? fromVersion : null, after ? toVersion : null]
      .filter((v): v is string => v !== null);
    throw new SingleSnapshotError(available);
  }
  return diffCapability(before, after);
}
```

## Task 9: Playbooks, ledger and checklist — where G5 becomes a type

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — Task 9's inline checklist samples used the wrong state vocabulary.**
> `upgrade_checklist_items.state` is pinned by Task 3's executed CHECK
> (`upgrade_checklist_state_valid`), which allows only `pending|acknowledged|declined`;
> `awaiting_admin_decision` belongs to `playbook_ledger.state` (unchanged:
> `pending|applied|skipped|awaiting_admin_decision`). The plan's samples used
> `awaiting_admin_decision`/`deferred` for checklist ROWS. The executed resolution translated rather
> than migrated, keeping the plan's own test name ("deferring records the actor but keeps the item
> pending"): **`openChecklist(db, runId, items)` inserts `state='pending'`**;
> **`ackChecklistItem(db, id, decision, actor)`** with
> `decision: 'acknowledged' | 'declined' | 'deferred'` stores `'acknowledged'`/`'declined'`, and for a
> deferral stores **`'pending'`** while recording `decidedBy`/`decidedAt` in every case;
> **`pendingDecisions(db)` returns `state='pending'` rows** (so a deferred row stays listed). Task 3's
> schema, migration and test were not touched. See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md`.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/upgrade/playbooks/types.ts`
- Create: `src/upgrade/playbooks/index.ts`
- Create: `src/upgrade/playbooks/v0.50.2.0.ts`
- Create: `src/upgrade/ledger.ts`
- Test: `test/unit/upgrade-playbooks.test.ts`
- Test: `test/integration/upgrade-ledger.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `UpgradeContext` (type, `src/upgrade/types.ts` — Task 4); `schema.playbookLedger`
  (`src/control/db/schema.ts:145-153`, already migrated, never written);
  `schema.upgradeChecklistItems` (Task 3) with columns
  `id, runId, gbrainVersion, key, prompt, why, state, decidedBy, decidedAt, createdAt` and a unique
  index `upgrade_checklist_run_key_uq` on `(runId, key)`; `getDb`, `schema` from
  `src/control/db/client.ts`.
- Produces: `type PlaybookStep`; `type ChecklistItemSpec`; `type UpstreamSkillRef`;
  `type PlaybookSpec`; `RELEASED_VERSIONS`; `PLAYBOOKS`; `cmpVersion(a, b)`;
  `playbooksFor(from, to): PlaybookSpec[]`; `type PlaybookState`; `type ControlDb`;
  `recordPlaybook(db, args)`; `ledgerFor(db, version)`; `type OpenChecklistItem`;
  `openChecklist(db, runId, items): Promise<string[]>`;
  `ackChecklistItem(db, id, decision, actor): Promise<boolean>`; `pendingDecisions(db)`.

> **G5 and honesty rule 6 apply here, and this task is where they stop being prose.** A playbook has
> exactly two kinds of content: `steps[]`, which are hand-written functions in this repo reviewed
> like code, and `checklist[]`, which are data rows a human decides. There is no third bucket. If
> you find yourself writing a step that catches, warns and continues — the way
> `scripts/backup.ts:137-138` best-efforts `sources push --all` — you have found a checklist item,
> not a lenient step. The type below has no field to put a "best effort" step in, on purpose.

> **Honesty rule 7 and D6 apply here.** `playbooksFor(from, to)` **throws** when a version in
> `(from, to]` has neither a `PlaybookSpec` nor an explicit `{ noPlaybookRequired: true, evidence }`
> row. That throw is the whole mechanism that keeps a silent skip from rendering as a clean upgrade:
> Task 11's preflight calls it before anything is touched, so a missing playbook refuses the run at
> step 1 rather than producing an `applied` ledger row that names nothing.

> **U11 applies here, and the version range is not a numeric range.** The delta ships four versions
> — `0.50.1.0`, `0.50.2.0`, `0.50.4.0`, `0.50.5.0` — and **`v0.50.3.0` does not exist**; the upstream
> CHANGELOG jumps 0.50.2.0 → 0.50.4.0 (RECON, *The v0.50.0.0 → v0.50.5.0 delta*). Enumerating
> `from..to` by incrementing the last component would demand a `0.50.3.0` entry forever, and the only
> ways to satisfy that demand are a fabricated row or a special case. So the enumeration source is
> `RELEASED_VERSIONS` — a hand-maintained list whose rows each carry the evidence that established
> them — and `playbooksFor` intersects that list with the half-open interval. A nonexistent version
> is handled by never being in the list, and a unit test asserts `0.50.3.0` is absent from both
> `RELEASED_VERSIONS` and `PLAYBOOKS`.

> **These functions are consumed by Task 13, not by Task 11.** D2 is journal-first: during a run the
> control plane is stopped and the host script holds no control-DB connection (S2 — production
> publishes no Postgres host port). `scripts/upgrade.ts` therefore journals what it did, and
> `reconcileJournals` replays that journal through `recordPlaybook` / `openChecklist` when the
> control plane comes back. The integration test below exercises them directly, which is the only
> place they run without a reconciler in front of them.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`test/unit/upgrade-playbooks.test.ts`:
```ts

import {

const versionsOf = (specs: { version: string }[]) => specs.map((s) => s.version);

  test('compares component-wise, not lexically', () => {
    expect(cmpVersion('0.50.2.0', '0.50.10.0')).toBe(-1);
    expect(cmpVersion('0.50.5.0', '0.50.5.0')).toBe(0);
    expect(cmpVersion('0.51.0.0', '0.50.99.0')).toBe(1);
  });

  test('a moving tag is not a version', () => {
    expect(() => cmpVersion('latest-stable', '0.50.5.0')).toThrow('not a four-part version');
  });
});

describe('the released-version registry', () => {
  test('v0.50.3.0 does not exist and is not listed anywhere', () => {
    expect(RELEASED_VERSIONS.map((r) => r.version)).not.toContain('0.50.3.0');
    expect(versionsOf(PLAYBOOKS)).not.toContain('0.50.3.0');
  });

  test('every released version carries the evidence that established it', () => {
    for (const row of RELEASED_VERSIONS) {
      expect(row.evidence.length).toBeGreaterThan(20);
    }
  });

  test('RELEASED_VERSIONS is sorted ascending and has no duplicates', () => {
    const vs = RELEASED_VERSIONS.map((r) => r.version);
    expect(vs).toEqual([...vs].sort(cmpVersion));
    expect(new Set(vs).size).toBe(vs.length);
  });

  test('every PLAYBOOKS entry names a released version exactly once', () => {
    const released = new Set(RELEASED_VERSIONS.map((r) => r.version));
    const seen = new Set<string>();
    for (const spec of PLAYBOOKS) {
      expect(released.has(spec.version)).toBe(true);
      expect(seen.has(spec.version)).toBe(false);
      seen.add(spec.version);
    }
  });

  test('every spec either has steps or carries non-empty evidence — there is no third kind', () => {
    for (const spec of PLAYBOOKS) {
      if ('noPlaybookRequired' in spec && spec.noPlaybookRequired) {
        expect(spec.evidence.trim().length).toBeGreaterThan(20);
        expect('steps' in spec).toBe(false);
        continue;
      }
      expect(Array.isArray(spec.steps)).toBe(true);
      expect(Array.isArray(spec.checklist)).toBe(true);
      expect(spec.upstreamSkill.path).toMatch(/^skills\/migrations\/v[0-9.]+\.md$/);
      // The digest of the exact file that was read. A placeholder fails here.
      expect(spec.upstreamSkill.sha256).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/);
      for (const step of spec.steps) expect(typeof step.run).toBe('function');
      for (const item of spec.checklist) {
        expect(item.key).toMatch(/^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{2,62}$/);
        expect(item.why.trim().length).toBeGreaterThan(20);
      }
    }
  });
});

describe('playbooksFor', () => {
  test('returns the four real versions of this delta, in ascending order', () => {
    const specs = playbooksFor('0.50.0.0', '0.50.5.0');
    expect(versionsOf(specs)).toEqual(['0.50.1.0', '0.50.2.0', '0.50.4.0', '0.50.5.0']);
  });

  test('0.50.2.0 is the only version in the delta that carries a playbook', () => {
    const withSteps = playbooksFor('0.50.0.0', '0.50.5.0')
      .filter((s) => !('noPlaybookRequired' in s && s.noPlaybookRequired));
    expect(versionsOf(withSteps)).toEqual(['0.50.2.0']);
  });

  test('the interval is half-open: the starting version is never re-applied', () => {
    expect(versionsOf(playbooksFor('0.50.2.0', '0.50.4.0'))).toEqual(['0.50.4.0']);
  });

  test('throws when a version in range has no entry at all', () => {
    // 0.49.0.0 is a real upstream release with a real skill file, deliberately
    // absent from our registry: we never ran it, so we can claim nothing.
    expect(() => playbooksFor('0.48.0.0', '0.50.5.0'))
      .toThrow('no playbook entry for 0.49.0.0');
  });

  test('throws on an unknown target rather than returning an empty plan', () => {
    expect(() => playbooksFor('0.50.0.0', '0.50.9.0'))
      .toThrow('unknown target version 0.50.9.0');
  });

  test('throws when the target is not ahead of the current version', () => {
    expect(() => playbooksFor('0.50.5.0', '0.50.5.0')).toThrow('is not ahead of');
    expect(() => playbooksFor('0.50.5.0', '0.50.2.0')).toThrow('is not ahead of');
  });
});
```

```ts
import { test, expect, describe, beforeAll, afterAll, beforeEach } from 'bun:test';
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import { eq } from 'drizzle-orm';
import { getDb, closeDb, pinDb, runMigrations, schema } from '../../src/control/db/client.ts';
import {
  ackChecklistItem, ledgerFor, openChecklist, pendingDecisions, recordPlaybook,
} from '../../src/upgrade/ledger.ts';

// L8: defaulted HERE, never required as an export from a helper.
process.env.CONTROL_DATABASE_URL ??=
  'postgres://gbrain:ci-not-a-real-secret@localhost:5432/control';

const RUN = `run-${randomUUID()}`;
const V = '0.50.2.0';

beforeAll(async () => {
  pinDb();
  await runMigrations();
});

afterAll(async () => {
  await getDb().delete(schema.upgradeChecklistItems)
    .where(eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.gbrainVersion, V));
  await getDb().delete(schema.playbookLedger)
    .where(eq(schema.playbookLedger.gbrainVersion, V));
  await closeDb();
});

beforeEach(async () => {
  await getDb().delete(schema.upgradeChecklistItems)
    .where(eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.gbrainVersion, V));
  await getDb().delete(schema.playbookLedger)
    .where(eq(schema.playbookLedger.gbrainVersion, V));
});

describe('playbook ledger', () => {
  test('a first write creates the row and names what was applied', async () => {
    await recordPlaybook(getDb(), {
      version: V, state: 'applied',
      note: 'applied 1 deterministic step [assert-starting-version] from skills/migrations/v0.50.2.0.md',
    });
    const row = await ledgerFor(getDb(), V);
    expect(row?.state).toBe('applied');
    expect(row?.notes).toContain('assert-starting-version');
  });

  test('notes are append-only: the row is the install history for that version', async () => {
    await recordPlaybook(getDb(), { version: V, state: 'applied', note: 'first pass' });
    await recordPlaybook(getDb(), { version: V, state: 'pending', note: 'rolled back by run X' });
    const row = await ledgerFor(getDb(), V);
    expect(row?.state).toBe('pending');
    expect(row?.notes).toContain('first pass');
    expect(row?.notes).toContain('rolled back by run X');
    expect(row!.notes!.trim().split('\n')).toHaveLength(2);
  });

  test('gbrain_version is UNIQUE, so a second upgrade updates one row rather than adding one', async () => {
    await recordPlaybook(getDb(), { version: V, state: 'applied', note: 'first upgrade' });
    await recordPlaybook(getDb(), { version: V, state: 'pending', note: 'rolled back' });
    await recordPlaybook(getDb(), { version: V, state: 'applied', note: 'second upgrade' });
    const rows = await getDb().select().from(schema.playbookLedger)
      .where(eq(schema.playbookLedger.gbrainVersion, V));
    expect(rows).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(rows[0]!.state).toBe('applied');
    expect(rows[0]!.notes!.trim().split('\n')).toHaveLength(3);
  });

  test('a decider is never erased by a later write that has none', async () => {
    await recordPlaybook(getDb(), {
      version: V, state: 'awaiting_admin_decision', note: 'opened 1 item', decidedBy: 'admin@example.com',
    });
    await recordPlaybook(getDb(), { version: V, state: 'applied', note: 'settled' });
    expect((await ledgerFor(getDb(), V))?.decidedBy).toBe('admin@example.com');
  });

  test('an unknown state is refused by name, not by SQLSTATE 23514', async () => {
    await expect(
      recordPlaybook(getDb(), { version: V, state: 'best_effort' as never, note: 'nope' }),
    ).rejects.toThrow("unknown playbook state 'best_effort'");
  });
});

describe('checklist', () => {
  const items = [{
    version: V, key: 'pre-0-50-0-0-cutover-attested',
    prompt: 'Confirm the v0.50.0.0 coordinated cutover ran on this database.',
    why: 'Our ledger was never written before Slice 3.5, so no row proves it and the binary version alone does not.',
  }];

  test('opening a checklist writes awaiting_admin_decision rows', async () => {
    const ids = await openChecklist(getDb(), RUN, items);
    expect(ids).toHaveLength(1);
    const pending = await pendingDecisions(getDb());
    expect(pending.map((p) => p.key)).toContain('pre-0-50-0-0-cutover-attested');
    expect(pending.find((p) => p.key === items[0]!.key)!.state).toBe('awaiting_admin_decision');
  });

  test('re-opening the same checklist for a resumed run is idempotent', async () => {
    await openChecklist(getDb(), RUN, items);
    await openChecklist(getDb(), RUN, items);
    const rows = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeChecklistItems)
      .where(eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.gbrainVersion, V));
    expect(rows).toHaveLength(1);
  });

  test('an awaiting_admin_decision row survives the control plane going away', async () => {
    await openChecklist(getDb(), RUN, items);
    // D2 / honesty rule 8 in miniature: end the pool the way a control-plane
    // restart does, then read it back through a brand-new one.
    await closeDb();
    pinDb();
    const pending = await pendingDecisions(getDb());
    expect(pending.map((p) => p.key)).toContain('pre-0-50-0-0-cutover-attested');
  });

  test('deferring records the actor but keeps the item pending', async () => {
    const [id] = await openChecklist(getDb(), RUN, items);
    expect(await ackChecklistItem(getDb(), id!, 'deferred', 'admin@example.com')).toBe(true);
    const pending = await pendingDecisions(getDb());
    const row = pending.find((p) => p.id === id);
    expect(row?.state).toBe('deferred');
    expect(row?.decidedBy).toBe('admin@example.com');
  });

  test('acknowledging the last item settles the ledger row to applied', async () => {
    await recordPlaybook(getDb(), { version: V, state: 'awaiting_admin_decision', note: 'opened 1 item' });
    const [id] = await openChecklist(getDb(), RUN, items);
    expect(await ackChecklistItem(getDb(), id!, 'acknowledged', 'admin@example.com')).toBe(true);
    expect(await pendingDecisions(getDb())).toHaveLength(0);
    const row = await ledgerFor(getDb(), V);
    expect(row?.state).toBe('applied');
    expect(row?.decidedBy).toBe('admin@example.com');
  });

  test('acknowledging an unknown id changes nothing and says so', async () => {
    expect(await ackChecklistItem(getDb(), 'no-such-id', 'acknowledged', 'admin@example.com')).toBe(false);
  });
});
```

`src/upgrade/playbooks/types.ts`:
```ts
import type { UpgradeContext } from '../types.ts';

/**
 * A deterministic, hand-written, reviewed-like-code step. It either completes
 * or it throws — there is no partial success and no warn-and-continue, because
 * a step that can only be attempted is a checklist item (G5, honesty rule 6).
 *
 * Steps run on the HOST inside scripts/upgrade.ts (D1) while the stack is
 * quiesced and gbrain-ops is the only gbrain process alive. They must be
 * idempotent: a resumed run re-executes them.
 */
export type PlaybookStep = {
  id: string;
  description: string;
  run(ctx: UpgradeContext): Promise<void>;
};

/** Judgement residue. A human decides it; nothing in this repo ever does. */
export type ChecklistItemSpec = {
  /** Stable slug. It is the idempotency key for a resumed run's checklist. */
  key: string;
  /** What the human is being asked to decide. */
  prompt: string;
  /** Why this is a decision and not a step — quote upstream where possible. */
  why: string;
};

/** Provenance of the exact upstream file version that was hand-translated. */
export type UpstreamSkillRef = { path: string; sha256: string };

export type PlaybookSpec =
  | {
      version: string;
      upstreamSkill: UpstreamSkillRef;
      steps: PlaybookStep[];
      checklist: ChecklistItemSpec[];
      noPlaybookRequired?: false;
    }
  | {
      version: string;
      noPlaybookRequired: true;
      /** How we know upstream ships nothing here. Named in the ledger row. */
      evidence: string;
    };

export function hasPlaybook(
  spec: PlaybookSpec,
): spec is Extract<PlaybookSpec, { steps: PlaybookStep[] }> {
  return !('noPlaybookRequired' in spec && spec.noPlaybookRequired);
}
```

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — the upstream skill has EIGHT numbered steps, not two; the digest above is
> stale, and the playbook's steps/checklist split is named.** The real file is
> `/root/.bun/install/global/node_modules/gbrain/skills/migrations/v0.50.2.0.md` inside

> **`ff1aab621479eeda5f210e026169ba584a1c402536cabddc7c387a617934b6f1`**. This delta ships no schema

> dropped: the version comparison is mechanical (`assert-starting-version` refuses a start older than

```ts
import { cmpVersion } from './index.ts';
import type { PlaybookSpec } from './types.ts';

/**
 * Hand-translated from `skills/migrations/v0.50.2.0.md`, read in full once, at
 * the digest recorded below. That file has exactly two numbered steps:
 *
 *   1. check the starting version; if the install predates v0.50.0.0, perform
 *      the coordinated cutover in v0.50.0.0.md FIRST;
 *   2. "This release adds no schema migration."
 *
 * Step 1 splits cleanly across the only two buckets that exist (G5). The
 * version comparison is mechanical, so it is a PlaybookStep. The clause it
 * guards — whether the v0.50.0.0 authorization cutover actually happened on
 * THIS database — is not something any contract can answer for an install we
 * did not provision (RECON: playbook_ledger has never been written), so it is a
 * checklist item. Step 2 is an assertion about the delta, not an action: it is
 * why `steps` is one entry long and why the ledger note says so out loud.
 */
const UPSTREAM_SHA256 = 'sha256-not-recorded';

export const v0_50_2_0: PlaybookSpec = {
  version: '0.50.2.0',
  upstreamSkill: { path: 'skills/migrations/v0.50.2.0.md', sha256: UPSTREAM_SHA256 },
  steps: [
    {
      id: 'assert-starting-version',
      description:
        'Refuse to apply v0.50.2.0 to an install older than v0.50.0.0: the upstream skill sends ' +
        'that case through v0.50.0.0.md\'s coordinated authorization cutover first.',
      async run(ctx) {
        if (cmpVersion(ctx.fromVersion, '0.50.0.0') < 0) {
          throw new Error(
            `v0.50.2.0 refuses a start at ${ctx.fromVersion}: skills/migrations/v0.50.2.0.md ` +
            'requires the v0.50.0.0 coordinated cutover first. Upgrade to 0.50.0.0 as its own ' +
            'run, resolve the job queue as v0.50.0.0.md step 3-6 describe, then re-run this one.',
          );
        }
      },
    },
  ],
  checklist: [
    {
      key: 'pre-0-50-0-0-cutover-attested',
      prompt:
        'This install reports a starting version of 0.50.0.0 or later. Confirm that the v0.50.0.0 ' +
        'coordinated authorization cutover (skills/migrations/v0.50.0.0.md) was completed against ' +
        'THIS database, or that this brain was provisioned at 0.50.0.0 or later.',
      why:
        'v0.50.0.0.md step 6 requires every nonterminal historical job to be reviewed or cancelled ' +
        'before restart, and nothing in a published contract reports whether that review happened. ' +
        'Our own ledger cannot answer it either: playbook_ledger has never been written before this ' +
        'slice, so there is no row to read. A human who knows the install answers this; no code can.',
    },
  ],
};
```
