`src/upgrade/playbooks/index.ts`:
```ts
import { v0_50_2_0 } from './v0.50.2.0.ts';
import type { PlaybookSpec } from './types.ts';

export type { ChecklistItemSpec, PlaybookSpec, PlaybookStep, UpstreamSkillRef } from './types.ts';
export { hasPlaybook } from './types.ts';

/** Four numeric components, exactly. A moving tag is not a version (G1). */
const VERSION_RE = /^\d+\.\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/;

export function cmpVersion(a: string, b: string): -1 | 0 | 1 {
  for (const v of [a, b]) {
    if (!VERSION_RE.test(v)) throw new Error(`${v} is not a four-part version`);
  }
  const pa = a.split('.').map(Number);
  const pb = b.split('.').map(Number);
  for (let i = 0; i < 4; i += 1) {
    const x = pa[i]!;
    const y = pb[i]!;
    if (x !== y) return x < y ? -1 : 1;
  }
  return 0;
}

export type ReleasedVersion = { version: string; evidence: string };

/**
 * The versions upstream ACTUALLY released, in order, each with the evidence
 * that established it. This list — not an arithmetic range — is what
 * playbooksFor enumerates.
 *
 * Incrementing the last component from `from` to `to` would demand a 0.50.3.0
 * entry, and 0.50.3.0 does not exist: upstream's CHANGELOG jumps 0.50.2.0 ->
 * 0.50.4.0 with no gap in the capture. A nonexistent version is handled by
 * never being in this list.
 */
export const RELEASED_VERSIONS: readonly ReleasedVersion[] = [
  {
    version: '0.50.0.0',
    evidence: 'our pin; CHANGELOG.md heading at tag v0.50.0.0 and skills/migrations/v0.50.0.0.md',
  },
  {
    version: '0.50.1.0',
    evidence:
      'CHANGELOG.md heading at v0.50.5.0; a 21-PR community fix wave. No ' +
      'skills/migrations/v0.50.1.0.md exists in the v0.50.5.0 tree.',
  },
  {
    version: '0.50.2.0',
    evidence:
      'CHANGELOG.md heading at v0.50.5.0; write-durability / idempotency fixes. ' +
      'skills/migrations/v0.50.2.0.md is the one new playbook file in this delta.',
  },
  {
    version: '0.50.4.0',
    evidence:
      'CHANGELOG.md heading at v0.50.5.0, immediately after 0.50.2.0 — 0.50.3.0 was never ' +
      'released. CI-only changes, no runtime behaviour change, no migration file.',
  },
  {
    version: '0.50.5.0',
    evidence:
      'our target; CHANGELOG.md heading at tag v0.50.5.0, a security-hardening wave ' +
      '(OAuth scope ceiling, transcript redaction, .env guardrail, bunfig preload lockdown). ' +
      'No skills/migrations/v0.50.5.0.md exists.',
  },
];

/**
 * HOW TO WRITE THE NEXT VERSION'S PLAYBOOK. Follow this exactly; it is the
 * procedure, not a suggestion, and it is here rather than in a runbook because
 * the registry is where someone will be standing when they need it.
 *
 * 1. Read `skills/migrations/v<version>.md` in the TARGET checkout, in full,
 *    once, yourself. Do not summarise it with an LLM and translate the summary:
 *    an LLM may PROPOSE the checklist, it never writes the steps (G5).
 * 2. Record `sha256sum` of that exact file into `upstreamSkill.sha256`. If
 *    upstream amends the file later, the digest mismatch is the signal to
 *    re-read it rather than to trust last year's translation.
 * 3. Take each numbered imperative in turn and put it in exactly ONE bucket:
 *      - it can be written as a function of UpgradeContext that either
 *        succeeds or throws, with no operator judgement -> a PlaybookStep;
 *      - it cannot -> a ChecklistItemSpec whose `why` quotes the sentence that
 *        makes it a judgement call.
 *    Never both. A step you are tempted to write as "try, log, continue" is a
 *    checklist item (honesty rule 6).
 * 4. Steps must be idempotent (a resumed run re-runs them) and must not touch
 *    Docker — only scripts/upgrade.ts does (D1). They execute with the stack
 *    quiesced and gbrain-ops as the only live gbrain process.
 * 5. Anything upstream tells you to do with SQL against gbrain's own tables is
 *    forbidden here (G3). It becomes a checklist item quoting the instruction.
 * 6. If the file exists but prescribes no action, the spec still exists, with
 *    `steps: []` and the quote that says so. Only a version upstream ships NO
 *    file for gets `{ noPlaybookRequired: true, evidence }`.
 * 7. Add the version to RELEASED_VERSIONS with its CHANGELOG evidence, add the
 *    spec to PLAYBOOKS, and extend test/unit/upgrade-playbooks.test.ts's
 *    expected window. The registry invariants in that file are the review gate.
 */
export const PLAYBOOKS: readonly PlaybookSpec[] = [
  {
    version: '0.50.1.0',
    noPlaybookRequired: true,
    evidence:
      'No skills/migrations/v0.50.1.0.md exists in the v0.50.5.0 tree (41 files at our pin, ' +
      'oldest v0.5.0.md, and the delta adds exactly one file: v0.50.2.0.md).',
  },
  v0_50_2_0,
  {
    version: '0.50.4.0',
    noPlaybookRequired: true,
    evidence:
      'No skills/migrations/v0.50.4.0.md exists in the v0.50.5.0 tree; the release is CI-only ' +
      'per its CHANGELOG entry and src/core/migrate.ts is byte-identical across the delta.',
  },
  {
    version: '0.50.5.0',
    noPlaybookRequired: true,
    evidence:
      'No skills/migrations/v0.50.5.0.md exists in the v0.50.5.0 tree. The release is a ' +
      'security-hardening wave whose risk is configuration (the .env guardrail quarantine, U12), ' +
      'probed by Task 1 and gated by Task 11, not a migration step.',
  },
];

const BY_VERSION = new Map(PLAYBOOKS.map((s) => [s.version, s]));

/**
 * Every spec that applies to the half-open interval (from, to], ascending.
 *
 * THROWS rather than skipping. A version in range with no entry means nobody
 * has read upstream's file for it, and an upgrade that silently skipped it
 * would still produce a green run and an `applied` ledger row — the exact
 * shape honesty rule 7 exists to prevent.
 */
export function playbooksFor(from: string, to: string): PlaybookSpec[] {
  if (cmpVersion(to, from) <= 0) {
    throw new Error(`target ${to} is not ahead of the current version ${from}`);
  }
  const known = new Set(RELEASED_VERSIONS.map((r) => r.version));
  if (!known.has(to)) {
    throw new Error(
      `unknown target version ${to}: add it to RELEASED_VERSIONS with the CHANGELOG evidence ` +
      'that establishes it, then write its playbook entry.',
    );
  }
  if (!known.has(from)) {
    throw new Error(`unknown starting version ${from}: it is not in RELEASED_VERSIONS`);
  }

  const window = RELEASED_VERSIONS
    .map((r) => r.version)
    .filter((v) => cmpVersion(v, from) > 0 && cmpVersion(v, to) <= 0)
    .sort(cmpVersion);

  return window.map((v) => {
    const spec = BY_VERSION.get(v);
    if (!spec) {
      throw new Error(
        `no playbook entry for ${v} in the (${from}, ${to}] window: add a PlaybookSpec, or an ` +
        'explicit { noPlaybookRequired: true, evidence } row naming how you know upstream ships ' +
        'none. A missing entry is not a skip.',
      );
    }
    return spec;
  });
}
```

`src/upgrade/ledger.ts`:
```ts
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import { and, asc, eq, inArray, sql } from 'drizzle-orm';
import { getDb, schema } from '../control/db/client.ts';
import type { ChecklistItemSpec } from './playbooks/types.ts';

export type ControlDb = ReturnType<typeof getDb>;

/** The four states src/control/db/schema.ts:152-153 already CHECKs. */
export const PLAYBOOK_STATES = ['pending', 'applied', 'skipped', 'awaiting_admin_decision'] as const;
export type PlaybookState = (typeof PLAYBOOK_STATES)[number];

/** Undecided in both senses: nothing has been done AND the banner stays up. */
const UNDECIDED = ['awaiting_admin_decision', 'deferred'] as const;

/**
 * One row per gbrain version, because `gbrain_version` is UNIQUE
 * (src/control/db/schema.ts:147). The row is therefore the INSTALL's history
 * for that version, not one run's outcome: upgrade to 0.50.2.0, roll back,
 * upgrade again, and there is still exactly one row.
 *
 * That is why `notes` appends instead of replacing. Each write adds one
 * timestamped line, so the row reads as a log — `applied by run A`,
 * `rolled back by run A`, `applied by run B` — and the current `state` column
 * is the last line's verdict. Task 12's rollback writes `pending` back with a
 * line naming the run that undid it, so a row can never say `applied` about a
 * version the binary is no longer at.
 */
export async function recordPlaybook(
  db: ControlDb,
  args: { version: string; state: PlaybookState; note: string; decidedBy?: string; now?: Date },
): Promise<void> {
  if (!(PLAYBOOK_STATES as readonly string[]).includes(args.state)) {
    throw new Error(
      `unknown playbook state '${args.state}': expected one of ${PLAYBOOK_STATES.join(', ')}`,
    );
  }
  const at = args.now ?? new Date();
  const line = `${at.toISOString()} ${args.state} — ${args.note}\n`;

  await db.insert(schema.playbookLedger).values({
    id: randomUUID(),
    gbrainVersion: args.version,
    state: args.state,
    notes: line,
    decidedBy: args.decidedBy ?? null,
    updatedAt: at,
  }).onConflictDoUpdate({
    target: schema.playbookLedger.gbrainVersion,
    set: {
      state: args.state,
      // In ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE the qualified column is the EXISTING row.
      notes: sql`coalesce(${schema.playbookLedger.notes}, '') || ${line}`,
      updatedAt: at,
      // Only when supplied: a later automated write must not erase the human
      // who decided the checklist.
      ...(args.decidedBy ? { decidedBy: args.decidedBy } : {}),
    },
  });
}

export async function ledgerFor(db: ControlDb, version: string) {
  const [row] = await db.select().from(schema.playbookLedger)
    .where(eq(schema.playbookLedger.gbrainVersion, version)).limit(1);
  return row ?? null;
}

export type OpenChecklistItem = ChecklistItemSpec & { version: string };

/**
 * Open the judgement residue as rows a human decides. Idempotent on
 * (runId, key) so a resumed run re-opens nothing.
 */
export async function openChecklist(
  db: ControlDb, runId: string, items: OpenChecklistItem[],
): Promise<string[]> {
  if (items.length === 0) return [];
  const values = items.map((i) => ({
    id: randomUUID(),
    runId,
    gbrainVersion: i.version,
    key: i.key,
    prompt: i.prompt,
    why: i.why,
    state: 'awaiting_admin_decision' as const,
  }));
  await db.insert(schema.upgradeChecklistItems).values(values)
    .onConflictDoNothing({
      target: [schema.upgradeChecklistItems.runId, schema.upgradeChecklistItems.key],
    });
  const rows = await db.select({ id: schema.upgradeChecklistItems.id })
    .from(schema.upgradeChecklistItems)
    .where(and(
      eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.runId, runId),
      inArray(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.key, items.map((i) => i.key)),
    ));
  return rows.map((r) => r.id);
}

export type PendingDecision = {
  id: string; runId: string; version: string; key: string; prompt: string; why: string;
  state: string; decidedBy: string | null; decidedAt: Date | null;
};

/**
 * Everything still undecided, oldest first. `deferred` stays in this list on
 * purpose: deferring records that a human looked and chose to wait, which is an
 * audit trail, not a resolution. Dropping it here would take the banner down
 * with nothing decided (honesty rule 8).
 */
export async function pendingDecisions(db: ControlDb): Promise<PendingDecision[]> {
  const rows = await db.select().from(schema.upgradeChecklistItems)
    .where(inArray(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.state, [...UNDECIDED]))
    .orderBy(asc(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.createdAt));
  return rows.map((r) => ({
    id: r.id, runId: r.runId, version: r.gbrainVersion, key: r.key,
    prompt: r.prompt, why: r.why, state: r.state,
    decidedBy: r.decidedBy, decidedAt: r.decidedAt,
  }));
}

/**
 * Record a human's decision. Returns whether a row changed, so an API can 404
 * an unknown id instead of reporting a success nobody caused.
 *
 * Acknowledging the LAST undecided item for a version settles that version's
 * ledger row to `applied` and names the actor — the run and the checklist then
 * agree, which is the other half of honesty rule 8.
 */
export async function ackChecklistItem(
  db: ControlDb, id: string, decision: 'acknowledged' | 'deferred', actor: string,
): Promise<boolean> {
  const [row] = await db.update(schema.upgradeChecklistItems)
    .set({ state: decision, decidedBy: actor, decidedAt: new Date() })
    .where(and(
      eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.id, id),
      inArray(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.state, [...UNDECIDED]),
    ))
    .returning({ version: schema.upgradeChecklistItems.gbrainVersion, key: schema.upgradeChecklistItems.key });
  if (!row) return false;

  if (decision === 'acknowledged') {
    const remaining = await db.select({ id: schema.upgradeChecklistItems.id })
      .from(schema.upgradeChecklistItems)
      .where(and(
        eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.gbrainVersion, row.version),
        inArray(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.state, [...UNDECIDED]),
      ));
    if (remaining.length === 0) {
      await recordPlaybook(db, {
        version: row.version, state: 'applied', decidedBy: actor,
        note: `every checklist item decided; last was '${row.key}', acknowledged by ${actor}`,
      });
    }
  }
  return true;
}
```

## Task 10: The run state machine and what a dead run is allowed to do next

**Files:**
- Create: `src/upgrade/state.ts`
- Test: `test/unit/upgrade-state.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `UpgradeRunState`, `UpgradeEvent` (types, `src/upgrade/types.ts` — Task 4;
  `UpgradeEvent` is `{ runId, seq, at, state, note?, data? }` and this module reads `seq` and
  `state` only); the `upgrade_run_state_valid` CHECK in `src/control/db/schema.ts` (Task 3).
- Produces: `UpgradeRunState` (re-exported, so consumers have one import site);
  `TRANSITIONS`; `SAFE_TO_RESUME`; `ROLLBACK_REQUIRED`; `canTransition(from, to)`;
  `nextState(current, outcome)`; `isTerminal(state)`; `type ResumeDecision`;
  `resumeFrom(journal): ResumeDecision`.

> **D2 applies here, and it is the whole reason this file is not just a tidy enum.** The journal on
> disk is the authority while the control plane is down, so a run that died mid-flight must be
> classifiable from `journal.ndjson` alone. `resumeFrom` is that classifier, and its answer is not
> always "resume": for four of the thirteen states the honest answer is "roll back".

> **The `swapped`-but-not-`migrated` case is the one worth stating out loud.** A stack in that window
> is running NEW images against a schema no migration step has completed, and there is no published
> contract that reports the schema version — RECON is explicit: it lives in a generic key-value
> `config` table, `--list`/`--dry-run` print it as prose, doctor embeds it in a `message` string, and
> reading it structurally would mean scraping prose or reading gbrain's tables (both forbidden,
> G3/U6). So the tool cannot observe whether the pairing is safe; it can only infer it.
> **`resumeFrom` therefore classifies every post-swap state as `rollback-required`, never
> resumable.** On *this* delta the pairing is in fact fine — upstream ships zero schema migrations
> between 0.50.0.0 and 0.50.5.0 (U10) — and that is exactly why the rule must not be relaxed to fit
> it: encoding "this delta is special" into the state machine is a shortcut that breaks on the next
> delta, silently, against a live brain.

> **`failed` is pre-swap-only and terminal.** Nothing has been mutated before the swap except stopped
> services, which `resume` (Task 6) puts back, so there is nothing to restore and `failed` has no
> edge to `rolling_back`. Post-swap failure goes to `rolling_back` and never to `failed`. Both halves
> of that rule are asserted below, because the tempting bug is a catch-all that journals `failed`
> from wherever it caught — which would leave a swapped stack marked terminal and un-rollbackable.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`test/unit/upgrade-state.test.ts`:
```ts
import { test, expect, describe } from 'bun:test';
import type { UpgradeEvent } from '../../src/upgrade/types.ts';
import {
  ROLLBACK_REQUIRED, SAFE_TO_RESUME, TRANSITIONS,
  canTransition, isTerminal, nextState, resumeFrom, type UpgradeRunState,
} from '../../src/upgrade/state.ts';

const ALL = Object.keys(TRANSITIONS) as UpgradeRunState[];

/** Build a journal the way scripts/upgrade.ts appends one: monotonic seq. */
function journal(...states: UpgradeRunState[]): UpgradeEvent[] {
  return states.map((state, i) => ({
    runId: 'run-1', seq: i + 1, at: new Date(1_700_000_000_000 + i * 1000).toISOString(), state,
  }));
}

const HAPPY_PATH: UpgradeRunState[] = [
  'planned', 'preflight_ok', 'quiesced', 'snapshot_taken', 'swapped',
  'migrated', 'playbooks_applied', 'gated_ok', 'completed',
];

describe('the state set', () => {
  test('matches the CHECK constraint in the control schema, exactly', async () => {
    // L9's lesson generalised: a duplicated list is only safe when something
    // mechanical compares the copies. test/unit/control-plane-dockerfile.test.ts
    // is the precedent for statically reading one of our own files in a unit test.
    const source = await Bun.file('src/control/db/schema.ts').text();
    const clause = /upgrade_run_state_valid[\s\S]*?in \(([\s\S]*?)\)/.exec(source);
    expect(clause).not.toBeNull();
    const declared = [...clause![1]!.matchAll(/'([a-z_]+)'/g)].map((m) => m[1]!).sort();
    expect(ALL.slice().sort()).toEqual(declared);
  });

  test('has exactly the thirteen states and four terminals', () => {
    expect(ALL).toHaveLength(13);
    expect(ALL.filter(isTerminal).sort())
      .toEqual(['completed', 'failed', 'rollback_failed', 'rolled_back']);
  });
});

describe('canTransition', () => {
  test('the happy path walks end to end', () => {
    for (let i = 0; i < HAPPY_PATH.length - 1; i += 1) {
      expect(canTransition(HAPPY_PATH[i]!, HAPPY_PATH[i + 1]!)).toBe(true);
    }
  });

  test('every pair not in TRANSITIONS is refused — no accidental edges', () => {
    for (const from of ALL) {
      for (const to of ALL) {
        expect(canTransition(from, to)).toBe(TRANSITIONS[from].includes(to));
      }
    }
  });

  test('a swapped run can never be marked failed — only rolled back', () => {
    for (const s of ['swapped', 'migrated', 'playbooks_applied', 'gated_ok'] as UpgradeRunState[]) {
      expect(canTransition(s, 'failed')).toBe(false);
      expect(canTransition(s, 'rolling_back')).toBe(true);
    }
  });

  test('failed is terminal: it never becomes a rollback after the fact', () => {
    expect(canTransition('failed', 'rolling_back')).toBe(false);
    expect(TRANSITIONS.failed).toEqual([]);
  });

  test('a completed run cannot be reopened', () => {
    for (const to of ALL) expect(canTransition('completed', to)).toBe(false);
  });

  test('no state transitions to itself', () => {
    for (const s of ALL) expect(canTransition(s, s)).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe('nextState', () => {
  test('drives the happy path one outcome at a time', () => {
    let s: UpgradeRunState = 'planned';
    const walked: UpgradeRunState[] = [s];
    while (!isTerminal(s)) { s = nextState(s, 'ok'); walked.push(s); }
    expect(walked).toEqual(HAPPY_PATH);
  });

  test('a pre-swap failure lands on failed, a post-swap failure on rolling_back', () => {
    for (const s of ['planned', 'preflight_ok', 'quiesced', 'snapshot_taken'] as UpgradeRunState[]) {
      expect(nextState(s, 'failed')).toBe('failed');
    }
    for (const s of ['swapped', 'migrated', 'playbooks_applied', 'gated_ok'] as UpgradeRunState[]) {
      expect(nextState(s, 'failed')).toBe('rolling_back');
    }
  });

  test('a failed rollback is its own terminal, not a generic failure', () => {
    expect(nextState('rolling_back', 'ok')).toBe('rolled_back');
    expect(nextState('rolling_back', 'failed')).toBe('rollback_failed');
  });

  test('every value it can return is a legal transition', () => {
    for (const s of ALL) {
      if (isTerminal(s)) continue;
      for (const outcome of ['ok', 'failed'] as const) {
        expect(canTransition(s, nextState(s, outcome))).toBe(true);
      }
    }
  });

  test('asking a terminal state for a next state throws rather than guessing', () => {
    expect(() => nextState('completed', 'ok')).toThrow('no transition from terminal state');
  });
});

describe('resumeFrom', () => {
  test('an empty journal is unknown, not resumable', () => {
    expect(resumeFrom([])).toEqual({
      kind: 'unknown',
      reason: 'the journal is empty: no run state was ever durably recorded',
    });
  });

  test('a truncated journal lands on the last DURABLE state', () => {
    // readJournal (Task 4) drops a half-written trailing line, so what reaches
    // this function is simply a shorter array. The last complete event wins.
    expect(resumeFrom(journal('planned', 'preflight_ok', 'quiesced')))
      .toEqual({ kind: 'resume', from: 'quiesced', next: 'snapshot_taken' });
  });

  test('out-of-order events are ordered by seq, not by arrival', () => {
    const events = journal('planned', 'preflight_ok', 'quiesced').reverse();
    expect(resumeFrom(events)).toMatchObject({ kind: 'resume', from: 'quiesced' });
  });

  test('every pre-swap state is resumable', () => {
    for (const s of SAFE_TO_RESUME) {
      if (s === 'rolling_back') continue;
      expect(resumeFrom(journal(s)).kind).toBe('resume');
    }
  });

  test('a run whose last event is swapped requires a rollback, not a resume', () => {
    const d = resumeFrom(journal('planned', 'preflight_ok', 'quiesced', 'snapshot_taken', 'swapped'));
    expect(d.kind).toBe('rollback-required');
    expect(d).toMatchObject({ from: 'swapped' });
    expect((d as { reason: string }).reason).toContain('no migration step has completed');
  });

  test('every post-swap state requires a rollback', () => {
    for (const s of ROLLBACK_REQUIRED) {
      expect(resumeFrom(journal(s)).kind).toBe('rollback-required');
    }
  });

  test('an interrupted rollback resumes as a rollback — restore is idempotent', () => {
    expect(resumeFrom(journal('swapped', 'rolling_back')))
      .toEqual({ kind: 'resume', from: 'rolling_back', next: 'rolled_back' });
  });

  test('terminal runs report whether they still need a human', () => {
    expect(resumeFrom(journal('completed')))
      .toEqual({ kind: 'terminal', from: 'completed', needsAttention: false });
    expect(resumeFrom(journal('rolled_back')))
      .toEqual({ kind: 'terminal', from: 'rolled_back', needsAttention: false });
    expect(resumeFrom(journal('failed')))
      .toEqual({ kind: 'terminal', from: 'failed', needsAttention: true });
    expect(resumeFrom(journal('rollback_failed')))
      .toEqual({ kind: 'terminal', from: 'rollback_failed', needsAttention: true });
  });

  test('a state this build does not know is unknown, never resumable', () => {
    const bogus = [{ runId: 'r', seq: 1, at: '2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z', state: 'half_swapped' }];
    expect(resumeFrom(bogus as unknown as UpgradeEvent[]).kind).toBe('unknown');
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

`src/upgrade/state.ts`:
```ts
import type { UpgradeEvent, UpgradeRunState } from './types.ts';

export type { UpgradeRunState };

/**
 * The legal edges, and only the legal edges. Keys are exactly the CHECK list in
 * src/control/db/schema.ts, and a unit test compares the two mechanically
 * rather than trusting that both were edited together (L9).
 *
 * Two shapes are load-bearing:
 *   - `failed` is reachable only BEFORE the swap. Up to that point the only
 *     mutation is stopped services, which resume() puts back, so there is
 *     nothing to restore. It is terminal and has no edge to rolling_back.
 *   - From `swapped` onward the only failure edge is `rolling_back`. The pin on
 *     disk and the running images have already moved; the honest response to
 *     any failure there is the snapshot, not a status change.
 */
export const TRANSITIONS: Readonly<Record<UpgradeRunState, readonly UpgradeRunState[]>> = {
  planned: ['preflight_ok', 'failed'],
  preflight_ok: ['quiesced', 'failed'],
  quiesced: ['snapshot_taken', 'failed'],
  snapshot_taken: ['swapped', 'failed'],
  swapped: ['migrated', 'rolling_back'],
  migrated: ['playbooks_applied', 'rolling_back'],
  playbooks_applied: ['gated_ok', 'rolling_back'],
  gated_ok: ['completed', 'rolling_back'],
  rolling_back: ['rolled_back', 'rollback_failed'],
  completed: [],
  failed: [],
  rolled_back: [],
  rollback_failed: [],
};

const ON_SUCCESS: Readonly<Record<UpgradeRunState, UpgradeRunState | null>> = {
  planned: 'preflight_ok',
  preflight_ok: 'quiesced',
  quiesced: 'snapshot_taken',
  snapshot_taken: 'swapped',
  swapped: 'migrated',
  migrated: 'playbooks_applied',
  playbooks_applied: 'gated_ok',
  gated_ok: 'completed',
  rolling_back: 'rolled_back',
  completed: null, failed: null, rolled_back: null, rollback_failed: null,
};

const ON_FAILURE: Readonly<Record<UpgradeRunState, UpgradeRunState | null>> = {
  planned: 'failed',
  preflight_ok: 'failed',
  quiesced: 'failed',
  snapshot_taken: 'failed',
  swapped: 'rolling_back',
  migrated: 'rolling_back',
  playbooks_applied: 'rolling_back',
  gated_ok: 'rolling_back',
  rolling_back: 'rollback_failed',
  completed: null, failed: null, rolled_back: null, rollback_failed: null,
};

/**
 * States a dead run may pick up from. Everything here mutated nothing that a
 * plain `resume` (Task 6) cannot undo — except `rolling_back`, which is here
 * because a restore from a verified manifest is idempotent: re-running it
 * re-restores the same four artifacts over whatever the dead attempt left.
 */
export const SAFE_TO_RESUME: readonly UpgradeRunState[] = [
  'planned', 'preflight_ok', 'quiesced', 'snapshot_taken', 'rolling_back',
];

/** Post-swap. The pin and the images moved; the snapshot is the way back. */
export const ROLLBACK_REQUIRED: readonly UpgradeRunState[] = [
  'swapped', 'migrated', 'playbooks_applied', 'gated_ok',
];

const ROLLBACK_REASONS: Readonly<Record<string, string>> = {
  swapped:
    'the pin was moved and the new images are live, but no migration step has completed and no ' +
    'published contract reports the running schema version (U6/G3) — the tool will not infer that ' +
    'this pairing is safe, so the snapshot is the way forward',
  migrated:
    'apply-migrations and post-upgrade ran but the playbook steps did not, so this install is at ' +
    'the new schema with an unknown amount of its playbook applied',
  playbooks_applied:
    'the playbooks ran but the doctor parity gate never returned a verdict, so nothing has ' +
    'established that this upgrade is healthy',
  gated_ok:
    'the gate passed but the run never reached completed, so the last step — resuming the stack ' +
    'and closing the run — is in an unknown state',
};

export function isTerminal(state: UpgradeRunState): boolean {
  return TRANSITIONS[state].length === 0;
}

export function canTransition(from: UpgradeRunState, to: UpgradeRunState): boolean {
  return TRANSITIONS[from]?.includes(to) ?? false;
}

export function nextState(current: UpgradeRunState, outcome: 'ok' | 'failed'): UpgradeRunState {
  const next = (outcome === 'ok' ? ON_SUCCESS : ON_FAILURE)[current];
  if (!next) throw new Error(`no transition from terminal state '${current}'`);
  return next;
}

export type ResumeDecision =
  | { kind: 'resume'; from: UpgradeRunState; next: UpgradeRunState }
  | { kind: 'rollback-required'; from: UpgradeRunState; reason: string }
  | { kind: 'terminal'; from: UpgradeRunState; needsAttention: boolean }
  | { kind: 'unknown'; reason: string };

/**
 * Classify a run from its journal alone (D2). The control plane may never have
 * seen this run: it is stopped for most of an upgrade, and for a run that died
 * during the swap it was stopped for all of it.
 *
 * The highest `seq` wins. A half-written trailing line never reaches here —
 * Task 4's readJournal drops it — so "truncated" simply means the last complete
 * event is the answer, which is the correct conservative reading: a state that
 * was not durably recorded did not happen.
 */
export function resumeFrom(journal: readonly UpgradeEvent[]): ResumeDecision {
  if (journal.length === 0) {
    return { kind: 'unknown', reason: 'the journal is empty: no run state was ever durably recorded' };
  }
  const last = [...journal].sort((a, b) => a.seq - b.seq).at(-1)!;
  const state = last.state as UpgradeRunState;

  if (!(state in TRANSITIONS)) {
    return {
      kind: 'unknown',
      reason: `the journal records state '${String(last.state)}', which this build does not know; ` +
        'refusing to guess what a newer or corrupted run was doing',
    };
  }
  if (isTerminal(state)) {
    return {
      kind: 'terminal', from: state,
      needsAttention: state === 'failed' || state === 'rollback_failed',
    };
  }
  if (ROLLBACK_REQUIRED.includes(state)) {
    return { kind: 'rollback-required', from: state, reason: ROLLBACK_REASONS[state]! };
  }
  return { kind: 'resume', from: state, next: nextState(state, 'ok') };
}
```

Run: `bun test test/unit/upgrade-state.test.ts`
Expected: PASS, 19 tests.

The assertion that matters is `a run whose last event is swapped requires a rollback, not a resume`.
If it goes red because someone moved `swapped` into `SAFE_TO_RESUME`, the reason will be that this
delta has no migrations and resuming forward "obviously works" — U10 says so. Do **not** make that
change, and do not add a per-delta exemption beside it. It is correct exactly once, for exactly this
jump, and the state machine has to be right for the jump after it, when nobody re-reads this
paragraph.

## Task 11: `scripts/upgrade.ts` — `plan` and `run`

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — the journal acquired structured records the plan did not specify, and
> `plan` cannot report `schema.pendingCount`.** Task 11's draft journaled only
> `{step, level, message, data}` state events. The exit gate required durable structured records that
> the reconciler, the ledger and the console all read: the schema step (`{ exitCode, outcome, evidence }`),
> the snapshot manifest (`step: 'snapshot'`, `{ manifest, durationMs }`), the doctor parity
> (`step: 'gate'`, `{ parity }`), and `run_completed.downtimeMs`. Also: `plan` cannot compute
> `schema.pendingCount` — there is **no published contract for the schema version** (U6) and scraping
> the prose is G3 — so Gate C derives pendency (6) from upstream's own `m.version > current` filter and
> names the derivation in its output. Separately, Tasks 11 and 12 were executed as one unit (see the
> Task-index blockquote).

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/upgrade.ts`
- Test: `test/unit/upgrade-cli.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `GBRAIN_PIN`, `verifyRuntimePin` (Task 2); `openJournal`, `appendEvent`, `readJournal`,
  `verifyManifest` (Task 4); `SnapshotDriver`, `dockerVolumeDriver`, `SNAPSHOT_VOLUMES`,
  `SNAPSHOT_DATABASES` (Task 5); `quiesce`, `resume`, `verifySupervisorDrained` (Task 6);
  `playbooksFor`, `hasPlaybook`, `PlaybookSpec` (Task 9); `TRANSITIONS`, `canTransition`,
  `nextState`, `UpgradeRunState` (Task 10); `stateDigest`, `compareDoctor`, `rollback` (Task 12);
  `OPERATIONS` (`src/contracts/registry.ts`); `type RepoStatus` (`src/ops/git.ts`).
- Produces: `type UpgradeContext`; `UPGRADE_DIR`; `SWAP_SERVICES`; `SNAPSHOT_SLACK`;
  `class UsageError`; `class PreflightRefusal`; `type PreflightCode`; `parseArgs(argv)`;
  `buildContext(args)`; `type PreflightDeps`; `type PreflightReport`; `preflight(ctx, deps)`;
  `type RunDeps`; `type UpgradeReport`; `runUpgrade(ctx, deps)`; `planUpgrade(ctx, deps)`;
  `statusOf(runId)`; `writePin(envPath, version)`; `opsRpc(ctx, op, params)`.

> **D1 and S2 apply here.** This is the only component that touches Docker, exactly like
> `scripts/backup.ts:21-25` and `scripts/restore.ts:62-66`, and it inherits their idiom without
> negotiation: the `COMPOSE` prefix with `--env-file .env` when present, `compose exec -T`, argv
> arrays and never shell strings (`lint:forbidden` rule `shell-string-gbrain` will catch a lapse),
> `captureToFile` for anything whose stdout is an artifact, the `if (import.meta.main)` entry that
> prints one JSON report, and exit-code discipline at the bottom. The control plane gains no Docker
> socket and no Docker CLI; `POST /api/backup`'s 503 `BACKUP_HOST_ONLY_ERROR` is how that boundary
> is expressed to the product, and nothing here changes it.

> **U4 and S3 apply here, and they reorder the roadmap's step 4.** `post-upgrade` does a lock-free
> read-modify-write on `config.json`'s `self_upgrade.*` subtree, and `src/commands/autopilot.ts`
> mutates the same subtree independently — a confirmed lost-update race. The roadmap says to recreate
> `gbrain-serve`, `gbrain-worker`, `gbrain-ops` and `control-plane` at the swap; doing that literally
> restarts the supervisor and puts the racing writer back before the schema step runs. So this task
> **builds all four images at the swap and recreates only `gbrain-ops`**, runs the schema step and the
> playbooks while ops is the only live gbrain process, and brings `gbrain-serve`, `gbrain-worker` and
> `control-plane` up in the resume that precedes the gate. The quiesce window structurally survives
> until the writers are back, which is what D3 says atomicity actually comes from. Record the
> measured length of that window in the findings doc.

> **U3 and G3 apply to the schema step, structurally rather than by convention.** `apply-migrations`
> has no `--json` and its exit codes are 0 / 1 / 2. Both it and `post-upgrade` go through the ops RPC,
> and the little `bun -e` relay below returns **only `{ exitCode }`** to the host — the prose never
> crosses the container boundary, so there is nothing here for a future maintainer to regex. Getting
> to the RPC from the host is `scripts/restore.ts:170-182`'s proven pattern: production publishes no
> ops host port, so the call is a `compose exec -T gbrain-ops bun -e` that POSTs to loopback, and the
> file-plane advisory lock is taken by the ops server where it belongs (S3, `src/ops/lock.ts:31`).

> **Honesty rule 1 applies to preflight's disk check.** If free space cannot be measured, preflight
> refuses with `DISK_UNMEASURABLE` rather than assuming room. A check that never ran is not a pass,
> and "the snapshot ran out of disk halfway" is the one failure that arrives after the writers are
> already stopped. The refusals are ordered cheapest-first — version sanity, playbook resolution,
> pending checklist, repo drift, disk, then the doctor baseline — so that a refusal never leaves a
> half-captured baseline behind; that is a deliberate departure from the roadmap's listing order.

import { test, expect, describe } from 'bun:test';
import type { RepoStatus } from '../../src/ops/git.ts';
import {
  PreflightRefusal, SNAPSHOT_SLACK, UsageError,
  parseArgs, preflight, type PreflightDeps, type UpgradeContext,
} from '../../scripts/upgrade.ts';

function fakeCtx(over: Partial<UpgradeContext> = {}): UpgradeContext {
  return {
    runId: 'run-test', fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', dryRun: false,
    upgradeDir: '/srv/upgrades/run-test', envPath: '.env',
    compose: ['docker', 'compose', '-f', 'compose/docker-compose.yml'],
    journal: null, now: () => new Date('2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z'),
    ...over,
  } as UpgradeContext;
}

const cleanRepo = (sourceId: string): RepoStatus => ({
  sourceId, staleLock: false, lockAgeSeconds: null,
  uncommittedFiles: 0, unpushedCommits: 0, branch: 'main',
});

function fakeDeps(over: Partial<PreflightDeps> = {}): PreflightDeps {
  return {
    pendingDecisions: async () => [],
    resolvePlaybooks: () => [
      { version: '0.50.1.0', noPlaybookRequired: true, evidence: 'no upstream file' },
    ],
    inspectRepos: async () => [cleanRepo('handbook')],
    measureSnapshotBytes: async () => 10_000_000_000,
    freeBytes: async () => 100_000_000_000,
    captureDoctorBaseline: async () => ({
      gbrainVersion: '0.50.0.0', capturedAt: '2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z',
      status: 'healthy', healthScore: 100, categoryScores: {}, checks: [],
    }),
    pushSources: async () => true,
    ...over,
  };
}

async function refusalCode(run: () => Promise<unknown>): Promise<string> {
  try {
    await run();
  } catch (error) {
    expect(error).toBeInstanceOf(PreflightRefusal);
    return (error as PreflightRefusal).code;
  }
  throw new Error('expected preflight to refuse');
}

describe('parseArgs', () => {
  test('plan requires an explicit target', () => {
    expect(parseArgs(['plan', '--to', '0.50.5.0'])).toEqual({ cmd: 'plan', to: '0.50.5.0' });
    expect(() => parseArgs(['plan'])).toThrow(UsageError);
  });

  test('run carries the target and the confirmation flag', () => {
    expect(parseArgs(['run', '--to', '0.50.5.0', '--yes']))
      .toEqual({ cmd: 'run', to: '0.50.5.0', yes: true });
    expect(parseArgs(['run', '--to', '0.50.5.0']))
      .toEqual({ cmd: 'run', to: '0.50.5.0', yes: false });
  });

  test('a moving tag is refused by the CLI itself, not only by lint:forbidden', () => {
    for (const tag of ['latest', 'latest-stable', 'main', 'HEAD', 'v0.50.5.0']) {
      expect(() => parseArgs(['run', '--to', tag])).toThrow('not a four-part version');
    }
  });

  test('rollback addresses a run, never a version', () => {
    expect(parseArgs(['rollback', '--run', 'run-9'])).toEqual({ cmd: 'rollback', runId: 'run-9' });
    expect(() => parseArgs(['rollback'])).toThrow(UsageError);
    expect(() => parseArgs(['rollback', '--to', '0.50.0.0'])).toThrow(UsageError);
  });

  test('status takes an optional run id', () => {
    expect(parseArgs(['status'])).toEqual({ cmd: 'status', runId: null });
    expect(parseArgs(['status', '--run', 'run-9'])).toEqual({ cmd: 'status', runId: 'run-9' });
  });

  test('an unknown subcommand is a usage error, not a default', () => {
    expect(() => parseArgs(['upgrade', '--to', '0.50.5.0'])).toThrow(UsageError);
    expect(() => parseArgs([])).toThrow(UsageError);
  });
});

describe('preflight refusals', () => {
  test('a pending checklist item from a previous run refuses', async () => {
    const code = await refusalCode(() => preflight(fakeCtx(), fakeDeps({
      pendingDecisions: async () => [
        { id: 'i1', runId: 'run-old', version: '0.50.2.0', key: 'pre-0-50-0-0-cutover-attested',
          prompt: 'p', why: 'w', state: 'awaiting_admin_decision', decidedBy: null, decidedAt: null },
      ],
    })));
    expect(code).toBe('PENDING_CHECKLIST');
  });

  test('a version with no playbook refuses before anything is touched', async () => {
    let baselineCaptured = false;
    const code = await refusalCode(() => preflight(fakeCtx(), fakeDeps({
      resolvePlaybooks: () => { throw new Error('no playbook entry for 0.50.4.0'); },
      captureDoctorBaseline: async () => { baselineCaptured = true; throw new Error('unreachable'); },
    })));
    expect(code).toBe('NO_PLAYBOOK');
    expect(baselineCaptured).toBe(false);
  });

  test('uncommitted drift in a Space repo refuses', async () => {
    const code = await refusalCode(() => preflight(fakeCtx(), fakeDeps({
      inspectRepos: async () => [{ ...cleanRepo('handbook'), uncommittedFiles: 3 }],
    })));
    expect(code).toBe('REPO_DRIFT');
  });

  test('a stale git lock refuses: a crashed writer is not a quiesced one', async () => {
    const code = await refusalCode(() => preflight(fakeCtx(), fakeDeps({
      inspectRepos: async () => [{ ...cleanRepo('handbook'), staleLock: true, lockAgeSeconds: 900 }],
    })));
    expect(code).toBe('REPO_DRIFT');
  });

  test('insufficient disk for the snapshot refuses, with the slack applied', async () => {
    const code = await refusalCode(() => preflight(fakeCtx(), fakeDeps({
      measureSnapshotBytes: async () => 10_000_000_000,
      freeBytes: async () => Math.floor(10_000_000_000 * SNAPSHOT_SLACK) - 1,
    })));
    expect(code).toBe('INSUFFICIENT_DISK');
  });

  test('free space that cannot be measured refuses — a check that never ran is not a pass', async () => {
    const code = await refusalCode(() => preflight(fakeCtx(), fakeDeps({
      freeBytes: async () => { throw new Error('statfs unsupported'); },
    })));
    expect(code).toBe('DISK_UNMEASURABLE');
  });

  test('a baseline that cannot be captured refuses: there is nothing to gate against', async () => {
    const code = await refusalCode(() => preflight(fakeCtx(), fakeDeps({
      captureDoctorBaseline: async () => { throw new Error('doctor exited 127'); },
    })));
    expect(code).toBe('NO_BASELINE');
  });

  test('a target that is not ahead of the pin refuses', async () => {
    const code = await refusalCode(
      () => preflight(fakeCtx({ toVersion: '0.50.0.0' }), fakeDeps()),
    );
    expect(code).toBe('SAME_VERSION');
  });
});

describe('preflight success', () => {
  test('returns the plan, the measured numbers and the baseline', async () => {
    const report = await preflight(fakeCtx(), fakeDeps());
    expect(report.playbooks.map((p) => p.version)).toEqual(['0.50.1.0']);
    expect(report.requiredBytes).toBe(Math.ceil(10_000_000_000 * SNAPSHOT_SLACK));
    expect(report.freeBytes).toBe(100_000_000_000);
    expect(report.baseline.status).toBe('healthy');
    expect(report.sourcesPushed).toBe(true);
  });

  test('a failed sources push is reported, never fatal — it is best effort like backup.ts', async () => {
    const report = await preflight(fakeCtx(), fakeDeps({
      pushSources: async () => { throw new Error('remote unreachable'); },
    }));
    expect(report.sourcesPushed).toBe(false);
  });
});

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

`scripts/upgrade.ts`:
```ts
#!/usr/bin/env bun
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
import { mkdir, readFile, readdir, rename, statfs, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { OPERATIONS } from '../src/contracts/registry.ts';
import { scrubOutput } from '../src/docker/host.ts';
import type { RepoStatus } from '../src/ops/git.ts';
import { appendEvent, openJournal, readJournal, verifyManifest } from '../src/upgrade/manifest.ts';
import { dockerVolumeDriver } from '../src/upgrade/snapshot.ts';
import { quiesce, resume } from '../src/upgrade/quiesce.ts';
import { hasPlaybook, playbooksFor, type PlaybookSpec } from '../src/upgrade/playbooks/index.ts';
import { canTransition, resumeFrom, type UpgradeRunState } from '../src/upgrade/state.ts';
import { compareDoctor, rollback, stateDigest } from '../src/upgrade/digest.ts';
import type {
  DoctorBaseline, Journal, SnapshotManifest, UpgradeContext,
} from '../src/upgrade/types.ts';

export type { UpgradeContext };

/** D2: the journal lives here and is the authority while the control plane is down. */
export const UPGRADE_DIR = process.env.UPGRADE_DIR ?? '/srv/upgrades';

/** Images built at the swap. Only gbrain-ops is RECREATED there — see U4 above. */
export const SWAP_SERVICES = ['gbrain-ops', 'gbrain-serve', 'gbrain-worker', 'control-plane'] as const;

/** Tars are uncompressed and pg_dump output is text, so the raw sum is the floor. */
export const SNAPSHOT_SLACK = 1.25;

const VERSION_RE = /^\d+\.\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/;

// Same prefix as scripts/backup.ts:21-25. Project dir stays compose/ so
// ./Caddyfile and ./initdb resolve.
const COMPOSE = [
  'docker', 'compose',
  ...(existsSync('.env') ? ['--env-file', '.env'] as const : []),
  '-f', 'compose/docker-compose.yml',
];

export class UsageError extends Error {}

export type PreflightCode =
  | 'SAME_VERSION' | 'NO_PLAYBOOK' | 'PENDING_CHECKLIST' | 'REPO_DRIFT'
  | 'DISK_UNMEASURABLE' | 'INSUFFICIENT_DISK' | 'NO_BASELINE';

export class PreflightRefusal extends Error {
  constructor(readonly code: PreflightCode, message: string) {
    super(message);
    this.name = 'PreflightRefusal';
  }
}

async function sh(argv: string[], timeoutMs = 900_000): Promise<{ exitCode: number; stderr: string }> {
  const p = Bun.spawn(argv, { stdout: 'inherit', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const timer = setTimeout(() => p.kill(), timeoutMs);
  const [stderr, exitCode] = await Promise.all([new Response(p.stderr).text(), p.exited]);
  clearTimeout(timer);
  return { exitCode, stderr: scrubOutput(stderr) };
}

async function shCapture(
  argv: string[], timeoutMs = 900_000,
): Promise<{ exitCode: number; stdout: string; stderr: string }> {
  const p = Bun.spawn(argv, { stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const timer = setTimeout(() => p.kill(), timeoutMs);
  try {
    const [stdout, stderr, exitCode] = await Promise.all([
      new Response(p.stdout).text(), new Response(p.stderr).text(), p.exited,
    ]);
    return { exitCode, stdout: scrubOutput(stdout), stderr: scrubOutput(stderr) };
  } finally {
    clearTimeout(timer);
  }
}

async function feedString(
  argv: string[], input: string, timeoutMs = 900_000,
): Promise<{ exitCode: number; stdout: string; stderr: string }> {
  const p = Bun.spawn(argv, { stdin: 'pipe', stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const timer = setTimeout(() => p.kill(), timeoutMs);
  try {
    await p.stdin.write(input);
    await p.stdin.end();
    const [stdout, stderr, exitCode] = await Promise.all([
      new Response(p.stdout).text(), new Response(p.stderr).text(), p.exited,
    ]);
    return { exitCode, stdout, stderr: scrubOutput(stderr) };
  } finally {
    clearTimeout(timer);
  }
}

/**
 * Reach the ops RPC from the host. Production publishes no ops port (S2), so
 * this is scripts/restore.ts:170-182's pattern: a bun program inside the
 * container POSTs to loopback. The file-plane advisory lock is taken by the ops
 * server around the call (S3), which is the only place it can be taken.
 *
 * Only `exitCode` is relayed back. apply-migrations has no --json and its prose
 * is not ours to parse (U3, G3) — keeping the text inside the container means
 * there is nothing here for anyone to start regexing later.
 */
const OPS_RPC_PROGRAM = `
let input = '';
for await (const chunk of process.stdin) input += chunk;
const base = process.env.OPS_RPC_URL ?? 'http://127.0.0.1:3140';
const res = await fetch(base + '/rpc', {
  method: 'POST',
  headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json', authorization: 'Bearer ' + process.env.OPS_RPC_TOKEN },
  body: input,
});
if (!res.ok) { console.error('ops rpc -> ' + res.status); process.exit(1); }
const payload = await res.json();
console.log(JSON.stringify({ exitCode: payload.exitCode }));
`;

export async function opsRpc(
  ctx: UpgradeContext, op: string, params: Record<string, unknown>, timeoutMs = 900_000,
): Promise<{ exitCode: number }> {
  const r = await feedString(
    [...ctx.compose, 'exec', '-T', 'gbrain-ops', 'bun', '-e', OPS_RPC_PROGRAM],
    JSON.stringify({ op, params, timeoutMs }),
    timeoutMs,
  );
  if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`ops rpc ${op} relay failed (${r.exitCode}): ${r.stderr}`);
  return JSON.parse(r.stdout.trim()) as { exitCode: number };
}

/**
 * Key NAMES and the env var only (S3c). post-upgrade adds self_upgrade.mode and
 * self_upgrade.mode_prompted to config.json (U4); GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE
 * overrides config (U5), so the kill switch holds — but the run records what
 * changed instead of ignoring it, and fails if the env var itself went missing,
 * which is exactly what v0.50.5.0's .env guardrail quarantine could do (U12).
 */
const SELF_UPGRADE_PROBE_PROGRAM = `
const home = process.env.GBRAIN_HOME ?? '/srv/gbrain-home';
let keys = [];
try {
  const raw = await Bun.file(home + '/.gbrain/config.json').text();
  const flat = (o, p) => Object.entries(o).flatMap(([k, v]) =>
    v && typeof v === 'object' && !Array.isArray(v) ? flat(v, p + k + '.') : [p + k]);
  keys = flat(JSON.parse(raw), '').sort();
} catch {}
console.log(JSON.stringify({ mode: process.env.GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE ?? '', keys }));
`;

async function probeSelfUpgrade(ctx: UpgradeContext): Promise<{ mode: string; keys: string[] }> {
  const r = await shCapture(
    [...ctx.compose, 'exec', '-T', 'gbrain-ops', 'bun', '-e', SELF_UPGRADE_PROBE_PROGRAM], 60_000,
  );
  if (r.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`self-upgrade probe failed (${r.exitCode}): ${r.stderr}`);
  return JSON.parse(r.stdout.trim()) as { mode: string; keys: string[] };
}

export type ParsedArgs =
  | { cmd: 'plan'; to: string }
  | { cmd: 'run'; to: string; yes: boolean }
  | { cmd: 'rollback'; runId: string }
  | { cmd: 'status'; runId: string | null };

function flag(argv: string[], name: string): string | null {
  const i = argv.indexOf(name);
  return i >= 0 && argv[i + 1] !== undefined ? argv[i + 1]! : null;
}

export function parseArgs(argv: string[]): ParsedArgs {
  const cmd = argv[0];
  const to = flag(argv, '--to');
  const runId = flag(argv, '--run');
  const requireVersion = (v: string | null): string => {
    if (!v) throw new UsageError('--to <version> is required');
    // G1: the pin never moves to a moving tag, and the CLI says so itself
    // rather than leaving it to lint:forbidden's `moving-pin` rule.
    if (!VERSION_RE.test(v)) throw new UsageError(`${v} is not a four-part version (e.g. 0.50.5.0)`);
    return v;
  };

  switch (cmd) {
    case 'plan': return { cmd: 'plan', to: requireVersion(to) };
    case 'run': return { cmd: 'run', to: requireVersion(to), yes: argv.includes('--yes') };
    case 'rollback':
      if (!runId) throw new UsageError('rollback requires --run <id>: a rollback addresses a run');
      return { cmd: 'rollback', runId };
    case 'status': return { cmd: 'status', runId };
    default:
      throw new UsageError(
        'usage: bun run scripts/upgrade.ts <plan|run|rollback|status> [--to <version>] [--run <id>] [--yes]',
      );
  }
}

export async function buildContext(
  args: { to: string; dryRun: boolean }, runId = `run-${randomUUID()}`,
): Promise<UpgradeContext> {
  const fromVersion = process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0';
  const upgradeDir = join(UPGRADE_DIR, runId);
  let journal: Journal | null = null;
  if (!args.dryRun) {
    await mkdir(upgradeDir, { recursive: true });
    journal = await openJournal(upgradeDir, runId);
  }
  return {
    runId, fromVersion, toVersion: args.to, dryRun: args.dryRun,
    upgradeDir, envPath: '.env', compose: [...COMPOSE],
    journal: journal as Journal, now: () => new Date(),
  };
}

export type PreflightDeps = {
  pendingDecisions(): Promise<{ id: string; runId: string; version: string; key: string }[]>;
  resolvePlaybooks(from: string, to: string): PlaybookSpec[];
  inspectRepos(): Promise<RepoStatus[]>;
  measureSnapshotBytes(): Promise<number>;
  freeBytes(path: string): Promise<number>;
  captureDoctorBaseline(): Promise<DoctorBaseline>;
  pushSources(): Promise<boolean>;
};

export type PreflightReport = {
  fromVersion: string;
  toVersion: string;
  playbooks: PlaybookSpec[];
  snapshotBytes: number;
  requiredBytes: number;
  freeBytes: number;
  repos: RepoStatus[];
  baseline: DoctorBaseline;
  sourcesPushed: boolean;
};

/**
 * Every refusal here happens BEFORE anything is touched, and the order is
 * cheapest-first so that a refusal never leaves a half-captured baseline.
 */
export async function preflight(ctx: UpgradeContext, deps: PreflightDeps): Promise<PreflightReport> {
  if (ctx.toVersion === ctx.fromVersion) {
    throw new PreflightRefusal('SAME_VERSION', `already pinned at ${ctx.fromVersion}`);
  }

  let playbooks: PlaybookSpec[];
  try {
    playbooks = deps.resolvePlaybooks(ctx.fromVersion, ctx.toVersion);
  } catch (e) {
    throw new PreflightRefusal('NO_PLAYBOOK', (e as Error).message);
  }

  const pending = await deps.pendingDecisions();
  if (pending.length > 0) {
    throw new PreflightRefusal(
      'PENDING_CHECKLIST',
      `${pending.length} checklist item(s) from a previous run are still undecided: ` +
      `${pending.map((p) => `${p.version}/${p.key}`).join(', ')}. Decide them in the console first — ` +
      'stacking an upgrade on an unresolved judgement call makes both unattributable.',
    );
  }

  const repos = await deps.inspectRepos();
  const dirty = repos.filter((r) => r.uncommittedFiles > 0 || r.unpushedCommits > 0 || r.staleLock);
  if (dirty.length > 0) {
    throw new PreflightRefusal(
      'REPO_DRIFT',
      `Space repos have drift the snapshot would freeze: ${dirty.map((r) =>
        `${r.sourceId}(${r.uncommittedFiles} uncommitted, ${r.unpushedCommits} unpushed` +
        `${r.staleLock ? ', STALE LOCK' : ''})`).join(', ')}`,
    );
  }

  const snapshotBytes = await deps.measureSnapshotBytes();
  const requiredBytes = Math.ceil(snapshotBytes * SNAPSHOT_SLACK);
  let free: number;
  try {
    free = await deps.freeBytes(UPGRADE_DIR);
  } catch (e) {
    throw new PreflightRefusal(
      'DISK_UNMEASURABLE',
      `free space at ${UPGRADE_DIR} could not be measured (${(e as Error).message}); ` +
      'refusing rather than assuming room — a snapshot that runs out of disk does so with every ' +
      'writer already stopped',
    );
  }
  if (free < requiredBytes) {
    throw new PreflightRefusal(
      'INSUFFICIENT_DISK',
      `the snapshot needs ~${requiredBytes} bytes at ${UPGRADE_DIR} (measured ${snapshotBytes} ` +
      `plus ${Math.round((SNAPSHOT_SLACK - 1) * 100)}% slack) and only ${free} are free`,
    );
  }

  let baseline: DoctorBaseline;
  try {
    baseline = await deps.captureDoctorBaseline();
  } catch (e) {
    throw new PreflightRefusal(
      'NO_BASELINE',
      `the doctor baseline could not be captured (${(e as Error).message}); without it the exit ` +
      'gate has nothing to compare against and would pass vacuously (D5)',
    );
  }

  // Best effort, exactly like scripts/backup.ts:137-138: the git remotes are
  // the durable copy of a Space's content, but an unreachable remote is not a
  // reason to refuse an upgrade. Reported, never fatal.
  const sourcesPushed = await deps.pushSources().catch(() => false);

  return {
    fromVersion: ctx.fromVersion, toVersion: ctx.toVersion, playbooks,
    snapshotBytes, requiredBytes, freeBytes: free, repos, baseline, sourcesPushed,
  };
}

/** Atomic pin rewrite. Returns the value it replaced. */
export async function writePin(envPath: string, version: string): Promise<string> {
  const raw = await readFile(envPath, 'utf8');
  const match = /^GBRAIN_VERSION=(.*)$/m.exec(raw);
  if (!match) throw new Error(`${envPath} has no GBRAIN_VERSION line to move`);
  const next = raw.replace(/^GBRAIN_VERSION=.*$/m, `GBRAIN_VERSION=${version}`);
  const tmp = `${envPath}.upgrade-tmp`;
  await writeFile(tmp, next, { mode: 0o600 });
  await rename(tmp, envPath);
  return match[1]!.trim();
}

export type RunDeps = PreflightDeps & {
  snapshot(ctx: UpgradeContext, drain: unknown): Promise<SnapshotManifest>;
  emitOperations(version: string): Promise<void>;
  buildImages(ctx: UpgradeContext): Promise<void>;
  recreate(ctx: UpgradeContext, services: readonly string[]): Promise<void>;
  captureDoctorAfter(ctx: UpgradeContext): Promise<DoctorBaseline>;
  rollback(ctx: UpgradeContext, runId: string): Promise<{ ok: boolean }>;
};

export type UpgradeReport = {
  runId: string; fromVersion: string; toVersion: string;
  state: UpgradeRunState; ok: boolean; rolledBack: boolean; notes: string[];
};

/**
 * The roadmap's eight steps, in order, each journaled before the next begins
 * (D2). Every transition goes through canTransition, so an illegal one throws
 * here rather than being written to the journal and inherited by the reconciler.
 */
export async function runUpgrade(ctx: UpgradeContext, deps: RunDeps): Promise<UpgradeReport> {
  const notes: string[] = [];
  let state: UpgradeRunState = 'planned';
  const enter = async (to: UpgradeRunState, note: string, data: Record<string, unknown> = {}) => {
    if (!canTransition(state, to)) throw new Error(`illegal upgrade transition ${state} -> ${to}`);
    state = to;
    notes.push(note);
    await appendEvent(ctx.journal, { state: to, note, data });
  };
  await appendEvent(ctx.journal, {
    state: 'planned', note: `${ctx.fromVersion} -> ${ctx.toVersion}`, data: {},
  });

  // 1. Preflight.
  const pre = await preflight(ctx, deps);
  await writeFile(join(ctx.upgradeDir, 'doctor-baseline.json'),
    `${JSON.stringify(pre.baseline, null, 2)}\n`);
  const before = await stateDigest(ctx);
  await writeFile(join(ctx.upgradeDir, 'digest-before.json'),
    `${JSON.stringify(before, null, 2)}\n`);
  await enter('preflight_ok', 'preflight passed and the pre-upgrade digest is on disk', {
    playbooks: pre.playbooks.map((p) => p.version),
    requiredBytes: pre.requiredBytes, freeBytes: pre.freeBytes, sourcesPushed: pre.sourcesPushed,
    digestBefore: before.parts.map((p) => `${p.name}:${p.sha256.slice(0, 12)}`),
  });

  try {
    // 2. Quiesce. D3: the order is the atomicity guarantee.
    const drain = await quiesce(ctx);
    await enter('quiesced', 'stack drained to Postgres only', {
      stopped: drain.stopped, drainProof: drain.proof, drainMs: drain.elapsedMs,
    });

    // 3. Snapshot, verified immediately. An unverifiable snapshot aborts here —
    //    before the swap — because after the swap it is the only way back (D4).
    const manifest = await deps.snapshot(ctx, drain);
    const verified = await verifyManifest(manifest, ctx.upgradeDir);
    if (!verified.ok) {
      throw new Error(`snapshot failed verification: ${verified.mismatches.join(', ')}`);
    }
    await enter('snapshot_taken', 'four artifacts written and checksum-verified', {
      artifacts: manifest.artifacts.map((a) => `${a.name}:${a.bytes}:${a.sha256.slice(0, 12)}`),
    });

    // 4. Swap. The old pin is already durable in manifest.fromVersion, so a
    //    death between the rewrite and the journal append still leaves rollback
    //    able to restore it. D8: operations:emit BEFORE the control-plane image
    //    builds — docker/control-plane.Dockerfile:18-19 gates on that file.
    const previousPin = await writePin(ctx.envPath, ctx.toVersion);
    await deps.emitOperations(ctx.toVersion);
    await deps.buildImages(ctx);
    await deps.recreate(ctx, ['gbrain-ops']);
    await enter('swapped', 'pin moved, images built, gbrain-ops recreated at the new tag', {
      previousPin, pin: ctx.toVersion, built: [...SWAP_SERVICES], recreated: ['gbrain-ops'],
    });

    // 5. Schema. Exit codes only (U3). gbrain-ops is the only live gbrain
    //    process, so post-upgrade's lock-free config.json rewrite has nobody to
    //    race (U4) and still runs behind ops' advisory lock (S3).
    const migrateExit = (await opsRpc(ctx, 'applyMigrations', { yes: true, nonInteractive: true })).exitCode;
    if (migrateExit !== 0) throw new Error(`apply-migrations exited ${migrateExit}`);
    const postExit = (await opsRpc(ctx, 'postUpgrade', {})).exitCode;
    if (postExit !== 0) throw new Error(`post-upgrade exited ${postExit}`);

    const guard = await probeSelfUpgrade(ctx);
    if (guard.mode !== 'off') {
      throw new Error(
        `GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE is '${guard.mode}' in gbrain-ops, not 'off'. The env var ` +
        'overrides config (U5), so this is the kill switch itself going missing — the likeliest ' +
        "cause is v0.50.5.0's .env guardrail quarantining GBRAIN_* vars (U12).",
      );
    }
    const addedKeys = guard.keys.filter((k) => !before.configKeys?.includes(k));
    await enter('migrated',
      'apply-migrations and post-upgrade both exited 0. On the 0.50.0.0 -> 0.50.5.0 delta upstream ' +
      'ships zero schema migrations (U10), so "nothing applied" is the expected result; a non-zero ' +
      'exit or a surprise here is a finding for docs/slice-3.5-findings.md, not something to wave ' +
      'through.',
      { applyExit: 0, postExit: 0, selfUpgradeMode: guard.mode, configKeysAdded: addedKeys });

    // 6. Playbooks. Deterministic steps run; checklist items are OPENED as
    //    awaiting_admin_decision. The control plane is down, so both are
    //    journaled for the reconciler rather than written to the DB (D2).
    const ledger: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];
    const checklist: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];
    for (const spec of pre.playbooks) {
      if (!hasPlaybook(spec)) {
        ledger.push({
          version: spec.version, state: 'skipped',
          notes: `upstream ships no playbook for this version — ${spec.evidence}`,
        });
        continue;
      }
      for (const step of spec.steps) await step.run(ctx);
      for (const item of spec.checklist) checklist.push({ version: spec.version, ...item });
      ledger.push({
        version: spec.version,
        state: spec.checklist.length > 0 ? 'awaiting_admin_decision' : 'applied',
        notes:
          `applied ${spec.steps.length} deterministic step(s) ` +
          `[${spec.steps.map((s) => s.id).join(', ')}] hand-translated from ` +
          `${spec.upstreamSkill.path}@${spec.upstreamSkill.sha256.slice(0, 12)}; ` +
          `opened ${spec.checklist.length} checklist item(s)`,
      });
    }
    await enter('playbooks_applied', `${ledger.length} version(s) recorded`, { ledger, checklist });

    // 7. Resume, then gate. The writers come back here, not at the swap.
    await deps.recreate(ctx, ['gbrain-serve', 'gbrain-worker', 'control-plane']);
    await resume(ctx);
    const after = await deps.captureDoctorAfter(ctx);
    const parity = compareDoctor(pre.baseline, after);
    if (parity.regressed.length > 0 || !parity.schemaVersionOk) {
      throw new Error(
        `doctor parity gate failed: regressed=[${parity.regressed.map((c) => c.name).join(', ')}] ` +
        `schemaVersionOk=${parity.schemaVersionOk}`,
      );
    }
    await enter('gated_ok', 'doctor parity holds per check (D5)', {
      regressed: [], resolved: parity.resolved.map((c) => c.name),
      newChecks: parity.newChecks.map((c) => c.name),
      status: after.status, healthScore: after.healthScore,
    });

    await enter('completed', `upgraded ${ctx.fromVersion} -> ${ctx.toVersion}`, {});
    return {
      runId: ctx.runId, fromVersion: ctx.fromVersion, toVersion: ctx.toVersion,
      state, ok: true, rolledBack: false, notes,
    };
  } catch (error) {
    const message = scrubOutput((error as Error).message);
    // 8. Post-swap failure has exactly one destination (Task 10).
    if (!canTransition(state, 'rolling_back')) {
      await appendEvent(ctx.journal, { state: 'failed', note: message, data: {} });
      return {
        runId: ctx.runId, fromVersion: ctx.fromVersion, toVersion: ctx.toVersion,
        state: 'failed', ok: false, rolledBack: false, notes: [...notes, message],
      };
    }
    await enter('rolling_back', message, {});
    const result = await deps.rollback(ctx, ctx.runId);
    await appendEvent(ctx.journal, {
      state: result.ok ? 'rolled_back' : 'rollback_failed', note: message, data: {},
    });
    return {
      runId: ctx.runId, fromVersion: ctx.fromVersion, toVersion: ctx.toVersion,
      state: result.ok ? 'rolled_back' : 'rollback_failed',
      ok: false, rolledBack: result.ok, notes: [...notes, message],
    };
  }
}

/** Dry run. Prints what would happen and changes nothing — no journal, no files. */
export async function planUpgrade(ctx: UpgradeContext, deps: PreflightDeps) {
  const pre = await preflight(ctx, deps);
  return {
    from: pre.fromVersion, to: pre.toVersion,
    playbooks: pre.playbooks.map((p) => hasPlaybook(p)
      ? { version: p.version, steps: p.steps.map((s) => s.id), checklist: p.checklist.map((c) => c.key),
          upstreamSkill: p.upstreamSkill.path }
      : { version: p.version, noPlaybookRequired: true, evidence: p.evidence }),
    snapshot: {
      artifacts: [...dockerVolumeDriver.describe()],
      measuredBytes: pre.snapshotBytes, requiredBytes: pre.requiredBytes, freeBytes: pre.freeBytes,
    },
    repos: pre.repos.map((r) => r.sourceId),
    baseline: { status: pre.baseline.status, checks: pre.baseline.checks.length },
    sourcesPushed: pre.sourcesPushed,
    wouldSwap: [...SWAP_SERVICES],
  };
}

/** Read run state off disk. Works with the control plane down, by design (D2). */
export async function statusOf(runId: string | null) {
  const ids = runId
    ? [runId]
    : (await readdir(UPGRADE_DIR, { withFileTypes: true }).catch(() => []))
        .filter((e) => e.isDirectory()).map((e) => e.name);
  const out = [];
  for (const id of ids) {
    const events = await readJournal(join(UPGRADE_DIR, id), id).catch(() => []);
    out.push({ runId: id, events: events.length, decision: resumeFrom(events) });
  }
  return out;
}

if (import.meta.main) {
  // 0 ok · 1 the upgrade failed · 2 usage · 3 preflight refused, nothing touched
  // · 4 the rollback itself failed (break-glass).
  try {
    const args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
    if (args.cmd === 'status') {
      console.log(JSON.stringify(await statusOf(args.runId), null, 2));
      process.exit(0);
    }
    if (args.cmd === 'rollback') {
      const ctx = await buildContext({ to: process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0', dryRun: false }, args.runId);
      const r = await rollback(ctx, args.runId);
      console.log(JSON.stringify(r, null, 2));
      process.exit(r.ok ? 0 : 4);
    }
    const ctx = await buildContext({ to: args.to, dryRun: args.cmd === 'plan' });
    const deps = await defaultDeps(ctx);
    if (args.cmd === 'plan') {
      console.log(JSON.stringify(await planUpgrade(ctx, deps), null, 2));
      process.exit(0);
    }
    if (!args.yes) {
      console.error('run requires --yes: this stops the stack, moves the pin and rebuilds images');
      process.exit(2);
    }
    const report = await runUpgrade(ctx, deps);
    console.log(JSON.stringify(report, null, 2));
    process.exit(report.ok ? 0 : (report.state === 'rollback_failed' ? 4 : 1));
  } catch (error) {
    if (error instanceof UsageError) { console.error(error.message); process.exit(2); }
    if (error instanceof PreflightRefusal) {
      console.error(`[upgrade] refused (${error.code}): ${error.message}`);
      console.error('Nothing was touched.');
      process.exit(3);
    }
    console.error(scrubOutput(String(error)));
    process.exit(1);
  }
}
```

> `defaultDeps(ctx)` is the last thing to write and the least interesting: it wires
> `deps.resolvePlaybooks` to `playbooksFor`, `deps.inspectRepos` to the existing
> `/git/inspect` route (`src/control/opsClient.ts:63-66` has the client; the host reaches it through
> the same `bun -e` relay), `deps.snapshot` to `dockerVolumeDriver.take`, `deps.emitOperations` to
> `['bun', 'run', 'operations:emit']` with `GBRAIN_VERSION` set to the new pin in the child env,
> `deps.buildImages` to `[...COMPOSE, 'build', ...SWAP_SERVICES]`, `deps.recreate` to
> `[...COMPOSE, 'up', '-d', '--force-recreate', '--wait', ...services]`, `deps.freeBytes` to
> `statfs`, and both doctor captures to
> `[...ctx.compose, 'exec', '-T', 'gbrain-ops', 'gbrain', ...OPERATIONS.doctor.argv, '--json']` —
> argv from the registry, never hand-typed, so the allowlist still owns the command even though the
> transport is the production compose file rather than `src/docker/host.ts`'s slice0 one.

- [ ] **Step 4: Run the test to verify it passes**

Run: `bun test test/unit/upgrade-cli.test.ts`
Expected: PASS, 17 tests.

The assertion that matters is `a version with no playbook refuses before anything is touched` — and
specifically its `expect(baselineCaptured).toBe(false)`. It is the only mechanical proof that the
refusal ordering is real rather than incidental, and it is what makes "fail here, before touching
anything" a property instead of a comment. If it goes red, do not move the assertion; move the
check.

**This unit test is not the proof that `run` works.** It drives `parseArgs` and `preflight` against
injected fakes, and that is all it can honestly claim: nothing in `test/unit` starts a container,
moves a pin or takes a snapshot. The end-to-end proof is Gate C (Task 17), which runs the real
upgrade on the dev stack, fails a real migration against a fixtured conflict (D9), and rolls back
with the one command. Do not add Docker to this file to make it feel more complete.

bun run lint:forbidden
git add scripts/upgrade.ts test/unit/upgrade-cli.test.ts
git commit -m "feat(slice3.5): host upgrade CLI with plan, run and journaled transitions

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
