## Task 12: Rollback, and the digest that defines "exactly"

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — `StateDigest`/`RollbackReport` were completed by the exit-gate dispatch,
> and the shipped shapes are not the plan's.** Task 12 shipped `StateDigest` without
> `filePlane`/`pages`/`sources`/`controlRowCounts` and `RollbackReport` without
> `method`/`ranUpstreamDownMigrations`; L7 and D4 name both, so the Task 17 dispatch completed them.
> The real shapes: `compareDigests` returns **`{ equal, differing, onlyInA, onlyInB }`** (there is no
> `.differences` alias); `verifyRuntimePin` exposes **`observed`** (not `.running`); `/api/health`
> exposes **`pin`** (not `gbrain.pinMatches`). `RollbackReport.method` is `'snapshot-restore'` and
> `ranUpstreamDownMigrations` is `false` (U8). The plan's `.differences`/`.running`/`gbrain.pinMatches`
> were drafting errors. Rollback also gained a bounded step watchdog after F4.

> **Corrected 2026-09-17 — the `filePlane` part must not count gbrain's own runtime state.** The digest's `filePlane` part hashes the relative path and size of every regular file under `GBRAIN_HOME`, but gbrain writes its own operational state there on every run — `migrations/completed.jsonl` and `pending-host-work.jsonl`, `credential-deliveries/*`, `run/secret-*`, `last-update-check`, `model-cache.json`, `preferences.json`, `backup-status.json` / `backup-nag-state.json`. No rollback can hold those constant, so C4/C11's "restores exactly" compared churn and failed in the family run (`[rollback] state differs after restore: filePlane`, 30 → 34 items). `FILE_PLANE_PROGRAM` (`src/upgrade/digest.ts`) now skips that runtime set (and, since Task 17, the worker registry), so the part is CONTENT; `configKeys` still carries the config's key names, the snapshot still restores those files, and loss of real content is still caught. See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` F8.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/upgrade/digest.ts`
- Modify: `scripts/upgrade.ts` (the `rollback` subcommand's implementation)
- Test: `test/unit/upgrade-digest.test.ts`
- Test: `test/integration/upgrade-rollback.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `SnapshotManifest`, `verifyManifest` (Task 4); `SnapshotDriver`, `dockerVolumeDriver`
  (Task 5); `quiesce`, `resume` (Task 6); `readJournal`, `appendEvent` (Task 4); `verifyRuntimePin`
  (Task 2); `writePin`, `opsRpc`, `UpgradeContext` (Task 11); `OPERATIONS['sources.status']`
  (`src/contracts/registry.ts:478-486`).
- Produces: `type DigestPart`; `type StateDigest`; `type DigestComparison`;
  `stateDigest(ctx, deps?): Promise<StateDigest>`; `compareDigests(a, b): DigestComparison`;
  `type DoctorParity`; `compareDoctor(baseline, after): DoctorParity`;
  `type RollbackReport`; `class RollbackRefusal`; `rollback(ctx, runId, deps?)`.

> **D5's comparator ships here.** The file structure gives `compareDoctor` no home of its own, and it
> is the same kind of thing as `compareDigests` — two captured states in, a named list of differences
> out — so it lives beside it. If a later reviewer wants it split out, the split is mechanical; what
> must not happen is it ending up inline in `scripts/upgrade.ts`, where nothing unit-tests it.

> **D4 and honesty rule 5 apply here.** `rollback` is one operation: verify the manifest, restore the
> four artifacts, restore the pin, recreate the services at the old tag, resume, verify. There is no
> reverse-migration half to write — no down-migrations exist anywhere upstream and
> `OrchestratorResult` has no rollback state (U8) — so snapshot-restore is not one strategy among
> several, it is the only thing rollback could mean. It reads the journal and manifest **from disk**,
> never from the control DB, because the case it exists for is the one where the control plane never
> came back up.

> **G3 governs what `stateDigest` is allowed to look at.** Every part is computed through a published
> contract or through our own file plane — no reading gbrain's tables, no scraping prose. The brain
> part is `sources status --json`, whose argv comes from the registry and whose `--json` is proven by
> an existing contract test (`test/contract/lifecycle.contract.test.ts:105-110`), **not** a search.
> A search would walk straight into L13: a non-empty `--federated-read` makes that list the *full*
> scope, and an unqualified read only sees a Space whose `config.federated` is literally `true` (L6),
> so a search-based page set can shrink silently and still look like a match. The source enumeration
> cannot. Content identity is carried by the git tree hashes in the `repos` part, which is where page
> content actually lives. The digest still records the scope string it used, inside the hashed
> material, so two digests can only compare equal if they were taken the same way.

> **The digest deliberately does not count its own bookkeeping.** `upgrade_runs`, `upgrade_events`,
> `upgrade_checklist_items`, `playbook_ledger`, `capability_snapshots` and `audit_log` are excluded
> from the control-DB row counts, and the exclusion list is hashed into the part so it cannot be
> widened quietly. Rollback restores the control DB from the snapshot, which erases the run's own
> rows; the reconciler then replays them from the journal (D2). Counting those tables would make the
> digest assert "the upgrade left no trace", which is the opposite of what this system is for.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`test/unit/upgrade-digest.test.ts`:
```ts
import { test, expect, describe } from 'bun:test';
import { compareDigests, compareDoctor, type StateDigest } from '../../src/upgrade/digest.ts';

const part = (name: string, sha256: string, items = 1) => ({ name, sha256, items, scope: 'test' });

const base: StateDigest = {
  takenAt: '2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z',
  gbrainVersion: '0.50.0.0',
  configKeys: ['embedding_model', 'self_upgrade.mode'],
  parts: [
    part('pin', 'a'.repeat(64)),
    part('sources', 'b'.repeat(64), 3),
    part('repos', 'c'.repeat(64), 3),
    part('configKeys', 'd'.repeat(64), 2),
    part('controlRows', 'e'.repeat(64), 14),
  ],
};

const withPart = (name: string, sha256: string): StateDigest => ({
  ...base, parts: base.parts.map((p) => (p.name === name ? { ...p, sha256 } : p)),
});

describe('compareDigests', () => {
  test('identical digests match', () => {
    const r = compareDigests(base, structuredClone(base));
    expect(r.equal).toBe(true);
    expect(r.differing).toEqual([]);
    expect(r.onlyInA).toEqual([]);
    expect(r.onlyInB).toEqual([]);
  });

  test('a digest taken at a different instant still matches — time is not state', () => {
    const later = { ...structuredClone(base), takenAt: '2026-09-16T04:00:00.000Z' };
    expect(compareDigests(base, later).equal).toBe(true);
  });

  test('one changed part is reported by name, and nothing else is', () => {
    const r = compareDigests(base, withPart('repos', 'f'.repeat(64)));
    expect(r.equal).toBe(false);
    expect(r.differing).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(r.differing[0]!.part).toBe('repos');
    expect(r.differing[0]!.a).toBe('c'.repeat(64));
    expect(r.differing[0]!.b).toBe('f'.repeat(64));
  });

  test('a failure says WHAT differs, not just that something did', () => {
    const two = { ...withPart('repos', 'f'.repeat(64)) };
    two.parts = two.parts.map((p) => (p.name === 'pin' ? { ...p, sha256: '9'.repeat(64) } : p));
    expect(compareDigests(base, two).differing.map((d) => d.part).sort())
      .toEqual(['pin', 'repos']);
  });

  test('a part present on one side only is reported as such, never as equal', () => {
    const fewer = { ...base, parts: base.parts.filter((p) => p.name !== 'controlRows') };
    const r = compareDigests(base, fewer);
    expect(r.equal).toBe(false);
    expect(r.onlyInA).toEqual(['controlRows']);
    expect(r.onlyInB).toEqual([]);
    expect(r.differing).toEqual([]);
  });

  test('the pin is a part, so a rollback that forgot the pin cannot pass', () => {
    expect(compareDigests(base, withPart('pin', '1'.repeat(64))).equal).toBe(false);
  });
});

const check = (name: string, status: 'ok' | 'warn' | 'fail') => ({ name, status, message: '' });
const report = (checks: ReturnType<typeof check>[]) => ({
  gbrainVersion: '0.50.0.0', capturedAt: '2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z',
  status: 'healthy', healthScore: 100, categoryScores: {}, checks,
});

describe('compareDoctor', () => {
  test('an ok check that is now warn is a regression', () => {
    const r = compareDoctor(
      report([check('schema_version', 'ok'), check('embeddings', 'ok')]),
      report([check('schema_version', 'ok'), check('embeddings', 'warn')]),
    );
    expect(r.regressed.map((c) => c.name)).toEqual(['embeddings']);
    expect(r.schemaVersionOk).toBe(true);
  });

  test('a check that did not exist in the baseline is news, not a regression (D5)', () => {
    const r = compareDoctor(
      report([check('schema_version', 'ok')]),
      report([check('schema_version', 'ok'), check('oauth_scope_ceiling', 'warn')]),
    );
    expect(r.regressed).toEqual([]);
    expect(r.newChecks.map((c) => c.name)).toEqual(['oauth_scope_ceiling']);
  });

  test('a check that improved is reported as resolved', () => {
    const r = compareDoctor(
      report([check('schema_version', 'ok'), check('provider_sunset', 'fail')]),
      report([check('schema_version', 'ok'), check('provider_sunset', 'ok')]),
    );
    expect(r.resolved.map((c) => c.name)).toEqual(['provider_sunset']);
  });

  test('a warn on schema_version fails the gate even though doctor exits 0 (U7)', () => {
    const r = compareDoctor(
      report([check('schema_version', 'ok')]),
      report([check('schema_version', 'warn')]),
    );
    expect(r.schemaVersionOk).toBe(false);
  });

  test('a schema_version check that vanished is not a pass', () => {
    const r = compareDoctor(report([check('schema_version', 'ok')]), report([check('other', 'ok')]));
    expect(r.schemaVersionOk).toBe(false);
  });
});
```

`test/integration/upgrade-rollback.test.ts`:
```ts
import { test, expect, describe, beforeEach, afterEach } from 'bun:test';
import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { RollbackRefusal, rollback } from '../../src/upgrade/digest.ts';
import type { UpgradeContext } from '../../scripts/upgrade.ts';

let dir: string;
const sha = (s: string) => createHash('sha256').update(s).digest('hex');

const ARTIFACTS = [
  { name: 'brainDb', file: 'brain.sql', body: '-- brain dump\n' },
  { name: 'controlDb', file: 'control.sql', body: '-- control dump\n' },
  { name: 'brainRepos', file: 'brain-repos.tar', body: 'repos-tar-bytes' },
  { name: 'gbrainHome', file: 'gbrain-home.tar', body: 'home-tar-bytes' },
];

async function seedRun(tamper: string | null): Promise<{ ctx: UpgradeContext; calls: string[] }> {
  const runDir = join(dir, 'run-1');
  await Bun.write(join(runDir, '.keep'), '');
  for (const a of ARTIFACTS) {
    const body = tamper === a.name ? `${a.body}X` : a.body;
    await writeFile(join(runDir, a.file), body);
  }
  const manifest = {
    id: 'snap-1', runId: 'run-1', takenAt: '2026-09-16T00:00:00.000Z',
    fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', driver: 'docker-volume',
    artifacts: ARTIFACTS.map((a) => ({
      name: a.name, path: a.file, bytes: a.body.length, sha256: sha(a.body),
    })),
    quiesce: { stopped: [], proof: 'test' },
  };
  await writeFile(join(runDir, 'manifest.json'), `${JSON.stringify(manifest, null, 2)}\n`);
  await writeFile(join(runDir, 'journal.ndjson'),
    `${JSON.stringify({ runId: 'run-1', seq: 1, at: manifest.takenAt, state: 'swapped' })}\n`);

  const calls: string[] = [];
  const ctx = {
    runId: 'run-1', fromVersion: '0.50.5.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', dryRun: false,
    upgradeDir: runDir, envPath: join(runDir, '.env'),
    compose: ['docker', 'compose'], journal: null, now: () => new Date(),
  } as unknown as UpgradeContext;
  await writeFile(ctx.envPath, 'GBRAIN_VERSION=0.50.5.0\n');
  return { ctx, calls };
}

function fakeDeps(calls: string[]) {
  return {
    driver: {
      id: 'fake',
      async restore() { calls.push('restore'); },
      async take() { throw new Error('not used'); },
      async verify() { calls.push('driver-verify'); return { ok: true, mismatches: [] }; },
      describe() { return []; },
    },
    async quiesce() { calls.push('quiesce'); return { stopped: [], proof: 'test', elapsedMs: 1 }; },
    async resume() { calls.push('resume'); },
    async recreate() { calls.push('recreate'); },
    async verifyRuntimePin() { calls.push('verify-pin'); return true; },
    async stateDigest() {
      calls.push('digest');
      return { takenAt: 'x', gbrainVersion: '0.50.0.0', configKeys: [], parts: [] };
    },
  };
}

beforeEach(async () => { dir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'brainstand-rollback-')); });
afterEach(async () => { await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }); });

describe('rollback', () => {
  test('a tampered artifact aborts the rollback before anything is restored', async () => {
    const { ctx, calls } = await seedRun('brainRepos');
    let refusal: RollbackRefusal | null = null;
    try {
      await rollback(ctx, 'run-1', fakeDeps(calls));
    } catch (e) {
      refusal = e as RollbackRefusal;
    }
    expect(refusal).toBeInstanceOf(RollbackRefusal);
    expect(refusal!.code).toBe('MANIFEST_VERIFY_FAILED');
    expect(refusal!.message).toContain('brainRepos');
    // The point of the test: a bad manifest must not restore garbage over a
    // stack that is merely half-upgraded.
    expect(calls).not.toContain('restore');
    expect(calls).not.toContain('quiesce');
  });

  test('an intact manifest restores, puts the pin back and verifies, in that order', async () => {
    const { ctx, calls } = await seedRun(null);
    const report = await rollback(ctx, 'run-1', fakeDeps(calls));
    expect(report.ok).toBe(true);
    expect(report.pin).toBe('0.50.0.0');
    expect(await Bun.file(ctx.envPath).text()).toContain('GBRAIN_VERSION=0.50.0.0');
    expect(calls).toEqual(['quiesce', 'restore', 'recreate', 'resume', 'verify-pin', 'digest']);
  });

  test('a missing artifact file is a verification failure, not a crash', async () => {
    const { ctx, calls } = await seedRun(null);
    await rm(join(dir, 'run-1', 'gbrain-home.tar'));
    await expect(rollback(ctx, 'run-1', fakeDeps(calls))).rejects.toThrow('MANIFEST_VERIFY_FAILED');
    expect(calls).not.toContain('restore');
  });

  test('a missing manifest refuses with the run id, not an ENOENT stack', async () => {
    const { ctx, calls } = await seedRun(null);
    await rm(join(dir, 'run-1', 'manifest.json'));
    await expect(rollback(ctx, 'run-1', fakeDeps(calls)))
      .rejects.toThrow('no snapshot manifest for run run-1');
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

`src/upgrade/digest.ts`:
```ts
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { OPERATIONS } from '../contracts/registry.ts';
import { verifyManifest } from './manifest.ts';
import { dockerVolumeDriver } from './snapshot.ts';
import { quiesce as realQuiesce, resume as realResume } from './quiesce.ts';
import type { DoctorBaseline, DoctorCheck, SnapshotManifest, UpgradeContext } from './types.ts';

export type DigestPart = { name: string; sha256: string; items: number; scope: string };

export type StateDigest = {
  takenAt: string;
  gbrainVersion: string;
  /** Key NAMES only (S3c). Kept unhashed so a run can name what post-upgrade added. */
  configKeys: string[];
  parts: DigestPart[];
};

/**
 * Tables the digest does NOT count. They record the upgrade itself: rollback
 * restores the control DB from the snapshot and the reconciler replays the run
 * from the journal (D2), so counting them would assert that a successful
 * rollback left no trace of the attempt. The list is hashed into the part, so
 * widening it changes the digest instead of quietly excusing a difference.
 */
const UNCOUNTED_TABLES = [
  'audit_log', 'capability_snapshots', 'playbook_ledger',
  'upgrade_checklist_items', 'upgrade_events', 'upgrade_runs',
] as const;

/** Exact counts per public table. n_live_tup is an estimate and will not do. */
const ROW_COUNT_SQL =
  "select table_name || '|' || (xpath('/row/c/text()', x))[1]::text as row from (" +
  "select table_name, query_to_xml(format('select count(*) as c from %I.%I', table_schema," +
  ' table_name), false, true, \'\') as x from information_schema.tables' +
  " where table_schema = 'public' and table_type = 'BASE TABLE') t order by table_name";

function hashPart(name: string, scope: string, lines: string[]): DigestPart {
  const body = [name, scope, ...[...lines].sort()].join('\n');
  return { name, scope, items: lines.length, sha256: createHash('sha256').update(body).digest('hex') };
}

export type DigestDeps = {
  exec(ctx: UpgradeContext, service: string, argv: string[]): Promise<{ exitCode: number; stdout: string }>;
};

async function defaultExec(ctx: UpgradeContext, service: string, argv: string[]) {
  const p = Bun.spawn([...ctx.compose, 'exec', '-T', service, ...argv], { stdout: 'pipe', stderr: 'pipe' });
  const [stdout, exitCode] = await Promise.all([new Response(p.stdout).text(), p.exited]);
  return { exitCode, stdout };
}

/** Sorted dotted key paths of the file-plane config. Names only, never values. */
const CONFIG_KEYS_PROGRAM = `
const home = process.env.GBRAIN_HOME ?? '/srv/gbrain-home';
let keys = [];
try {
  const raw = await Bun.file(home + '/.gbrain/config.json').text();
  const flat = (o, p) => Object.entries(o).flatMap(([k, v]) =>
    v && typeof v === 'object' && !Array.isArray(v) ? flat(v, p + k + '.') : [p + k]);
  keys = flat(JSON.parse(raw), '').sort();
} catch {}
console.log(JSON.stringify(keys));
`;

/** Tree hash + working-tree cleanliness per Space repo. */
const REPO_TREES_PROGRAM = `
const { readdirSync } = require('node:fs');
const root = process.env.BRAIN_REPOS ?? '/srv/brain-repos';
const out = [];
for (const e of readdirSync(root, { withFileTypes: true })) {
  if (!e.isDirectory()) continue;
  const git = (args) => {
    const p = Bun.spawnSync(['git', '-C', root + '/' + e.name, ...args]);
    return new TextDecoder().decode(p.stdout).trim();
  };
  out.push(e.name + ':' + git(['rev-parse', 'HEAD^{tree}']) +
    ':' + git(['status', '--porcelain']).split('\\n').filter(Boolean).length);
}
console.log(JSON.stringify(out.sort()));
`;

/**
 * The definition of "restores the pre-upgrade state exactly" that the exit gate
 * asserts. Four parts plus the pin, every one of them computed through a
 * published contract or our own file plane (G3).
 */
export async function stateDigest(
  ctx: UpgradeContext, deps: DigestDeps = { exec: defaultExec },
): Promise<StateDigest> {
  const pin = (await readFile(ctx.envPath, 'utf8')).match(/^GBRAIN_VERSION=(.*)$/m)?.[1]?.trim() ?? '';

  // 1. The brain's source set, via the registry's own argv. NOT a search: a
  //    non-empty --federated-read is the FULL scope (L13) and an unqualified
  //    read only sees sources whose config.federated is literally true (L6), so
  //    a search-based set can shrink silently and still look equal.
  const statusArgv = OPERATIONS['sources.status']!.argv;
  const sourcesRaw = await deps.exec(ctx, 'gbrain-ops', ['gbrain', ...statusArgv, '--json']);
  const parsed: unknown = JSON.parse(sourcesRaw.stdout.trim().slice(
    Math.max(sourcesRaw.stdout.trim().indexOf('{'), sourcesRaw.stdout.trim().indexOf('[')),
  ));
  const rows: Record<string, unknown>[] = Array.isArray(parsed)
    ? parsed as Record<string, unknown>[]
    : Object.values(parsed as Record<string, unknown>).find(Array.isArray) as Record<string, unknown>[] ?? [];
  // Shape-tolerant projection: identity plus any count-like number, timestamps
  // dropped. No contract test pins this JSON's exact field list, so the digest
  // must not depend on one it invented.
  const sources = rows.map((r) => {
    const id = String(r.id ?? r.source_id ?? r.name ?? '');
    const counts = Object.entries(r)
      .filter(([k, v]) => typeof v === 'number' && /count|documents|pages|chunks/i.test(k))
      .map(([k, v]) => `${k}=${v}`).sort();
    return [id, ...counts].join('|');
  });

  // 2. Git tree hashes — where page CONTENT identity actually lives.
  const reposRaw = await deps.exec(ctx, 'gbrain-ops', ['bun', '-e', REPO_TREES_PROGRAM]);
  const repos = JSON.parse(reposRaw.stdout.trim()) as string[];

  // 3. File-plane config KEY NAMES (S3c). Values never leave the container.
  const keysRaw = await deps.exec(ctx, 'gbrain-ops', ['bun', '-e', CONFIG_KEYS_PROGRAM]);
  const configKeys = JSON.parse(keysRaw.stdout.trim()) as string[];

  // 4. Exact control-DB row counts, minus this system's own bookkeeping.
  const countsRaw = await deps.exec(ctx, 'postgres',
    ['psql', '-U', 'gbrain', '-d', 'control', '-tAc', ROW_COUNT_SQL]);
  const controlRows = countsRaw.stdout.split('\n').map((l) => l.trim()).filter(Boolean)
    .filter((l) => !(UNCOUNTED_TABLES as readonly string[]).includes(l.split('|')[0]!));

  return {
    takenAt: new Date().toISOString(),
    gbrainVersion: pin,
    configKeys,
    parts: [
      hashPart('pin', '.env GBRAIN_VERSION', [pin]),
      hashPart('sources', 'gbrain-ops local admin; sources status --json enumeration; ' +
        'no federated-read qualifier applies to this scope', sources),
      hashPart('repos', 'every directory under BRAIN_REPOS; HEAD^{tree} plus dirty-file count', repos),
      hashPart('configKeys', 'GBRAIN_HOME/.gbrain/config.json; dotted key names only', configKeys),
      hashPart('controlRows', `control public tables, excluding ${UNCOUNTED_TABLES.join(',')}`, controlRows),
    ],
  };
}

export type DigestComparison = {
  equal: boolean;
  differing: { part: string; a: string; b: string; aItems: number; bItems: number }[];
  onlyInA: string[];
  onlyInB: string[];
};

/** Per-part differences, never a bare boolean: a failure has to say WHAT moved. */
export function compareDigests(a: StateDigest, b: StateDigest): DigestComparison {
  const byName = (d: StateDigest) => new Map(d.parts.map((p) => [p.name, p]));
  const [ma, mb] = [byName(a), byName(b)];
  const differing: DigestComparison['differing'] = [];
  for (const [name, pa] of ma) {
    const pb = mb.get(name);
    if (!pb || pa.sha256 === pb.sha256) continue;
    differing.push({ part: name, a: pa.sha256, b: pb.sha256, aItems: pa.items, bItems: pb.items });
  }
  const onlyInA = [...ma.keys()].filter((n) => !mb.has(n)).sort();
  const onlyInB = [...mb.keys()].filter((n) => !ma.has(n)).sort();
  return {
    equal: differing.length === 0 && onlyInA.length === 0 && onlyInB.length === 0,
    differing: differing.sort((x, y) => x.part.localeCompare(y.part)),
    onlyInA, onlyInB,
  };
}

export type DoctorParity = {
  regressed: DoctorCheck[];
  newChecks: DoctorCheck[];
  resolved: DoctorCheck[];
  schemaVersionOk: boolean;
  status: string;
  healthScore: number;
};

/**
 * D5: per-check parity, not the exit code (`warnings` exits 0, U7), not
 * `blockers` (it does not exist, L4), not `health_score` (it moves when
 * upstream adds a check).
 */
export function compareDoctor(baseline: DoctorBaseline, after: DoctorBaseline): DoctorParity {
  const before = new Map(baseline.checks.map((c) => [c.name, c]));
  const now = new Map(after.checks.map((c) => [c.name, c]));
  const schema = now.get('schema_version');
  return {
    regressed: after.checks.filter((c) => before.get(c.name)?.status === 'ok' && c.status !== 'ok'),
    newChecks: after.checks.filter((c) => !before.has(c.name)),
    resolved: after.checks.filter((c) => {
      const was = before.get(c.name);
      return was !== undefined && was.status !== 'ok' && c.status === 'ok';
    }),
    // A check that is not there did not pass (honesty rule 1).
    schemaVersionOk: schema?.status === 'ok',
    status: after.status,
    healthScore: after.healthScore,
  };
}

export class RollbackRefusal extends Error {
  constructor(readonly code: 'NO_MANIFEST' | 'MANIFEST_VERIFY_FAILED', message: string) {
    super(`${code}: ${message}`);
    this.name = 'RollbackRefusal';
  }
}

export type RollbackReport = {
  runId: string; pin: string; restored: string[];
  digest: StateDigest | null; pinVerified: boolean; ok: boolean;
};

export type RollbackDeps = {
  driver: typeof dockerVolumeDriver;
  quiesce: typeof realQuiesce;
  resume: typeof realResume;
  recreate(ctx: UpgradeContext, services: readonly string[]): Promise<void>;
  verifyRuntimePin(ctx: UpgradeContext, version: string): Promise<boolean>;
  stateDigest(ctx: UpgradeContext): Promise<StateDigest>;
};

/**
 * One operation (D4, honesty rule 5). Reads the journal and manifest from DISK,
 * because the case this exists for is the control plane never coming back.
 *
 * Verification comes FIRST and nothing is touched until it passes: restoring an
 * artifact whose checksum does not match would overwrite a half-upgraded stack
 * with garbage, which is strictly worse than the state it was called to fix.
 */
export async function rollback(
  ctx: UpgradeContext, runId: string, deps: Partial<RollbackDeps> = {},
): Promise<RollbackReport> {
  const d: RollbackDeps = {
    driver: dockerVolumeDriver,
    quiesce: realQuiesce,
    resume: realResume,
    recreate: async () => { throw new Error('recreate dependency is required'); },
    verifyRuntimePin: async () => true,
    stateDigest,
    ...deps,
  } as RollbackDeps;

  let manifest: SnapshotManifest;
  try {
    manifest = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(ctx.upgradeDir, 'manifest.json'), 'utf8')) as SnapshotManifest;
  } catch {
    throw new RollbackRefusal('NO_MANIFEST', `no snapshot manifest for run ${runId}`);
  }

  const verified = await verifyManifest(manifest, ctx.upgradeDir);
  if (!verified.ok) {
    throw new RollbackRefusal(
      'MANIFEST_VERIFY_FAILED',
      `${verified.mismatches.join(', ')} — refusing to restore unverified artifacts over the stack`,
    );
  }

  await d.quiesce(ctx);
  await d.driver.restore(ctx, manifest);

  // The pin moves back before the images are recreated, so compose reads the
  // old value; D8 removed the ${GBRAIN_VERSION:-0.50.0.0} fallbacks precisely so
  // a missed --env-file fails loudly instead of silently rebuilding the new tag.
  const { writePin } = await import('../../scripts/upgrade.ts');
  await writePin(ctx.envPath, manifest.fromVersion);
  await d.recreate(ctx, ['gbrain-ops', 'gbrain-serve', 'gbrain-worker', 'control-plane']);
  await d.resume(ctx);

  const pinVerified = await d.verifyRuntimePin(ctx, manifest.fromVersion);
  const digest = await d.stateDigest(ctx);
  return {
    runId, pin: manifest.fromVersion,
    restored: manifest.artifacts.map((a) => a.name),
    digest, pinVerified, ok: pinVerified,
  };
}
```

`scripts/upgrade.ts` — the `rollback` subcommand already dispatches to this function (Task 11's
`import.meta.main` block calls `rollback(ctx, args.runId)`); the only change is supplying the real
deps and comparing against the stored pre-upgrade digest:

```ts
    if (args.cmd === 'rollback') {
      const ctx = await buildContext(
        { to: process.env.GBRAIN_VERSION ?? '0.50.0.0', dryRun: false }, args.runId,
      );
      const r = await rollback(ctx, args.runId, {
        recreate: (c, services) => defaultRecreate(c, services),
      });
      // The exit gate's definition of "restores the pre-upgrade state exactly".
      const beforePath = join(ctx.upgradeDir, 'digest-before.json');
      const before = JSON.parse(await readFile(beforePath, 'utf8')) as StateDigest;
      const parity = compareDigests(before, r.digest!);
      console.log(JSON.stringify({ ...r, parity }, null, 2));
      if (!parity.equal) {
        console.error(`[rollback] state differs after restore: ${parity.differing
          .map((p) => p.part).concat(parity.onlyInA, parity.onlyInB).join(', ')}`);
      }
      process.exit(r.ok && parity.equal ? 0 : 4);
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run the tests to verify they pass**

Run: `bun test test/unit/upgrade-digest.test.ts`
Expected: PASS, 11 tests.

Run: `bun test test/integration/upgrade-rollback.test.ts`
Expected: PASS, 4 tests.

The assertion that matters is `a tampered artifact aborts the rollback before anything is restored`,
and specifically `expect(calls).not.toContain('restore')`. A rollback called on a half-upgraded stack
is already the bad day; restoring a corrupt dump over it is the worse one, and it is unrecoverable
because the thing that would have fixed it is the artifact that just failed its checksum. If this
goes red, do **not** downgrade the verification to a warning or add a `--force` that skips it — fix
the snapshot path, and file the corruption in `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` with the mismatching
digests.

## Task 13: The reconciler and the admin API

**Files:**
- Create: `src/upgrade/reconcile.ts`, `src/control/routes/upgrades.ts`
- Modify: `src/control/app.ts`, `compose/docker-compose.yml`
- Test: `test/integration/upgrades.test.ts`, `test/integration/compose-invariants.test.ts` (extend)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `UpgradeEvent`, `UpgradeRunState` (`src/upgrade/types.ts`, Task 2);
  `canTransition(from, to): boolean` (`src/upgrade/state.ts`, Task 10);
  `recordPlaybook(db, gbrainVersion, state, notes, decidedBy?): Promise<void>` and
  `openChecklist(db, runId, items): Promise<void>` and
  `ackChecklistItem(db, itemId, decidedBy, decision): Promise<ChecklistItemRow>` (`src/upgrade/ledger.ts`, Task 9);
  `detectUpdate(): Promise<CheckUpdateResult>` (`src/upgrade/detect.ts`, Task 7);
  `driftBetween(from, to): Promise<DriftResult>` (`src/upgrade/capability.ts`, Task 8);
  `playbooksFor(from, to)` (`src/upgrade/playbooks/index.ts`, Task 9);
  `schema.upgradeRuns`, `schema.upgradeEvents`, `schema.upgradeChecklistItems`, `schema.playbookLedger`,
  `schema.auditLog` (Task 3); `getDb`, `requireRole` (existing).
- Produces: `reconcileJournals(db, dir): Promise<ReconcileSummary>`, `UPGRADE_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_MS`;
  the `upgrades` router mounted at `/api`; `UPGRADE_HOST_ONLY_ERROR`.

> **D2 and S2 apply here, together.** The control plane is stopped for most of an upgrade (D3 stops
> `control-plane` third), so it cannot be the run's system of record while it is down —
> `scripts/upgrade.ts` journals to `/srv/upgrades/<runId>/journal.ndjson` and this reconciler is the
> only thing that ever turns that into rows. S2 says the control plane never gains a Docker socket or
> CLI, and `compose/docker-compose.yml:99-117` confirms today it mounts nothing at `/srv/upgrades` at
> all — the reconciler is unreachable in production without the read-only bind mount this task adds,
> mirroring the existing `gbrain-home:/srv/gbrain-home:ro` line exactly. Honesty rule 1 (U1) is
> enforced one level up, in `/api/upgrades/available`: `check-update` never sets an exit code, so a
> network failure is visible only through `.error`, and this route must not be the place that quietly
> drops it.

async function addUser(email: string, role: 'owner' | 'admin' | 'member' | 'viewer'): Promise<void> {
  const res = await app.request('/api/auth/sign-up/email', {

  const [user] = await getDb().select().from(schema.users).where(eq(schema.users.email, email));
  createdUsers.push(user!.id);
  await getDb().insert(schema.memberships).values({ id: randomUUID(), userId: user!.id, organizationId: ORG, role });
}

async function signIn(email: string): Promise<string> {
  const res = await app.request('/api/auth/sign-in/email', {
    method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify({ email, password: PASS }),
  });
  expect(res.status).toBe(200);
  return res.headers.get('set-cookie') ?? '';
}

async function hit(method: string, path: string, cookie?: string, body?: unknown) {
  const headers: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (cookie) headers.cookie = cookie;
  const hasBody = method !== 'GET' && method !== 'DELETE';
  if (hasBody || body !== undefined) headers['content-type'] = 'application/json';
  return app.request(path, { method, headers, body: hasBody ? JSON.stringify(body ?? {}) : undefined });
}

function freshRunId(): string {
  const id = randomUUID();
  createdRunIds.push(id);
  return id;
}

async function writeLine(runId: string, seq: number, type: string, data: unknown, at = new Date().toISOString()) {
  await mkdir(join(upgradeDir, runId), { recursive: true });
  await writeFile(
    join(upgradeDir, runId, 'journal.ndjson'),
    JSON.stringify({ runId, seq, at, type, data }) + '\n',
    { flag: 'a' },
  );
}

async function writeRaw(runId: string, text: string) {
  await mkdir(join(upgradeDir, runId), { recursive: true });
  await writeFile(join(upgradeDir, runId, 'journal.ndjson'), text, { flag: 'a' });
}

async function insertRun(over: Record<string, unknown> = {}): Promise<string> {
  const id = freshRunId();
  await getDb().insert(schema.upgradeRuns).values({
    id, fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', state: 'completed',
    startedAt: new Date(), finishedAt: new Date(), createdBy: 'test', ...over,
  });
  return id;
}

beforeAll(async () => {
  await runMigrations();
  upgradeDir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'bs-upgrades-'));
  await getDb().insert(schema.organizations).values({ id: ORG, name: 'Upgrades', slug: `upgrades-${RUN}` });
  await addUser(`up-admin-${RUN}@example.test`, 'admin');
  await addUser(`up-member-${RUN}@example.test`, 'member');
  await addUser(`up-viewer-${RUN}@example.test`, 'viewer');
  adminCookie = await signIn(`up-admin-${RUN}@example.test`);
  memberCookie = await signIn(`up-member-${RUN}@example.test`);
  viewerCookie = await signIn(`up-viewer-${RUN}@example.test`);
}, 90_000);

afterAll(async () => {
  if (createdRunIds.length > 0) {
    await getDb().delete(schema.upgradeEvents).where(inArray(schema.upgradeEvents.runId, createdRunIds));
    await getDb().delete(schema.upgradeChecklistItems).where(inArray(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.runId, createdRunIds));
    await getDb().delete(schema.upgradeRuns).where(inArray(schema.upgradeRuns.id, createdRunIds));
  }
  await getDb().delete(schema.playbookLedger).where(eq(schema.playbookLedger.gbrainVersion, '9.9.9-fixture'));
  await getDb().delete(schema.organizations).where(eq(schema.organizations.id, ORG));
  await getDb().delete(schema.users).where(inArray(schema.users.id, createdUsers));
  await closeDb();
});

describe('reconcileJournals', () => {
  test('a fresh journal imports the run and its events', async () => {
    const runId = freshRunId();
    await writeLine(runId, 1, 'run_started', { fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', createdBy: 'operator' });
    await writeLine(runId, 2, 'state_changed', { to: 'snapshotting' });

    const summary = await reconcileJournals(getDb(), upgradeDir);
    expect(summary.runsScanned).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(summary.eventsImported).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);

    const [run] = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeRuns).where(eq(schema.upgradeRuns.id, runId));
    expect(run!.state).toBe('snapshotting');
    expect(run!.fromVersion).toBe('0.50.0.0');

    const events = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeEvents).where(eq(schema.upgradeEvents.runId, runId));
    expect(events).toHaveLength(2);
  });

  test('re-importing the same journal is a no-op — UNIQUE (runId, seq) is the idempotency key', async () => {
    const runId = freshRunId();
    await writeLine(runId, 1, 'run_started', { fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', createdBy: 'operator' });
    await reconcileJournals(getDb(), upgradeDir);
    const before = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeEvents).where(eq(schema.upgradeEvents.runId, runId));
    await reconcileJournals(getDb(), upgradeDir);
    const after = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeEvents).where(eq(schema.upgradeEvents.runId, runId));
    expect(after).toHaveLength(before.length);
    expect(after).toHaveLength(1);
  });

  test('a truncated tail line imports everything complete and reports the truncation', async () => {
    const runId = freshRunId();
    const complete = [
      JSON.stringify({ runId, seq: 1, at: new Date().toISOString(), type: 'run_started', data: { fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', createdBy: 'operator' } }),
      JSON.stringify({ runId, seq: 2, at: new Date().toISOString(), type: 'state_changed', data: { to: 'snapshotting' } }),
    ].join('\n') + '\n';
    // A real partial write: cut off mid-object, no trailing newline — exactly
    // what a reconcile pass can see if it reads while scripts/upgrade.ts is
    // mid-append.
    const partial = `{"runId":"${runId}","seq":3,"at":"2026-0`;
    await writeRaw(runId, complete + partial);

    const summary = await reconcileJournals(getDb(), upgradeDir);
    expect(summary.eventsImported).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);
    expect(summary.truncatedRuns).toContain(runId);

    const events = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeEvents).where(eq(schema.upgradeEvents.runId, runId));
    expect(events).toHaveLength(2);
  });

  test('the DB never goes backwards — a stale state_changed event is logged but not applied', async () => {
    const runId = freshRunId();
    await writeLine(runId, 1, 'run_started', { fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', createdBy: 'operator' });
    await writeLine(runId, 2, 'state_changed', { to: 'migrating' });
    await writeLine(runId, 3, 'state_changed', { to: 'quiescing' }); // deliberately regressive

    const summary = await reconcileJournals(getDb(), upgradeDir);
    expect(summary.eventsImported).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(3);

    const [run] = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeRuns).where(eq(schema.upgradeRuns.id, runId));
    expect(run!.state).toBe('migrating'); // the derived state never regresses...

    const events = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeEvents).where(eq(schema.upgradeEvents.runId, runId));
    expect(events.map((e) => e.type)).toEqual(['run_started', 'state_changed', 'state_changed']); // ...but the log stays complete
  });

  test('a playbooks_applied event replays into the ledger and opens the checklist', async () => {
    const runId = freshRunId();
    await writeLine(runId, 1, 'run_started', { fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', createdBy: 'operator' });
    await writeLine(runId, 2, 'playbooks_applied', {
      ledger: [{ gbrainVersion: '9.9.9-fixture', state: 'applied', notes: 'upstream ships no playbook for this version — CHANGELOG confirmed' }],
      checklist: [{ itemKey: 'confirm-thing', gbrainVersion: '9.9.9-fixture', prompt: 'Confirm the thing', why: 'because' }],
    });

    await reconcileJournals(getDb(), upgradeDir);

    const [ledgerRow] = await getDb().select().from(schema.playbookLedger)
      .where(eq(schema.playbookLedger.gbrainVersion, '9.9.9-fixture'));
    expect(ledgerRow!.state).toBe('applied');
    expect(ledgerRow!.notes).toContain('no playbook');

    const checklist = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeChecklistItems).where(eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.runId, runId));
    expect(checklist).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(checklist[0]!.itemKey).toBe('confirm-thing');
    expect(checklist[0]!.state).toBe('awaiting_admin_decision');
  });

  test('a missing upgrade directory is a clean zero, not a crash', async () => {
    const summary = await reconcileJournals(getDb(), join(upgradeDir, 'does-not-exist'));
    expect(summary).toEqual({ runsScanned: 0, eventsImported: 0, eventsSkipped: 0, truncatedRuns: [] });
  });

  test('the timer interval is a stated number, not ad hoc', () => {
    expect(UPGRADE_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_MS).toBe(60_000);
  });
});

describe('upgrade routes', () => {
  test('an unauthenticated caller gets 401 on every upgrade route', async () => {
    const table: Array<[string, string]> = [
      ['GET', '/api/upgrades'],
      ['GET', '/api/upgrades/available'],
      ['GET', '/api/upgrades/drift?from=0.50.0.0&to=0.50.5.0'],
      ['GET', `/api/upgrades/${NOPE}`],
      ['POST', '/api/upgrades'],
      ['POST', `/api/upgrades/checklist/${NOPE}/ack`],
      ['POST', `/api/upgrades/checklist/${NOPE}/decline`],
    ];
    for (const [method, path] of table) expect((await hit(method, path)).status).toBe(401);
  });

  test('POST /api/upgrades is 503, unconditionally — starting an upgrade is a host operation', async () => {
    const res = await hit('POST', '/api/upgrades', adminCookie);
    expect(res.status).toBe(503);
    const body = await res.json() as { error: string };
    expect(body.error).toBe(UPGRADE_HOST_ONLY_ERROR);
    expect(body.error).toMatch(/scripts\/upgrade\.ts/);
    expect(body.error).toMatch(/host/i);
  });

  test('a viewer reads every GET route, and a member is refused the same as a viewer for POST', async () => {
    expect((await hit('GET', '/api/upgrades', viewerCookie)).status).toBe(200);
    expect((await hit('GET', '/api/upgrades/available', viewerCookie)).status).toBe(200);
    expect((await hit('GET', '/api/upgrades/drift?from=0.50.0.0&to=0.50.5.0', viewerCookie)).status).toBe(200);
    expect((await hit('POST', '/api/upgrades', viewerCookie)).status).toBe(403);
    expect((await hit('POST', '/api/upgrades', memberCookie)).status).toBe(403);
  });

  test('a failed check-update renders as "could not check" — the .error survives, never dropped', async () => {
    detectHandler = async () => ({
      current_version: '0.50.0.0', latest_version: '', update_available: false,
      changelog_diff: '', release_url: '', error: 'network_error',
    });
    const res = await hit('GET', '/api/upgrades/available', viewerCookie);
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
    const body = await res.json() as DetectResult;
    expect(body.error).toBe('network_error');
    expect(body.update_available).toBe(false);
  });

  test('drift with only one recorded snapshot passes single_snapshot through, not an empty diff', async () => {
    driftHandler = async (from, to) => ({ signal: 'single_snapshot', from, to, diff: null });
    const res = await hit('GET', '/api/upgrades/drift?from=0.50.0.0&to=0.50.5.0', viewerCookie);
    const body = await res.json() as DriftResult;
    expect(body.signal).toBe('single_snapshot');
    expect(body.signal).not.toBe('no_drift');
    expect(body.diff).toBeNull();
  });

  test('drift refuses without both versions named', async () => {
    expect((await hit('GET', '/api/upgrades/drift?from=0.50.0.0', viewerCookie)).status).toBe(400);
  });

  test('GET /api/upgrades lists run history', async () => {
    await insertRun();
    const res = await hit('GET', '/api/upgrades', viewerCookie);
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
    const body = await res.json() as { runs: unknown[] };
    expect(Array.isArray(body.runs)).toBe(true);
    expect(body.runs.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
  });

  test('GET /api/upgrades/:id assembles the run, its events, checklist and the ledger rows it touched', async () => {
    const runId = await insertRun();
    await getDb().insert(schema.upgradeEvents).values([
      { id: randomUUID(), runId, seq: 1, at: new Date(), type: 'run_started', data: {} },
      { id: randomUUID(), runId, seq: 2, at: new Date(), type: 'state_changed', data: { to: 'completed' } },
    ]);
    await getDb().insert(schema.upgradeChecklistItems).values({
      id: randomUUID(), runId, gbrainVersion: '9.9.9-fixture', itemKey: 'confirm-thing',
      prompt: 'Confirm the thing', why: 'because', state: 'awaiting_admin_decision',
    });
    await getDb().insert(schema.playbookLedger).values({
      id: randomUUID(), gbrainVersion: '9.9.9-fixture', state: 'applied', notes: 'test evidence',
    }).onConflictDoNothing();

    const res = await hit('GET', `/api/upgrades/${runId}`, viewerCookie);
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
    const body = await res.json() as { run: { id: string }; events: Array<{ type: string }>; checklist: unknown[]; ledger: Array<{ gbrainVersion: string }> };
    expect(body.run.id).toBe(runId);
    expect(body.events.map((e) => e.type)).toEqual(['run_started', 'state_changed']);
    expect(body.checklist).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(body.ledger.map((l) => l.gbrainVersion)).toEqual(['9.9.9-fixture']);
  });

  test('an unknown run id is a 404', async () => {
    expect((await hit('GET', `/api/upgrades/${NOPE}`, viewerCookie)).status).toBe(404);
  });

  test('acknowledging a checklist item writes the audit row before the state change, admin only', async () => {
    const runId = await insertRun();
    const itemId = randomUUID();
    await getDb().insert(schema.upgradeChecklistItems).values({
      id: itemId, runId, gbrainVersion: '9.9.9-fixture', itemKey: 'confirm-thing',
      prompt: 'Confirm the thing', why: 'because', state: 'awaiting_admin_decision',
    });

    expect((await hit('POST', `/api/upgrades/checklist/${itemId}/ack`, viewerCookie)).status).toBe(403);
    expect((await hit('POST', `/api/upgrades/checklist/${itemId}/ack`, memberCookie)).status).toBe(403);

    const res = await hit('POST', `/api/upgrades/checklist/${itemId}/ack`, adminCookie);
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
    const body = await res.json() as { item: { state: string } };
    expect(body.item.state).toBe('acknowledged');

    const [row] = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeChecklistItems).where(eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.id, itemId));
    expect(row!.state).toBe('acknowledged');
    expect(row!.decidedBy).toBeTruthy();
    expect(row!.decidedAt).toBeTruthy();

    // The audit row IS the durable record, written before the state change —
    // the same idiom as schedule-alert acknowledgement and connection revoke.
    const audit = await getDb().select().from(schema.auditLog)
      .where(eq(schema.auditLog.action, 'upgrade.checklist.acknowledged'));
    expect(audit.some((a) => a.target === itemId)).toBe(true);
  });

  test('declining a checklist item is the same audited path, with its own action name', async () => {
    const runId = await insertRun();
    const itemId = randomUUID();
    await getDb().insert(schema.upgradeChecklistItems).values({
      id: itemId, runId, gbrainVersion: '9.9.9-fixture', itemKey: 'confirm-other',
      prompt: 'p', why: 'w', state: 'awaiting_admin_decision',
    });
    const res = await hit('POST', `/api/upgrades/checklist/${itemId}/decline`, adminCookie);
    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
    const [row] = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeChecklistItems).where(eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.id, itemId));
    expect(row!.state).toBe('declined');
    const audit = await getDb().select().from(schema.auditLog)
      .where(eq(schema.auditLog.action, 'upgrade.checklist.declined'));
    expect(audit.some((a) => a.target === itemId)).toBe(true);
  });

  test('an unknown checklist item is a 404, never a 403 leak', async () => {
    expect((await hit('POST', `/api/upgrades/checklist/${NOPE}/ack`, adminCookie)).status).toBe(404);
  });
});

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

`src/upgrade/reconcile.ts`:
```ts
/**
 * Journal → control DB. The control plane is stopped for most of an upgrade
 * (D3's quiesce order stops it third), so it cannot be the run's system of
 * record while it is down. scripts/upgrade.ts — a HOST process (D1) that
 * holds no control-DB connection at all — journals every transition to
 * `<UPGRADE_DIR>/<runId>/journal.ndjson`, one JSON event per line, seq
 * monotonic per run. This file replays that journal into upgrade_runs /
 * upgrade_events / playbook_ledger / upgrade_checklist_items, on boot and on
 * a timer (src/control/app.ts), idempotent on the UNIQUE (runId, seq) Task 3
 * put on upgrade_events.
 */
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import { readdir, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { eq } from 'drizzle-orm';
import { getDb, schema } from '../control/db/client.ts';
import type { UpgradeEvent, UpgradeRunState } from './types.ts';
import { canTransition } from './state.ts';
import { recordPlaybook, openChecklist } from './ledger.ts';

type Db = ReturnType<typeof getDb>;

/**
 * A boot reconcile catches everything that happened while the control plane
 * was down — most of any upgrade. The timer catches what lands while it is
 * back up: a rollback run (`scripts/upgrade.ts rollback`) can happen with the
 * control plane already serving traffic, and a checklist item opened mid-run
 * should not wait for the next restart. 60s is frequent enough that an admin
 * sees a rollback's checklist inside a minute; upgrade_events is a handful of
 * rows per run, so polling that often costs nothing worth measuring.
 */
export const UPGRADE_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_MS = 60_000;

export type ReconcileSummary = {
  runsScanned: number;
  eventsImported: number;
  eventsSkipped: number;
  truncatedRuns: string[];
};

/**
 * Read one run's journal, tolerating a partial trailing write. NDJSON
 * written by a live host process can be read mid-append: every COMPLETE
 * line parses and imports; an incomplete tail is dropped and reported, never
 * thrown — the next pass (this timer, or the next boot) picks it up once the
 * writer finishes the line. A corrupt line that is NOT the last one is a
 * real bug, not a race, so it still throws.
 */
async function readRunJournal(dir: string, runId: string): Promise<{ events: UpgradeEvent[]; truncated: boolean }> {
  const path = join(dir, runId, 'journal.ndjson');
  let raw: string;
  try {
    raw = await readFile(path, 'utf8');
  } catch (e) {
    if ((e as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === 'ENOENT') return { events: [], truncated: false };
    throw e;
  }
  const lines = raw.split('\n').filter((l) => l.length > 0);
  const events: UpgradeEvent[] = [];
  let truncated = false;
  lines.forEach((line, i) => {
    try {
      events.push(JSON.parse(line) as UpgradeEvent);
    } catch {
      if (i === lines.length - 1) truncated = true;
      else throw new Error(`upgrade journal ${runId} is corrupt at a non-trailing line`);
    }
  });
  events.sort((a, b) => a.seq - b.seq);
  return { events, truncated };
}

export async function reconcileJournals(db: Db, dir: string): Promise<ReconcileSummary> {
  const summary: ReconcileSummary = { runsScanned: 0, eventsImported: 0, eventsSkipped: 0, truncatedRuns: [] };

  let entries;
  try {
    entries = await readdir(dir, { withFileTypes: true });
  } catch (e) {
    if ((e as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === 'ENOENT') return summary; // no upgrade has ever run
    throw e;
  }

  for (const entry of entries) {
    if (!entry.isDirectory()) continue;
    const runId = entry.name;
    summary.runsScanned += 1;
    const { events, truncated } = await readRunJournal(dir, runId);
    if (truncated) summary.truncatedRuns.push(runId);
    for (const event of events) {
      if (await importEvent(db, event)) summary.eventsImported += 1;
      else summary.eventsSkipped += 1;
    }
  }
  return summary;
}

async function importEvent(db: Db, event: UpgradeEvent): Promise<boolean> {
  // UNIQUE (runId, seq) — Task 3 — IS the idempotency mechanism: a re-import
  // of an already-seen event inserts nothing, and its side effect (below)
  // never runs a second time.
  const inserted = await db.insert(schema.upgradeEvents)
    .values({
      id: randomUUID(), runId: event.runId, seq: event.seq,
      at: new Date(event.at), type: event.type, data: event.data as object,
    })
    .onConflictDoNothing({ target: [schema.upgradeEvents.runId, schema.upgradeEvents.seq] })
    .returning({ id: schema.upgradeEvents.id });
  if (inserted.length === 0) return false;

  await applyEffects(db, event);
  return true;
}

async function applyEffects(db: Db, event: UpgradeEvent): Promise<void> {
  switch (event.type) {
    case 'run_started': {
      const data = event.data as { fromVersion: string; toVersion: string; createdBy: string };
      await db.insert(schema.upgradeRuns).values({
        id: event.runId, fromVersion: data.fromVersion, toVersion: data.toVersion,
        state: 'planned', startedAt: new Date(event.at), createdBy: data.createdBy,
      }).onConflictDoNothing({ target: schema.upgradeRuns.id });
      return;
    }
    case 'state_changed': {
      const data = event.data as { to: UpgradeRunState; snapshotId?: string };
      await advanceRunState(db, event.runId, data.to, data.snapshotId);
      return;
    }
    case 'playbooks_applied': {
      // D6/D2: the host CLI holds no control-DB connection, so it cannot
      // call Task 9's ledger functions itself — it journals the payload and
      // THIS is where they finally run, exactly once, against the real DB.
      const data = event.data as {
        ledger?: Array<{ gbrainVersion: string; state: 'pending' | 'applied' | 'skipped' | 'awaiting_admin_decision'; notes: string; decidedBy?: string }>;
        checklist?: Array<{ itemKey: string; gbrainVersion: string; prompt: string; why: string }>;
      };
      for (const row of data.ledger ?? []) {
        await recordPlaybook(db, row.gbrainVersion, row.state, row.notes, row.decidedBy);
      }
      if (data.checklist && data.checklist.length > 0) {
        await openChecklist(db, event.runId, data.checklist);
      }
      return;
    }
    default:
      // Every other event type (quiesce_verified, step_started, step_failed,
      // doctor_compared, ...) is recorded in upgrade_events for the console's
      // timeline and needs no side effect of its own.
      return;
  }
}

/**
 * Never regress. A late or duplicated `state_changed` event naming an
 * earlier state than the run already holds is recorded in upgrade_events —
 * the append-only timeline stays complete and honest — but does not move
 * `upgrade_runs.state` backwards. This is the D2 guarantee the boot
 * reconcile and the timer both share.
 */
async function advanceRunState(db: Db, runId: string, to: UpgradeRunState, snapshotId?: string): Promise<void> {
  const [run] = await db.select({ state: schema.upgradeRuns.state })
    .from(schema.upgradeRuns).where(eq(schema.upgradeRuns.id, runId));
  if (!run) return; // run_started has not landed yet — a later pass catches up
  if (!canTransition(run.state as UpgradeRunState, to)) return;

  const patch: Record<string, unknown> = { state: to };
  if (snapshotId) patch.snapshotId = snapshotId;
  if (to === 'completed' || to === 'failed' || to === 'rolled_back') patch.finishedAt = new Date();
  await db.update(schema.upgradeRuns).set(patch).where(eq(schema.upgradeRuns.id, runId));
}
```

`src/control/routes/upgrades.ts`:
```ts
import { Hono } from 'hono';
import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';
import { desc, eq, inArray } from 'drizzle-orm';
import { getDb, schema } from '../db/client.ts';
import { requireRole, type Role } from '../rbac.ts';
import { detectUpdate } from '../../upgrade/detect.ts';
import { driftBetween } from '../../upgrade/capability.ts';
import { playbooksFor } from '../../upgrade/playbooks/index.ts';
import { ackChecklistItem } from '../../upgrade/ledger.ts';

export const upgrades = new Hono();

async function audit(actor: string, action: string, target: string, after: unknown) {
  // upgrade_runs/events/checklist/ledger are deployment-level, not org-scoped
  // (there is one gbrain binary for the whole stack) — same shape as
  // capability_snapshots and playbook_ledger already are.
  await getDb().insert(schema.auditLog)
    .values({ id: randomUUID(), organizationId: null, actor, action, target, before: null, after: after as object });
}

const guard = (min: Role, handler: (c: any, ctx: { userId: string; orgId: string; role: Role }) => Promise<Response>) =>
  async (c: any) => {
    let ctx;
    try { ctx = await requireRole(c, min); } catch (r) { return r as Response; }
    try { return await handler(c, ctx); }
    catch (e: any) { return c.json({ error: e?.message ?? 'upgrades_error' }, e?.status ?? 400); }
  };

/**
 * D1: scripts/upgrade.ts, run by a human on the Docker host, is the only
 * thing that quiesces services or swaps images — and it holds no control-DB
 * connection (D2), so this route does not even import it. Unlike backup,
 * whose route calls the SAME runBackup() the host CLI does, gated by a
 * docker-availability check, there is no conditional path here where
 * starting an upgrade from the control plane could ever work: doing so would
 * mean shelling out to `docker compose` from inside a container that has
 * neither a Docker socket nor a Docker CLI (S2). This route exists only so a
 * stray POST gets an honest 503 naming the real command, not a bare 404.
 */
export const UPGRADE_HOST_ONLY_ERROR =
  'Starting an upgrade is a host operation. Run scripts/upgrade.ts on the Docker host — '
  + 'the control-plane container cannot invoke docker compose.';

upgrades.post('/upgrades', guard('admin', async (c) => c.json({ error: UPGRADE_HOST_ONLY_ERROR }, 503)));

upgrades.get('/upgrades', guard('viewer', async (c) => {
  const runs = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeRuns).orderBy(desc(schema.upgradeRuns.startedAt));
  return c.json({ runs });
}));

upgrades.get('/upgrades/available', guard('viewer', async (c) => {
  // Honesty rule 1 / U1: check-update NEVER sets an exit code, so `.error`
  // is the only signal a network failure happened at all. Pass it through
  // untouched — collapsing a failed check into update_available:false would
  // read as "up to date" when the truth is "we do not know."
  return c.json(await detectUpdate());
}));

upgrades.get('/upgrades/drift', guard('viewer', async (c) => {
  const from = c.req.query('from');
  const to = c.req.query('to');
  if (!from || !to) return c.json({ error: 'from and to are required' }, 400);
  // Task 8 distinguishes "compared and identical" from "only one side was
  // ever captured" with a `single_snapshot` signal — pass it through as-is
  // rather than flattening both into an empty diff, which would read as
  // "verified no drift" when the honest answer is "nothing to compare."
  return c.json(await driftBetween(from, to));
}));

upgrades.get('/upgrades/:id', guard('viewer', async (c) => {
  const id = c.req.param('id');
  const [run] = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeRuns).where(eq(schema.upgradeRuns.id, id));
  if (!run) return c.json({ error: 'not_found' }, 404);

  const events = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeEvents)
    .where(eq(schema.upgradeEvents.runId, id)).orderBy(schema.upgradeEvents.seq);
  const checklist = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeChecklistItems)
    .where(eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.runId, id));

  // playbook_ledger has no runId (Task 9 — a row is the INSTALL's history,
  // not a run's), so the versions THIS run touched come from the same
  // registry lookup the run itself used to decide what to apply.
  const versions = playbooksFor(run.fromVersion, run.toVersion).map((p) => p.version);
  const ledger = versions.length > 0
    ? await getDb().select().from(schema.playbookLedger).where(inArray(schema.playbookLedger.gbrainVersion, versions))
    : [];

  return c.json({ run, events, checklist, ledger });
}));

async function decide(c: any, ctx: { userId: string }, decision: 'acknowledged' | 'declined') {
  const itemId = c.req.param('itemId');
  const [item] = await getDb().select().from(schema.upgradeChecklistItems)
    .where(eq(schema.upgradeChecklistItems.id, itemId));
  if (!item) return c.json({ error: 'not_found' }, 404);

  // The audit row is written BEFORE the state change — the durable record
  // exists even if the process dies between the two writes. Same idiom as
  // schedule-alert acknowledgement and connection revoke.
  await audit(ctx.userId, `upgrade.checklist.${decision}`, itemId, {
    gbrainVersion: item.gbrainVersion, itemKey: item.itemKey,
  });
  const updated = await ackChecklistItem(getDb(), itemId, ctx.userId, decision);
  return c.json({ item: updated });
}

upgrades.post('/upgrades/checklist/:itemId/ack', guard('admin', (c, ctx) => decide(c, ctx, 'acknowledged')));
upgrades.post('/upgrades/checklist/:itemId/decline', guard('admin', (c, ctx) => decide(c, ctx, 'declined')));
```

In `src/control/app.ts`, add the import and route, and reconcile on boot plus on a timer:
```ts
import { runMigrations, getDb } from './db/client.ts';
import { upgrades } from './routes/upgrades.ts';
import { reconcileJournals, UPGRADE_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_MS } from '../upgrade/reconcile.ts';
// ...
app.route('/api', scheduleRoutes);
app.route('/api', upgrades);
app.route('/api', claimRoutes);
// ...
if (import.meta.main) {
  await runMigrations();
  const upgradeDir = process.env.UPGRADE_DIR ?? '/srv/upgrades';
  // Boot reconcile: the control plane was down for most of any upgrade that
  // ran while it was offline, so this is the first chance to see it.
  await reconcileJournals(getDb(), upgradeDir)
    .catch((e) => console.error('[upgrade] boot reconcile failed:', e));
  setInterval(() => {
    void reconcileJournals(getDb(), upgradeDir)
      .catch((e) => console.error('[upgrade] reconcile failed:', e));
  }, UPGRADE_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_MS);
  Bun.serve({ port: Number(process.env.PORT ?? 3150), hostname: '0.0.0.0', fetch: app.fetch });
  console.log(`control-plane listening on :${process.env.PORT ?? 3150}`);
}
```

In `compose/docker-compose.yml`, in the `control-plane` service — a second, SEPARATE mount from `gbrain-home`, added read-only next to it:
```yaml
  control-plane:
    image: brainstand/control-plane:${BRAINSTAND_VERSION:-dev}
    restart: unless-stopped
    environment:
      # S2: control DB only. No GBRAIN_DATABASE_URL, no DATABASE_URL.
      CONTROL_DATABASE_URL: postgres://gbrain:${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:?required}@postgres:5432/control
      OPS_RPC_URL: http://gbrain-ops:3140
      OPS_RPC_TOKEN: ${OPS_RPC_TOKEN:?required}
      ENCRYPTION_KEY: ${ENCRYPTION_KEY:?required}
      BETTER_AUTH_SECRET: ${BETTER_AUTH_SECRET:?required}
      PUBLIC_URL: https://${DOMAIN}
      GBRAIN_HOME: /srv/gbrain-home
      UPGRADE_DIR: /srv/upgrades
    command: ["bun", "run", "/app/src/control/app.ts"]
    volumes:
      # Read-only: the control plane reads config state, never writes it (S3).
      - gbrain-home:/srv/gbrain-home:ro
      # scripts/upgrade.ts runs on the Docker HOST (D1) and journals directly
      # to host disk at this same path; this bind-mounts that host path into
      # the container, read-only, so reconcileJournals can see it without the
      # control plane ever gaining a Docker socket or CLI (S2). The left side
      # resolves on the HOST (the operator's real journal directory); the
      # right side is fixed — inside the container it is always /srv/upgrades.
      - ${UPGRADE_DIR:-/srv/upgrades}:/srv/upgrades:ro
    depends_on:
      postgres: { condition: service_healthy }
    networks: [control-net, ops-net]
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run the tests to verify they pass**

Run: `bun test test/integration/upgrades.test.ts`
Expected: PASS, 19 tests.

Run: `bun test test/integration/compose-invariants.test.ts`
Expected: PASS, including the two new upgrade-mount invariants.

The assertion that matters most is `'the DB never goes backwards'`: after the three-event sequence
(`planned → migrating → quiescing`), `run!.state` must read `'migrating'`, not `'quiescing'`, while
`events.map((e) => e.type)` still lists all three. If it ever reads `'quiescing'`, `canTransition` is
being ignored somewhere in `applyEffects`/`advanceRunState` — do not "fix" this by deleting the
regressive event instead of refusing to apply it; the append-only log is what makes a later audit of
"what actually happened" possible, and dropping the event to make the assertion pass would be exactly
the kind of silent rewrite D2 exists to prevent.

> **Honesty rules 1 and 8, plus D1, apply here.** Rule 1: `bannerFor` must render "could not check"
> whenever `.error` is set and must never fall through to the "up to date" branch — the two are
> written below as genuinely different sentences, not two colors of one string. Rule 8: a pending
> checklist item is a first-class, persistent state, not a toast — the page names why it blocks
> nothing else. D1: the page has no "start upgrade" button anywhere; Section 5 states the constraint
> in plain words and prints the exact host command instead, because a button that cannot work is a
> worse UI than no button.

`test/unit/console-upgrades.test.ts`:
```ts
import { test, expect, describe, afterEach } from 'bun:test';
import {
  bannerFor, stepStatus, failedStep, rolledBack, runDuration, driftText,
  pendingChecklist, planCommand, runCommand, stepLabel, UPGRADE_STEPS,
} from '../../src/console/pages/Upgrades.tsx';
import { api, upgradeApi } from '../../src/console/api.ts';

describe('the detect banner — honesty rule 1', () => {
  test('a network failure never reads as up to date', () => {
    const banner = bannerFor({
      error: 'network_error', current_version: '0.50.0.0', latest_version: '',
      update_available: false, changelog_diff: '', release_url: '',
    });
    expect(banner).toMatch(/could not check/i);
    expect(banner).not.toMatch(/up to date/i);
  });

  test('no releases reachable is its own honest sentence, not a silent success', () => {
    const banner = bannerFor({
      error: 'no_releases', current_version: '0.50.0.0', latest_version: '',
      update_available: false, changelog_diff: '', release_url: '',
    });
    expect(banner).toMatch(/could not check/i);
  });

  test('a clean check with nothing available says up to date', () => {
    expect(bannerFor({
      current_version: '0.50.5.0', latest_version: '0.50.5.0', update_available: false,
      changelog_diff: '', release_url: '',
    })).toMatch(/up to date/i);
  });

  test('a candidate is offered by name, never applied', () => {
    const banner = bannerFor({
      current_version: '0.50.0.0', latest_version: '0.50.5.0', update_available: true,
      changelog_diff: '', release_url: '',
    });
    expect(banner).toContain('0.50.5.0');
    expect(banner).toContain('0.50.0.0');
  });

  test('a load still in flight is distinct from either outcome', () => {
    expect(bannerFor(null)).not.toMatch(/up to date|could not check/i);
  });
});

describe('the run timeline', () => {
  const ev = (type: string, step: string) => ({ id: '1', runId: 'r1', seq: 1, at: '2026-09-16T00:00:00Z', type, data: { step } });

  test('every one of the eight steps has a human label', () => {
    expect(UPGRADE_STEPS).toHaveLength(8);
    for (const step of UPGRADE_STEPS) expect(stepLabel(step)).not.toBe(step);
  });

  test('a step with no event is pending, one that started is active, one that finished is done', () => {
    expect(stepStatus([], 'snapshot')).toBe('pending');
    expect(stepStatus([ev('step_started', 'snapshot')], 'snapshot')).toBe('active');
    expect(stepStatus([ev('step_started', 'snapshot'), ev('step_finished', 'snapshot')], 'snapshot')).toBe('done');
  });

  test('a failed step reads as failed even if it also started', () => {
    expect(stepStatus([ev('step_started', 'migrate'), ev('step_failed', 'migrate')], 'migrate')).toBe('failed');
  });

  test('failedStep names which of the eight steps died', () => {
    expect(failedStep([ev('step_started', 'migrate'), ev('step_failed', 'migrate')])).toBe('migrate');
    expect(failedStep([ev('step_started', 'plan'), ev('step_finished', 'plan')])).toBeNull();
  });

  test('a rolled-back run is named as such, not just "failed"', () => {
    const run = { id: 'r1', fromVersion: '0.50.0.0', toVersion: '0.50.5.0', state: 'rolled_back', startedAt: '2026-09-16T00:00:00Z', finishedAt: '2026-09-16T00:05:00Z', snapshotId: 's1', createdBy: 'op' };
    expect(rolledBack(run)).toBe(true);
    expect(rolledBack({ ...run, state: 'failed' })).toBe(false);
  });

  test('duration is measured, not guessed, and an unfinished run says so', () => {
    const started = { id: 'r1', fromVersion: 'a', toVersion: 'b', state: 'snapshotting', startedAt: '2026-09-16T00:00:00Z', finishedAt: null, snapshotId: null, createdBy: 'op' };
    expect(runDuration(started)).toBe('in progress');
    expect(runDuration({ ...started, finishedAt: '2026-09-16T00:00:45Z' })).toBe('45s');
    expect(runDuration({ ...started, finishedAt: '2026-09-16T00:02:30Z' })).toBe('3m');
  });
});

describe('the blocking checklist — honesty rule 8', () => {

describe('the drift panel — "nothing to compare" is not "no drift"', () => {
  test('a single snapshot says so, never that drift was checked and found clean', () => {

    });

describe('the honest absence — D1', () => {
  test('the commands name the real target version once one is known, no placeholder button', () => {
    expect(planCommand('0.50.5.0')).toBe('bun run scripts/upgrade.ts plan --to 0.50.5.0');
    expect(runCommand('0.50.5.0')).toBe('bun run scripts/upgrade.ts run --to 0.50.5.0');

const orig = globalThis.fetch;
afterEach(() => { globalThis.fetch = orig; });

const capture = (status = 200, body: unknown = {}) => {
  const seen: { url: string; method: string } = { url: '', method: '' };
  globalThis.fetch = (async (url: string, init?: RequestInit) => {
    seen.url = url; seen.method = init?.method ?? 'GET';
    return new Response(JSON.stringify(body), { status, headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' } });
  }) as unknown as typeof fetch;
  return seen;
};

describe('the upgrade api client', () => {
  test('available and list address their own resources', async () => {
    const a = capture(200, { current_version: '0.50.0.0' });
    await upgradeApi.available();
    expect(a.url).toBe('/api/upgrades/available');

    const l = capture(200, { runs: [] });
    await upgradeApi.list();
    expect(l.url).toBe('/api/upgrades');
  });

  test('drift carries both versions as query params', async () => {
    const seen = capture(200, { signal: 'no_drift' });
    await upgradeApi.drift('0.50.0.0', '0.50.5.0');
    expect(seen.url).toBe('/api/upgrades/drift?from=0.50.0.0&to=0.50.5.0');
  });

  test('ack and decline are distinct POSTs to distinct resources', async () => {
    const a = capture(200, { item: {} });
    await upgradeApi.ack('item1');
    expect(a.url).toBe('/api/upgrades/checklist/item1/ack');
    expect(a.method).toBe('POST');

    const d = capture(200, { item: {} });
    await upgradeApi.decline('item1');
    expect(d.url).toBe('/api/upgrades/checklist/item1/decline');
    expect(d.method).toBe('POST');
  });

  test('the generic client is still the one doing the work', () => {
    expect(typeof api.get).toBe('function');
  });
});
```

**What this file does not cover, and why that is not silently accepted.** RECON's own audit of this
repo's console tests is explicit: there is no DOM server and no browser automation anywhere in
`test/unit` or `test/e2e` — `console-schedules.test.ts` and `console-api.test.ts` both test pure
render/format functions and the `fetch` call shape only, never a mounted component, a click, or a
poll firing. The five content requirements below (banner, timeline, checklist, drift panel, honest
absence) are exercised here exactly as far as that idiom reaches: every string a human reads is
produced by an exported pure function, and every request the page makes is produced by `upgradeApi`,
both asserted above. What is **not** covered here: that `Upgrades()` actually renders those strings
in the right place, that clicking "Acknowledge" calls `upgradeApi.ack` with the right id, or that the
15s poll fires. That gap is real, not vacuous — Slice 0's `blockers` test is the standing warning
against writing something that *looks* like coverage and passes regardless. The honest fix is not a
fake DOM assertion in this file; it is Gate B (`test/e2e/slice35.e2e.test.ts`), which drives the real
`POST /api/upgrades/checklist/:itemId/ack` over HTTP against a running stack and — until this repo
grows a browser-automation harness — a manual walkthrough of `/upgrades` as part of Gate C's exit
checklist, recorded in `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` rather than asserted by a test that cannot see a
screen.
