Add to `src/console/api.ts`, after `scheduleApi`:
```ts
/**
 * The upgrades surface (`src/control/routes/upgrades.ts`). Field names stay
 * snake_case where they echo upstream's own `check-update --json` shape
 * (D7/G3) — this client does not re-interpret the contract, only forwards it.
 */
export type DetectResult = {
  current_version: string;
  latest_version: string;
  update_available: boolean;
  changelog_diff: string;
  release_url: string;
  error?: 'network_error' | 'no_releases';
};

export type UpgradeRunRow = {
  id: string;
  fromVersion: string;
  toVersion: string;
  state: string;
  startedAt: string;
  finishedAt: string | null;
  snapshotId: string | null;
  createdBy: string;
};

export type UpgradeEventRow = { id: string; runId: string; seq: number; at: string; type: string; data: unknown };

export type ChecklistItemRow = {
  id: string; runId: string; gbrainVersion: string; itemKey: string;
  prompt: string; why: string; state: string; decidedBy: string | null; decidedAt: string | null;
};

export type LedgerRow = { id: string; gbrainVersion: string; state: string; notes: string | null };

export type CapabilityDrift = {
  versionChanged: boolean; toolsAdded: string[]; toolsRemoved: string[];
  adaptersAdded: string[]; adaptersRemoved: string[]; profilesChanged: boolean;
};

export type DriftResult = {
  signal: 'drift' | 'no_drift' | 'single_snapshot';
  from: string; to: string;
  diff: CapabilityDrift | null;
};

export type UpgradeRunDetail = {
  run: UpgradeRunRow; events: UpgradeEventRow[]; checklist: ChecklistItemRow[]; ledger: LedgerRow[];
};

export const upgradeApi = {
  available: () => api.get<DetectResult>('/upgrades/available'),
  list: () => api.get<{ runs: UpgradeRunRow[] }>('/upgrades'),
  detail: (id: string) => api.get<UpgradeRunDetail>(`/upgrades/${encodeURIComponent(id)}`),
  drift: (from: string, to: string) =>
    api.get<DriftResult>(`/upgrades/drift?from=${encodeURIComponent(from)}&to=${encodeURIComponent(to)}`),
  ack: (itemId: string) => api.post<{ item: ChecklistItemRow }>(`/upgrades/checklist/${encodeURIComponent(itemId)}/ack`),
  decline: (itemId: string) => api.post<{ item: ChecklistItemRow }>(`/upgrades/checklist/${encodeURIComponent(itemId)}/decline`),
};
```

`src/console/pages/Upgrades.tsx`:
```tsx
import { useEffect, useState } from 'react';
import {
  api, upgradeApi, type DetectResult, type UpgradeRunRow, type UpgradeEventRow,
  type ChecklistItemRow, type DriftResult, type UpgradeRunDetail,
} from '../api.ts';

/**
 * The eight steps every run passes through, in order (Task 10's run state
 * machine). Kept LOCAL to the console on purpose: src/upgrade/state.ts pulls
 * in server-only modules (Postgres, node:fs), and this bundle ships to a
 * browser.
 */
export const UPGRADE_STEPS = [
  'plan', 'snapshot', 'quiesce', 'swap', 'migrate', 'playbooks', 'resume', 'verify',
] as const;

const STEP_LABEL: Record<string, string> = {
  plan: 'Plan', snapshot: 'Snapshot', quiesce: 'Quiesce', swap: 'Swap images',
  migrate: 'Apply migrations', playbooks: 'Playbooks', resume: 'Resume', verify: 'Verify (doctor)',
};

export function stepLabel(step: string): string {
  return STEP_LABEL[step] ?? step;
}

/**
 * Honesty rule 1: `check-update` never sets an exit code, so a network
 * failure is visible ONLY through `.error`. These branches read as two
 * unrelated facts, not two shades of the same one.
 */
export function bannerFor(detect: DetectResult | null): string {
  if (!detect) return 'Checking…';
  if (detect.error === 'network_error') return 'Could not check for an update — the network request failed.';
  if (detect.error === 'no_releases') return 'Could not check for an update — upstream has no release info.';
  if (!detect.update_available) return `Up to date at ${detect.current_version}.`;
  return `${detect.latest_version} is available (currently ${detect.current_version}).`;
}

export function stepStatus(events: UpgradeEventRow[], step: string): 'pending' | 'active' | 'done' | 'failed' {
  const has = (type: string) => events.some((e) => e.type === type && (e.data as { step?: string } | null)?.step === step);
  if (has('step_failed')) return 'failed';
  if (has('step_finished')) return 'done';
  if (has('step_started')) return 'active';
  return 'pending';
}

/** The step a failed run died on, or null for a run that never failed. */
export function failedStep(events: UpgradeEventRow[]): string | null {
  const ev = events.find((e) => e.type === 'step_failed');
  return ev ? String((ev.data as { step?: string } | null)?.step ?? 'unknown') : null;
}

export function rolledBack(run: UpgradeRunRow): boolean {
  return run.state === 'rolled_back';
}

export function runDuration(run: UpgradeRunRow): string {
  if (!run.finishedAt) return 'in progress';
  const ms = new Date(run.finishedAt).getTime() - new Date(run.startedAt).getTime();
  if (ms < 0) return 'unknown';
  const s = Math.round(ms / 1000);
  return s < 90 ? `${s}s` : `${Math.round(s / 60)}m`;
}

/**
 * "Nothing to compare" and "compared, identical" are different facts. Task 8
 * marks the first with `single_snapshot`; collapsing both into one string
 * would tell an operator "verified no drift" when only one side was ever
 * captured.
 */
export function driftText(drift: DriftResult | null): string {
  if (!drift) return 'Loading…';
  if (drift.signal === 'single_snapshot') {
    return `Nothing to compare yet — only one of ${drift.from} / ${drift.to} has a recorded capability snapshot.`;
  }
  if (drift.signal === 'no_drift') return `No capability drift between ${drift.from} and ${drift.to}.`;
  const d = drift.diff!;
  const parts: string[] = [];
  if (d.toolsAdded.length) parts.push(`+${d.toolsAdded.length} tools`);
  if (d.toolsRemoved.length) parts.push(`-${d.toolsRemoved.length} tools`);
  if (d.adaptersAdded.length) parts.push(`+${d.adaptersAdded.length} adapters`);
  if (d.adaptersRemoved.length) parts.push(`-${d.adaptersRemoved.length} adapters`);
  if (d.profilesChanged) parts.push('profiles changed');
  return parts.length > 0 ? `Drift: ${parts.join(', ')}.` : `Drift recorded between ${drift.from} and ${drift.to}.`;
}

export function pendingChecklist(items: ChecklistItemRow[]): ChecklistItemRow[] {
  return items.filter((i) => i.state === 'awaiting_admin_decision');
}

/** The exact host command this page names in place of a "start" button (D1). */
export function planCommand(toVersion: string): string {
  return `bun run scripts/upgrade.ts plan --to ${toVersion}`;
}
export function runCommand(toVersion: string): string {
  return `bun run scripts/upgrade.ts run --to ${toVersion}`;
}

export function Upgrades() {
  const [detect, setDetect] = useState<DetectResult | null>(null);
  const [runs, setRuns] = useState<UpgradeRunRow[]>([]);
  const [openRun, setOpenRun] = useState<UpgradeRunDetail | null>(null);
  const [drift, setDrift] = useState<DriftResult | null>(null);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false);

  const load = async () => {
    const [d, r] = await Promise.all([
      upgradeApi.available().catch((): DetectResult => ({
        current_version: '', latest_version: '', update_available: false,
        changelog_diff: '', release_url: '', error: 'network_error',
      })),
      upgradeApi.list(),
    ]);
    setDetect(d);
    setRuns(r.runs);
    const latest = r.runs[0];
    if (latest) {
      const detail = await upgradeApi.detail(latest.id).catch(() => null);
      if (detail) setOpenRun(detail);
      const dr = await upgradeApi.drift(latest.fromVersion, latest.toVersion).catch(() => null);
      if (dr) setDrift(dr);
    }
    setError(null);
  };

  useEffect(() => { void load().catch((e) => setError(e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Load failed')); }, []);
  useEffect(() => {
    const t = setInterval(() => { void load().catch(() => { /* the next poll retries */ }); }, 15_000);
    return () => clearInterval(t);
  }, []);

  const decide = (itemId: string, decision: 'ack' | 'decline') => async () => {
    setBusy(true);
    try {
      await (decision === 'ack' ? upgradeApi.ack(itemId) : upgradeApi.decline(itemId));
      await load();
    } catch (e) {
      setError(e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Could not record the decision');
    } finally {
      setBusy(false);
    }
  };

  const target = detect && !detect.error && detect.update_available ? detect.latest_version : '<version>';

  return (
    <div className="max-w-3xl space-y-6">
      <header><h1 className="text-xl font-semibold">Upgrades</h1></header>

      {error && <div className="text-danger text-sm">{error}</div>}

      <section className="card space-y-2">
        <div className={detect?.error ? 'text-warning' : 'text-sm'}>{bannerFor(detect)}</div>
        {detect && !detect.error && detect.update_available && detect.changelog_diff && (
          <pre className="text-xs text-muted whitespace-pre-wrap font-mono max-h-40 overflow-y-auto">
            {detect.changelog_diff}
          </pre>
        )}
      </section>

      <section className="card space-y-2">
        <h2 className="text-lg font-semibold">Runs</h2>
        {runs.length === 0 ? <p className="text-sm text-muted">No upgrade has run yet.</p> : (
          <table className="w-full text-sm">
            <thead>
              <tr className="border-b border-subtle text-left">
                <th className="py-2 pr-3">version</th>
                <th className="py-2 pr-3">state</th>
                <th className="py-2 pr-3">duration</th>
                <th className="py-2">snapshot</th>
              </tr>
            </thead>
            <tbody>
              {runs.map((r) => (
                <tr key={r.id} className="border-b border-subtle">
                  <td className="py-2 pr-3 font-mono">{r.fromVersion} → {r.toVersion}</td>
                  <td className="py-2 pr-3">{r.state}{rolledBack(r) ? ' (rolled back)' : ''}</td>
                  <td className="py-2 pr-3">{runDuration(r)}</td>
                  <td className="py-2 font-mono text-xs">{r.snapshotId ?? '—'}</td>
                </tr>
              ))}
            </tbody>
          </table>
        )}

        {openRun && (
          <div className="pt-2 space-y-1">
            <div className="text-sm text-muted">
              Steps for {openRun.run.fromVersion} → {openRun.run.toVersion}
            </div>
            <div className="flex gap-2 flex-wrap">
              {UPGRADE_STEPS.map((step) => {
                const status = stepStatus(openRun.events, step);
                const tone = status === 'failed' ? 'text-danger'
                  : status === 'done' ? 'text-ok'
                  : status === 'active' ? 'text-warning' : 'text-muted';
                return <span key={step} className={`text-xs ${tone}`}>{stepLabel(step)}</span>;
              })}
            </div>
            {failedStep(openRun.events) && (
              <div className="text-sm text-danger">
                Failed at: {stepLabel(failedStep(openRun.events)!)}
                {rolledBack(openRun.run) ? ' — rolled back.' : ' — not rolled back.'}
              </div>
            )}
          </div>
        )}
      </section>

      {openRun && pendingChecklist(openRun.checklist).length > 0 && (
        <section className="card border-warning space-y-3">
          <h2 className="text-lg font-semibold text-warning">Awaiting your decision</h2>
          <p className="text-xs text-muted">
            These persist until acknowledged or declined and block nothing else while they wait.
          </p>
          {pendingChecklist(openRun.checklist).map((item) => (
            <div key={item.id} className="border-b border-subtle pb-2 space-y-1">
              <div className="font-medium">{item.prompt}</div>
              <div className="text-sm text-muted">{item.why}</div>
              <div className="flex gap-2">
                <button disabled={busy} className="nav-item" onClick={decide(item.id, 'ack')}>Acknowledge</button>
                <button disabled={busy} className="nav-item" onClick={decide(item.id, 'decline')}>Decline</button>
              </div>
            </div>
          ))}
        </section>
      )}

      <section className="card space-y-1">
        <h2 className="text-lg font-semibold">Capability drift</h2>
        <div className="text-sm">{driftText(drift)}</div>
      </section>

      <section className="card space-y-2">
        <h2 className="text-lg font-semibold">Starting an upgrade</h2>
        <p className="text-sm text-muted">
          Upgrades run on the Docker host, not from this console — the control plane holds no Docker
          socket and cannot swap images or quiesce services. That is a privilege boundary, not a
          missing feature. Run it from the host:
        </p>
        <pre className="text-xs font-mono bg-subtle p-2 rounded">
          {planCommand(target)}{'\n'}{runCommand(target)}
        </pre>
      </section>
    </div>
  );
}
```

In `src/console/App.tsx`, wire the nav entry exactly the way `/schedules` is wired:

  if (path === '/schedules') return 'schedules';

// ...
        {phase === 'ready' && (
          <>
            <button className="nav-item" onClick={() => setPage('members')}>Members</button>

      <main className="flex-1 p-6">
        {page === 'health' && <Health />}
        {page === 'setup' && <Wizard onReady={refresh} />}
        {page === 'members' && <Members />}
        {page === 'connection' && <Connection />}

        {page === 'upgrades' && <Upgrades />}

- [ ] **Step 4: Run the test to verify it passes**

Run: `bun test test/unit/console-upgrades.test.ts`
Expected: PASS, 20 tests.

The assertion that matters most is the pair in `'the detect banner'`: `bannerFor` on an
`error: 'network_error'` result must match `/could not check/i` **and** fail `/up to date/i`. If a
future edit ever makes both pass on the same input (e.g. by making "Could not check — currently up to
date at X" the fallback text), that is honesty rule 1 breaking in slow motion — split the sentence
back into two unrelated ones rather than relaxing the `.not.toMatch`.

## Task 15: Seam Gate A — the upgrade seam, headless

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — A7's `clean===true` is unachievable through `gbrain jobs supervisor stop`
> at this pin; the gate drives the honest verdict instead.** `jobs supervisor stop` writes a clean
> in-window `shutting_down{reason:SIGTERM,exit_code:0}` audit event but leaves its **worker** process
> alive — the pid in `/srv/gbrain-home/.gbrain/workers/worker-*.json` was still in `/proc` more than
> 42 s later — and `--concurrency 0` still spawns one worker. `verifySupervisorDrained` counts live
> workers, so a supervisor-CLI-only stop classifies `unproven`, never `clean`. A7 first drove the
> container boundary (option B), which yields **no clean event at all** — a detached supervisor inside
> slice0's `gbrain-ops` is not tini's child, so it never receives SIGTERM — then asserts the honest
> `unproven` verdict for the CLI path and keeps the honesty-rule-3 property as a deterministic fixture
> (a clean `shutting_down{exit_code:0}` with a live worker must not be recorded clean). The
> `clean===true` positive is proven end-to-end by Gate C's C6 against the real quiesce, which stops the
> `gbrain-worker` container and takes the worker with it. This is a **supervisor-CLI-usage hazard, not
> a quiesce defect**. See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` F2.

**Files:**
- Modify: `test/contract/upgrade.contract.test.ts` (created in Task 7; this task appends
  `describe('Slice 3.5 seam gate A')` and the tripwire)

**Inherited harness rule, load-bearing — do not rediscover it.** Slice 3's Gate A was corrected
mid-execution because `composeExec` in `src/docker/host.ts` targets the **slice0 harness project**
(`compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml`), which runs the pinned `brainstand/gbrain-base:0.50.0.0` image
and is **never rebuilt from the working tree** — so a regenerated `contracts/v<pin>/operations.json`
does not exist there and the RPC seam is untestable. Every test in this gate that crosses the **RPC
boundary** therefore targets the **MAIN product stack**'s `gbrain-ops` at `http://127.0.0.1:3140`
(published by `compose/docker-compose.dev.yml`, the same port `test/e2e/host-ops-env.ts` binds).
Tests that only need the pinned binary may use `execOperation`. **Do not add a `ports:` entry to
anything** to make this easier.

**What this gate proves that unit and integration tests cannot:** the two new operations work
*through the RPC boundary*, in the real container, against the **real allowlist artifact** — the
failure Slice 0 named exactly. A registry entry added without running `bun run operations:emit` is
*"a revoke button that does nothing"*: `validateRequest` (`src/ops/dispatch.ts:23-49`) 400s with
`unknown operation ${op}` even though `registry.ts` knows the id perfectly well. Unit tests import
the module. **Only this gate loads the JSON.**

It also proves four things the type system cannot: that `check-update` really exits 0 when the
network is gone (U1), that `post-upgrade` really rewrites `config.json` and really is idempotent
(U4), that `apply-migrations` really has no JSON to parse (U3), and that a snapshot manifest really
detects a tampered artifact (honesty rule 10).

```ts
describe('Slice 3.5 seam gate A', () => {
  test('slice3.5 contract tests are not silently skipped', () => {
    if (dockerAvailable) return;
    if (process.env.BRAINSTAND_ALLOW_SKIP_SLICE35_CONTRACT === '1') return;
    throw new Error(
      'docker compose is not usable, so the slice3.5 contract suite was skipped rather than run. '
        + 'Install/start Docker, then rerun:\n\n  bun test test/contract/upgrade.contract.test.ts\n\n'
        + 'If skipping is genuinely intended, set BRAINSTAND_ALLOW_SKIP_SLICE35_CONTRACT=1.',
    );
  });

  test('A1. the emitted allowlist carries checkUpdate and postUpgrade', async () => {
    // operationsPath() comes from src/contracts/pin.ts (Task 2) — NOT a literal
    // 'contracts/v0.50.0.0/...', which is the defect D8 exists to kill.
    const doc = JSON.parse(readFileSync(operationsPath(), 'utf8'));
    expect(doc.gbrainVersion).toBe(GBRAIN_PIN);
    const byId = Object.fromEntries(doc.operations.map((o: any) => [o.id, o]));
    expect(byId['checkUpdate']).toBeDefined();
    expect(byId['postUpgrade']).toBeDefined();
    expect(doc.operations.length).toBe(33);              // 31 at Slice 3 close + 2
    // U1/U3: neither declares a dry run, and only checkUpdate has --json.
    expect(byId['checkUpdate'].supportsJson).toBe(true);
    expect(byId['postUpgrade'].supportsJson).toBe(false);
    expect(byId['checkUpdate'].privilege).toBe('read-only');
    expect(byId['postUpgrade'].privilege).toBe('brain-db');

    // The seam itself: the id is reachable, not merely declared.
    const r = await opsRpc('checkUpdate', {}, { json: true });
    expect(r.error).toBeUndefined();                     // NOT "unknown operation"
  });

  test('A2. check-update --json emits the documented key set, through the RPC', async () => {
    const r = await opsRpc('checkUpdate', {}, { json: true });
    expect(r.exitCode).toBe(0);
    const body = r.json as Record<string, unknown>;
    for (const k of ['current_version', 'current_source', 'latest_version', 'update_available',
                     'upgrade_command', 'release_url', 'changelog_diff', 'published_at']) {
      expect(Object.keys(body)).toContain(k);
    }
    expect(body.current_source).toBe('package-json');
    expect(body.current_version).toBe(GBRAIN_PIN);
    // U2: the source has no timestamp. If this ever stops being '', the banner
    // may start showing a release date — until then it must not pretend to.
    expect(body.published_at).toBe('');
    expect(typeof body.update_available).toBe('boolean');
    // L12: an accepted flag is not an implemented flag. This is the proof that
    // --json is real for THIS command, never an assumption inherited from another.
    expect(r.json).not.toBeNull();
  }, 120_000);

  test('A3. check-update exits 0 when the network is genuinely gone, and says so', async () => {
    // U1 is the single most dangerous upstream fact in this slice: there is no
    // process.exit() anywhere in runCheckUpdate, so an exit-code check is a
    // silent lie. Asserting `expect([0]).toContain(r.exitCode)` on a HEALTHY
    // network proves nothing at all — it would pass even if upstream added an
    // exit code tomorrow, because the happy path exits 0 either way.
    //
    // So the failure is OBSERVED, not hoped for: a throwaway container on the
    // pinned image with `--network none` and its own tmpfs GBRAIN_HOME (so the
    // cached last-update-check cannot answer instead of the network). argv
    // array, no shell string, same convention as the snapshot driver (D3).
    const r = await dockerRunOffline([
      'gbrain', 'check-update', '--json',
    ]);

    const body = lastJsonValue(r.stdout) as Record<string, unknown> | null;
    // The assertion is "we induced a real failure", and it FAILS rather than
    // passes vacuously if the container reached the network after all.
    if (!body || body.error === undefined) {
      throw new Error(
        `the offline probe did not fail: exit ${r.exitCode}, stdout ${r.stdout.slice(0, 400)}. `
        + 'U1 cannot be proven against a healthy network — fix the probe, never the assertion.',
      );
    }
    expect(body.error).toBe('network_error');
    expect(r.exitCode).toBe(0);                          // U1, observed
    expect(body.update_available).toBe(false);

    // Honesty rule 1 at the seam: our parser must NOT read that as "up to date".
    const parsed = detectUpdate(body);
    expect(parsed.status).toBe('unknown');
    expect(parsed.error).toBe('network_error');
    expect(parsed.candidateVersion).toBeNull();
  }, 180_000);

  test('A4. post-upgrade is idempotent and what it wrote to config.json is observable', async () => {
    // U4: applySelfUpgradeSetup() does a LOCK-FREE read-modify-write of
    // ~/.gbrain/config.json (self_upgrade.*), which is why our lane only ever
    // runs it inside the quiesce window behind the ops advisory lock (S3).
    const before = await readGbrainConfig();

    const first = await opsRpc('postUpgrade', {}, {});
    expect(first.exitCode).toBe(0);
    const afterFirst = await readGbrainConfig();
    expect(afterFirst.self_upgrade?.mode).toBe('notify');        // set when unset
    expect(afterFirst.self_upgrade?.mode_prompted).toBe(true);

    const second = await opsRpc('postUpgrade', {}, {});
    expect(second.exitCode).toBe(0);
    const afterSecond = await readGbrainConfig();
    // Idempotent means the SECOND run changed nothing, not merely exited 0.
    expect(afterSecond.self_upgrade).toEqual(afterFirst.self_upgrade);

    // U5, and the reason we tolerate the write at all: the env var overrides
    // config (src/core/self-upgrade.ts:475-484), so `notify` in config.json
    // does not re-arm the self-upgrade channel in our containers. Assert the
    // override is still IN PLACE — the behavioural half is Task 1's P-row.
    const env = await mainExec(['printenv', 'GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE']);
    expect(env.stdout.trim()).toBe('off');
    expect(before).toBeTruthy();   // the file existed before we touched it
  }, 600_000);

  test('A5. apply-migrations exits 0 on an up-to-date brain and has no JSON to parse', async () => {
    const spec = OPERATIONS.applyMigrations!;
    // G3/U3: there is no --json. buildArgv must not be able to produce one.
    expect(spec.supportsJson).toBe(false);
    expect(buildArgv(spec, { yes: true, nonInteractive: true }, { json: true }))
      .toEqual(['apply-migrations', '--yes', '--non-interactive']);

    const r = await execOperation(spec, { yes: true, nonInteractive: true }, { timeoutMs: 300_000 });
    expect(r.exitCode).toBe(0);
    expect(r.json).toBeNull();                 // prose only; parsing it is a G3 violation
    // U3: 0 / 1 / 2 are the whole universe. A code outside it means upstream
    // changed and our runner's branching is now incomplete.
    expect([0, 1, 2]).toContain(r.exitCode);
    // The declared contract agrees.
    expect([...spec.okExitCodes]).toEqual([0]);
  }, 600_000);

  test('A6. doctor --json has the documented keys, no blockers, and the comparator works on it', async () => {
    const r = await opsRpc('doctor', {}, { json: true });
    const report = r.json as any;
    expect(Object.keys(report)).not.toContain('blockers');    // L4, re-asserted
    for (const k of ['schema_version', 'status', 'health_score', 'category_scores', 'checks']) {
      expect(Object.keys(report)).toContain(k);
    }
    expect(['healthy', 'warnings', 'unhealthy']).toContain(report.status);
    expect(report.checks.every((c: any) => ['ok', 'warn', 'fail'].includes(c.status))).toBe(true);
    expect(report.checks.some((c: any) => c.name === 'schema_version')).toBe(true);

    // D5, against a REAL report rather than a literal fixture.
    expect(compareDoctor(report, report))
      .toEqual({ regressed: [], newChecks: [], resolved: [] });

    const firstOk = report.checks.find((c: any) => c.status === 'ok');
    const regressedReport = mutateCheck(report, firstOk.name, 'warn');
    expect(compareDoctor(report, regressedReport).regressed.map((c: any) => c.name))
      .toEqual([firstOk.name]);
    expect(gateVerdict(compareDoctor(report, regressedReport))).toBe('fail');

    // A check upstream ADDS is news, not a regression we caused.
    const widened = { ...report, checks: [...report.checks, { name: 'brand_new_upstream_check', status: 'warn', message: 'x' }] };
    const widenedDiff = compareDoctor(report, widened);
    expect(widenedDiff.regressed).toEqual([]);
    expect(widenedDiff.newChecks.map((c: any) => c.name)).toEqual(['brand_new_upstream_check']);
    expect(gateVerdict(widenedDiff)).toBe('pass');

    // U7: schema_version warn is the ONLY way to see "binary behind DB", and
    // `warnings` exits 0 — so the gate must fail on it even at exit 0.
    expect(gateVerdict(compareDoctor(report, mutateCheck(report, 'schema_version', 'warn')))).toBe('fail');
    expect(r.exitCode).toBe(report.status === 'unhealthy' ? 1 : 0);
  }, 900_000);

  test('A7. the drain proof distinguishes a clean exit from a crash', async () => {
    // L2: `start --detach` ALWAYS exits 0, so the start code is not evidence.
    const start = await execOperation(OPERATIONS['jobs.supervisor']!, { detach: true, concurrency: '2' },
      { json: true, timeoutMs: 120_000 });
    expect(start.exitCode).toBe(0);
    await waitForSupervisorRunning(60_000);              // poll status --json, never trust start

    const stop = await execOperation(OPERATIONS['jobs.supervisorStop']!, {}, { timeoutMs: 90_000 });
    expect(stop.exitCode).toBe(0);                       // reason:'drained' — NOT yet evidence (U9)

    const proof = await verifySupervisorDrained();
    expect(proof.clean).toBe(true);
    expect(proof.exitCode).toBe(0);                      // ExitCodes.CLEAN
    expect(['SIGTERM', 'SIGINT']).toContain(proof.reason);
    expect(proof.running).toBe(false);
    expect(proof.workers).toEqual([]);

    // The negative half is a REAL audit file parsed by the SAME reader, written
    // into a throwaway GBRAIN_AUDIT_DIR — not a hand-mocked return value. We do
    // not induce MAX_CRASHES in a live supervisor; that is stated here and in
    // the findings doc rather than dressed up.
    const dir = await writeAuditFixture([
      { event: 'stopped', exit_code: 1, reason: 'MAX_CRASHES' },
    ]);
    expect((await verifySupervisorDrained({ auditDir: dir })).clean).toBe(false);

    // L3: 4 is LOCK_LOST — "someone else owns this", not an undifferentiated crash.
    const lost = await writeAuditFixture([{ event: 'stopped', exit_code: 4, reason: 'SIGTERM' }]);
    const lostProof = await verifySupervisorDrained({ auditDir: lost });
    expect(lostProof.clean).toBe(false);
    expect(lostProof.classification).toBe('lock_lost');
  }, 600_000);

  test('A8. a snapshot manifest verifies against the artifacts it names', async () => {
    // A real driver run against a throwaway volume: no product service is
    // touched, and the docker-volume driver's own argv path is exercised.
    const vol = `slice35-gate-a-${RUN_ID}`;
    await seedThrowawayVolume(vol, 'canary.txt', 'gate-a\n');
    const taken = await dockerVolumeDriver.take({
      runId: `gate-a-${RUN_ID}`, volumes: [vol], databases: [], outDir: OUT,
    });
    expect(taken.artifacts.length).toBe(1);
    expect(taken.artifacts[0]!.sha256).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/);
    expect(taken.artifacts[0]!.bytes).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(taken.driver).toBe('docker-volume');
    expect(await verifyManifest(taken)).toEqual({ ok: true, mismatches: [] });

    // Re-hashing off disk, not off the manifest's own memory.
    expect(await sha256File(taken.artifacts[0]!.path)).toBe(taken.artifacts[0]!.sha256);
  }, 600_000);

  test('A9. a tampered artifact fails verification', async () => {
    // Honesty rule 10: a snapshot nobody verified is not a backup. One appended
    // byte must be enough — this is the assertion that makes the checksum real.
    appendFileSync(manifestFromA8.artifacts[0]!.path, '\0');
    const verdict = await verifyManifest(manifestFromA8);
    expect(verdict.ok).toBe(false);
    expect(verdict.mismatches).toEqual([manifestFromA8.artifacts[0]!.name]);

    // A missing artifact is a failure, never a skip.
    rmSync(manifestFromA8.artifacts[0]!.path);
    expect((await verifyManifest(manifestFromA8)).ok).toBe(false);
  }, 300_000);
});
```

`opsRpc` posts to `http://127.0.0.1:3140/rpc` with the bearer `OPS_RPC_TOKEN` from `.env`, exactly as
Slice 3's Gate A does; `mainExec` is `docker compose --env-file .env -f compose/docker-compose.yml -f
compose/docker-compose.dev.yml exec -T gbrain-ops <argv>`. `dockerRunOffline` is
`docker run --rm --network none --tmpfs /gb -e GBRAIN_HOME=/gb brainstand/gbrain-base:${GBRAIN_PIN} <argv>`.
`readGbrainConfig` reads `${GBRAIN_HOME}/config.json` through `gbrain-ops` — a **file-plane** read, the
lane `src/ops/fileplane.ts` and `test/integration/fileplane.test.ts` already own, not a table read.

## Task 16: Seam Gate B — HTTP end to end

> **Corrected 2026-09-17 — the shipped drift route returned 400 for the single-snapshot case; it now passes the signal through, and B9 asserts it.** This task's own sample (and the plan's self-review #6) already said Gate B must hold whether it runs before or after Gate C: with only one pin captured, the route must not render an empty diff as "no drift" and must not 400. The executed route let `driftBetween`'s `SingleSnapshotError` escape the handler and become a 400, and the shipped B9 asserted a two-sided diff unconditionally, so a fresh family run (`restore.e2e` wipes the control DB first) failed at B9. `src/control/routes/upgrades.ts` now catches `SingleSnapshotError` and returns `200 { single_snapshot: true, available }`; B9 asserts that branch and still asserts the real diff when both pins exist. The real two-sided diff is C10's, after the upgrade. See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` F7.

**Files:**
- Create: `test/e2e/slice35.e2e.test.ts` (Gate B here; Gate C in Task 17, same file)

**Harness rules, all inherited, all load-bearing, each cited by the learning that bought it:**

- **L10 / Slice 3 F5 — the `beforeAll` waits on TWO surfaces, bounded.** `bun test --parallel=1
  test/e2e` runs `restore.e2e.test.ts` **first**, and that file cycles the volumes itself, so a later
  file re-ups from cold. Console `/api/health` and `/.well-known/gbrain` are **two different
  containers behind the same proxy**; a measured **3073 ms** window existed where console health
  answered `200` while the reserved path answered `502`. Poll both, return only when console health
  is `< 500` **and** `/.well-known/gbrain` is exactly `200`, bounded at `120 × 2s` so a genuinely
  dead `gbrain-serve` fails the assertion instead of hanging the suite. Copy
  `test/e2e/slice1.e2e.test.ts`'s `beforeAll` shape verbatim.
- **`import './host-ops-env.ts';` is a required side-effect import, first.** `opsClient.ts` binds
  `OPS_URL` at module load from `process.env.OPS_RPC_URL ?? 'http://gbrain-ops:3140'`, and the
  container DNS name is unreachable from the host runner.
- **`--parallel=1`.** Every e2e file drives the same single stack.
- **L11 — between repeat runs restart `control-plane` and `gbrain-serve`, never the volumes.** Claim
  is 20 hits / 15 min in-process and OAuth `/token` is 50 / 15 min on serve; `down -v` would also
  destroy the populated brain Gate C needs. The `beforeAll` restarts those two and re-waits.
- **L8 — default `CONTROL_DATABASE_URL` inside this file**, never as a required export.
- **L7 — no wall-clock assertion tighter than 2s per `compose exec` probe.** Assert DB state and file
  state.
- **Tripwire.** `BRAINSTAND_ALLOW_SKIP_SLICE35_E2E`, same shape as `test/e2e/slice2.e2e.test.ts:410`.
  Docker-gated suites do not get to pass by not running.
- **No `docker compose down -v` anywhere in this file**, including its `afterAll`. Gate C's populated
  brain is the expensive fixture, and L6/F28 means a re-provisioned volume is not equivalent.

**What this gate proves that Gate A cannot:** that the product surface exists — a banner an operator
reads, a run history that outlives the container that wrote it, and a checklist acknowledgement that
is durable rather than remembered. B4 is the seam that proves **D2** (journal-first, reconciled
second) without paying for a full upgrade, and B6 is the one that proves **honesty rule 8** by
restarting a real container rather than simulating one.

```ts
describe('Slice 3.5 seam gate B', () => {
  test('slice3.5 e2e tests are not silently skipped', () => { /* as slice2.e2e.test.ts:410 */ });

  test('B1. sign in as admin, and the run list is empty before any run', async () => {
    cookie = await signIn(ADMIN_EMAIL, ADMIN_PASSWORD);
    expect(cookie).toBeTruthy();
    const body = await readJson<{ runs: any[] }>(await get('/api/upgrades'), 'GET /api/upgrades');
    expect(body.runs).toEqual([]);            // nothing fabricated at boot
  });

  test('B2. the banner offers a candidate version from a REAL check-update call', async () => {
    const body = await readJson<any>(await get('/api/upgrades/available'), 'GET available');
    expect(body.currentVersion).toBe('0.50.0.0');
    expect(body.checkedAt).toBeTruthy();
    // D7/honesty rule 2: an OFFER, never an action. The banner never carries a
    // "start" affordance and the payload never carries an upgrade command we run.
    expect(body.candidateVersion).toMatch(/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/);
    expect(body.status).toBe('update_available');
    expect(body.source).toBe('upstream-master-VERSION');   // U2, stated to the operator
    expect(body.publishedAt).toBeNull();                   // U2: '' is not a date
    expect(body.changelog).toBeTruthy();
    expect(Object.keys(body)).not.toContain('startUrl');
  }, 120_000);

  test('B3. a check that FAILED renders "could not check", never "up to date"', async () => {
    // Honesty rule 1. The failing check is the real upstream payload captured by
    // Gate A's A3 (error: 'network_error'), fed through the real route by
    // pointing the detect step at the offline probe — not a hand-written string.
    const body = await readJson<any>(
      await get('/api/upgrades/available?probe=offline'), 'GET available (offline)');
    expect(body.status).toBe('unknown');
    expect(body.error).toBe('network_error');
    expect(body.candidateVersion).toBeNull();
    expect(body.message).toMatch(/could not check/i);
    expect(body.message).not.toMatch(/up to date/i);
    // And the page says the same thing the API does.
    expect(await consoleText('/upgrades')).toContain('could not check');
  }, 120_000);

  test('B4. a journal written into UPGRADE_DIR is imported by the reconciler (D2)', async () => {
    // THE seam of this slice: the control plane is stopped for most of a real
    // upgrade, so the journal — not the control DB — is the authority while it
    // is down. Written with the PRODUCT's own writer (openJournal/appendEvent,
    // Task 4), exactly as scripts/upgrade.ts writes it; never hand-rolled JSON.
    journalRunId = `gate-b-${RUN_ID}`;
    const j = await openJournal(journalRunId);
    await appendEvent(j, { type: 'run_started', from: '0.50.0.0', to: '0.50.5.0' });
    await appendEvent(j, { type: 'state', state: 'quiesced' });
    await appendEvent(j, { type: 'playbook', version: '0.50.2.0', state: 'applied', notes: 'gate-b fixture' });
    await appendEvent(j, { type: 'checklist', key: 'gate-b-decision', prompt: 'Confirm X', why: 'judgement residue (G5)' });
    await appendEvent(j, { type: 'state', state: 'awaiting_admin_decision' });

    await post('/api/upgrades/reconcile', {});            // admin-only kick; also runs on a timer
    const runs = await readJson<any>(await get('/api/upgrades'), 'GET /api/upgrades');
    const row = runs.runs.find((r: any) => r.id === journalRunId);
    expect(row).toBeTruthy();
    expect(row.state).toBe('awaiting_admin_decision');
    expect(row.fromVersion).toBe('0.50.0.0');

    const detail = await readJson<any>(await get(`/api/upgrades/${journalRunId}`), 'GET run');
    expect(detail.events.map((e: any) => e.seq)).toEqual([1, 2, 3, 4, 5]);   // monotonic, in order
    expect(detail.ledger.map((l: any) => l.gbrainVersion)).toEqual(['0.50.2.0']);
    expect(detail.checklist.length).toBe(1);
    expect(detail.checklist[0].state).toBe('awaiting_admin_decision');
    expect(detail.checklist[0].acknowledgedBy).toBeNull();
  }, 300_000);

  test('B5. re-importing the same journal is a no-op', async () => {
    const before = await readJson<any>(await get(`/api/upgrades/${journalRunId}`), 'GET run');
    await post('/api/upgrades/reconcile', {});
    await post('/api/upgrades/reconcile', {});
    const after = await readJson<any>(await get(`/api/upgrades/${journalRunId}`), 'GET run');
    // Idempotent on (runId, seq): no duplicated events, no duplicated ledger row
    // (playbook_ledger.gbrainVersion is UNIQUE), no reopened checklist item.
    expect(after.events.length).toBe(before.events.length);
    expect(after.ledger.length).toBe(before.ledger.length);
    expect(after.checklist.length).toBe(before.checklist.length);
    expect(after.updatedAt).toBe(before.updatedAt);
  }, 300_000);

  test('B6. an admin acknowledges a checklist item, it is audited, and it SURVIVES a restart', async () => {
    const ack = await post(`/api/upgrades/${journalRunId}/checklist/gate-b-decision/ack`,
      { note: 'reviewed by gate B' });
    expect(ack.status).toBe(200);

    const audit = await auditRows({ action: 'upgrade.checklist.ack' });
    expect(audit.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(audit[0].target).toBe(`${journalRunId}/gate-b-decision`);
    expect(audit[0].actor).toBe(ADMIN_EMAIL);
    expect(audit[0].before.state).toBe('awaiting_admin_decision');
    expect(audit[0].after.state).toBe('acknowledged');

    // Honesty rule 8: RESTART THE CONTAINER. Do not simulate it — an in-memory
    // acknowledgement passes every assertion up to this line and fails here.
    await compose('restart', 'control-plane');
    await waitForStackReady(300_000);
    cookie = await signIn(ADMIN_EMAIL, ADMIN_PASSWORD);

    const detail = await readJson<any>(await get(`/api/upgrades/${journalRunId}`), 'GET run');
    expect(detail.checklist[0].state).toBe('acknowledged');
    expect(detail.checklist[0].acknowledgedBy).toBe(ADMIN_EMAIL);
    // A cleared item stops blocking; the run may complete with the item resolved.
    expect((await readJson<any>(await get('/api/upgrades/pending-decisions'), 'GET pending')).items)
      .toEqual([]);
  }, 900_000);

  test('B7. a viewer gets 403 on acknowledge', async () => {
    const viewer = await signIn(VIEWER_EMAIL, VIEWER_PASSWORD);
    const res = await fetchInsecure(
      `/api/upgrades/${journalRunId}/checklist/gate-b-decision/ack`,
      { method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json', cookie: viewer }, body: '{}' });
    expect(res.status).toBe(403);
    // G5: acknowledging judgement residue is an admin decision, not a read.
    expect((await res.json()).error).toMatch(/forbidden|role/i);
    // And the read surface is still readable — 403 on the write, not a blanket wall.
    expect((await fetchInsecure('/api/upgrades', { headers: { cookie: viewer } })).status).toBe(200);
  });

  test('B8. there is NO route that starts an upgrade, and the API says so', async () => {
    // D1/S2: the executor is a host CLI. The control plane holds no Docker
    // socket and no Docker CLI, and POST /api/backup's 503 BACKUP_HOST_ONLY_ERROR
    // is the precedent for how that boundary is expressed rather than hidden.
    const res = await post('/api/upgrades', { to: '0.50.5.0' });
    expect(res.status).toBe(503);
    const body = await res.json();
    expect(body.error).toBe('UPGRADE_HOST_ONLY_ERROR');
    expect(body.message).toMatch(/scripts\/upgrade\.ts/);
    // Nothing else accepts a start either.
    for (const p of ['/api/upgrades/start', '/api/upgrades/run', `/api/upgrades/${journalRunId}/start`]) {
      expect([404, 405, 503]).toContain((await post(p, {})).status);
    }
    // Rollback is host-only for the same reason.
    expect((await post(`/api/upgrades/${journalRunId}/rollback`, {})).status).toBe(503);
  });

  test('B9. the drift panel renders the capability diff, and will not call one snapshot "no drift"', async () => {
    const body = await readJson<any>(await get('/api/upgrades/drift'), 'GET drift');
    if (body.comparable === false) {
      // Only the baseline pin has been captured yet (Gate B may run before C).
      // A check that never ran is not a pass: it must say so, not render zeros.
      expect(body.reason).toBe('single_snapshot');
      expect(await consoleText('/upgrades')).toMatch(/no comparison yet/i);
    } else {
      expect(body.from).toBe('0.50.0.0');
      expect(body.to).toBe('0.50.5.0');
      expect(body.drift).toEqual({
        versionChanged: true, toolsAdded: [], toolsRemoved: [],
        adaptersAdded: [], adaptersRemoved: [], profilesChanged: false,
      });
    }
    // Either way the panel is built on the EXISTING primitive (diffCapability,
    // src/contracts/capability.ts:94-109). A second differ would be the defect.
    expect(Object.keys(body.drift ?? {}).sort()).toEqual(
      ['adaptersAdded', 'adaptersRemoved', 'profilesChanged', 'toolsAdded', 'toolsRemoved', 'versionChanged']);
  }, 120_000);

  test('B10. /admin is still not reachable', async () => {
    for (const p of ['/admin', '/admin/', '/admin/api/grants']) {
      expect((await fetchInsecure(p)).status).toBe(404);
    }
  });
});
```

## Task 17: Gate C — the Slice 3.5 exit gate

> **Corrected 2026-09-16 — Gate C could not run as written; six execution corrections.**
> (a) **C2's `plan.json.schema.pendingCount` is not added.** There is no published contract for the
> schema version (U6) and scraping the prose is G3, so `plan` cannot compute it. The gate derives
> pendency (**6** migrations, `144..149`) from upstream's own `m.version > current` filter and names the
> derivation in its output; it asserts the fixture's observed recorded schema version instead.
> (b) **C3's `schemaStep.stderrDigest` match on SQLSTATE 42703 contradicts G3.** `opsRpc` relays only
> `{ exitCode }` on purpose — there is nothing for a maintainer to regex. The SQLSTATE is observed on the
> **test-only** fixture lane (the same `CREATE INDEX` upstream v144 runs), and the product's failure is
> proven by the non-zero `apply-migrations` exit code plus the recorded schema version still being `143`.
> (c) **Fixture/leg-ordering defect (real).** The plan applied the fixture before the run's snapshot and
> never undid it, so Leg 1's rollback restored the fixtured brain and C4's `recordedSchemaVersion() === '149'`
> could not hold; Leg 2 would then have failed at migration 144. Executed order: pre-digest taken AFTER
> the fixture; rollback asserted to restore the **fixtured** state exactly (recorded `143` and the
> incompatible `open_loops`, digest equal); an explicit **UN-FIXTURE** between the legs; Leg 2 clean
> against a `149` brain. Both the fixture and its removal are printed in the gate's own output.
> (d) **Shapes.** `compareDigests` is `{equal, differing, onlyInA, onlyInB}`; `verifyRuntimePin` exposes
> `observed`; `/api/health` exposes `pin`. The plan's `.differences`/`.running`/`gbrain.pinMatches` were
> drafting errors (see Task 12's blockquote).
> (e) **No `--pause-after`/`resumeUpgrade`.** The journal is durable on disk, so C6–C10 are asserted
> from the completed run's journal.
> (f) **Digest churn.** The content digest gained exclusions for continuously-churning runtime state
> (`schedule_runs`/`schedule_alerts`, the worker registry), and the gate pauses schedules for its window
> and restores them after; the snapshot still restores the excluded rows (F6).

> **Corrected 2026-09-17 — the gate never established its own entry state, so it could not be re-run (F9).**
> C12 deliberately leaves the stack on `0.50.5.0` (Slice 4 inherits it), but neither this task nor Task 16
> ever put it back on `0.50.0.0`, while B2 and C1 assert the FROM pin and C3's failure leg is
> `run --to 0.50.5.0`. A second family run therefore started red: B2/C1 read `0.50.5.0`, C3's preflight
> refused `SAME_VERSION`, and C4–C12 cascaded (65 pass / 12 fail). The abort then skipped C5's un-fixture
> and left the brain fixtured (`config.version` 143, one-column `open_loops`), poisoning the next run too.
> The executed file now normalizes in its own file-level `beforeAll` — `normalizeToFromPin()` rolls the
> newest **completed** run from `0.50.0.0` back to that pin and verifies it (accepting a parity-only
> rollback exit 4) — and Gate C's `afterAll` un-fixtures as a backstop if a leg aborts with the fixture
> still applied. Two consecutive `bun test --parallel=1 test/e2e` runs are green (77/0 each). The plan's
> "apply the fixture before the run's snapshot" and C1's implicit `0.50.0.0` entry state are corrected
> accordingly. See `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md` F9.

**Files:**
- Modify: `test/e2e/slice35.e2e.test.ts` (append `describe('Slice 3.5 seam gate C')`)
- Create: `test/e2e/fixtures/migration-collision.ts` (**test-only**, never imported from `src/`)

**The roadmap's exit gate, verbatim:** *upgrade a populated deployment to the next release; assert
doctor parity; the `playbook_ledger` records the version; then **deliberately fail a migration and
assert one-click rollback restores the pre-upgrade state exactly**.*

### How it is proven honestly

State the facts plainly, before any assertion.

**v0.50.0.0 → v0.50.5.0 ships zero schema migrations** (U10). `src/core/migrate.ts` is byte-identical
at both tags; 149 is the maximum migration number at both. So the Schema step is a **genuine no-op on
the real delta**, and the exit gate's *"deliberately fail a migration"* half has nothing to fail on
its own. Waving that half through, or writing a fake migration of our own and failing it, would both
be theatre — the first proves nothing, the second proves our code can fail, which was never in doubt.

Slice 3 hit this exact shape with DST — a boundary you cannot wait for — and solved it by injecting
the clock into the **production** code path and saying so. The same convention applies here. The gate
splits:

- **The real leg runs for real, once** (Leg 2): a clean, unfixtured upgrade of the populated dev
  stack to 0.50.5.0, with the real quiesce, the real four-artifact snapshot, the real image swap, the
  real `apply-migrations`, the real ledger and the real per-check doctor parity. Nothing in that leg
  is simulated.
- **The failure half is fixtured, and the fixture is two values** (Leg 1, D9):
  1. **Regress the brain's recorded schema version** from `149` to `143`, so upstream's own
     migrations 144–149 become **genuinely pending** rather than invented.
  2. **Pre-create one object migration 144 will collide with.** Upstream's v144 (`open_loops`) runs
     `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS open_loops (…)` and then
     `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS open_loops_status_idx ON open_loops (…)`. Replacing `open_loops`
     with an incompatible table of the same name makes the `CREATE TABLE` a no-op and the following
     `CREATE INDEX` fail with SQLSTATE **42703**, `column … does not exist` — inside upstream's own
     DDL, at upstream's own migration number.

Then the real upgrade runs. `apply-migrations` fails for a real reason, in upstream's code, with a
real exit code; the run transitions to `rolling_back`; the rollback is the product's one command; and
`compareDigests` decides whether the pre-upgrade state came back.

**This is not a weakened gate.** The migration code is upstream's. The failure is genuine — we did
not raise it, catch it or inject it; we created the conditions and upstream's own SQL raised it. The
rollback is the shipped path, not a test helper. The **only** fixtured inputs are the recorded schema
version and one conflicting object, and both are named in the gate's own output (`C2` prints
`FIXTURE: config.version 149 -> 143; open_loops replaced (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY)`) and in
`docs/slice-3.5-findings.md`.

### The constraint the fixture must respect (G3)

Both fixture values live **past the published contracts**: the recorded schema version is a row in
gbrain's generic `config` table (`engine.getConfig('version')`,
`src/core/pglite-schema.ts:428-438` — there is no `schema_migrations` table and no command that
returns it as a first-class JSON field, U6), and `open_loops` is one of gbrain's own tables. **We may
not write either as normal product behaviour.** So, exactly:

- The fixture lives in **`test/e2e/fixtures/migration-collision.ts`** and is applied by
  `docker compose … exec -T gbrain-ops psql "$GBRAIN_DATABASE_URL" -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c '<sql>'` —
  an **argv array**, never a shell string, and never through our ops RPC (there is no allowlist entry
  for it, and there must not be one).
- It is **test-only and never ships in `src/`**. The gate asserts this mechanically:

  ```ts
  test('C0. the migration fixture never leaks into shipped code', async () => {
    const hits = await grepTree(['src', 'scripts'], /open_loops|FROM config WHERE key = 'version'/);
    expect(hits).toEqual([]);          // the fixture exists only under test/
  });
  ```
  (folded into C2's body so the leg count stays twelve).
- Both statements, and the observed `apply-migrations` exit code, are recorded verbatim in the
  findings doc. Nothing about the fixture is implicit.

### Why the legs run in this order

**Leg 1 (failure) → Leg 2 (real) → Leg 3 (rollback of a success).** Two reasons, both concrete:

1. **It minimises image rebuilds.** Leg 1 is the leg that first builds the v0.50.5.0 images (the
   swap happens *before* the Schema step, so the failure leg pays the build). Its rollback returns to
   the already-present 0.50.0.0 images. Legs 2 and 3 then hit warm layer cache for both tags, so the
   whole gate builds each image exactly once.
2. **It leaves the stack in the right end state.** Leg 3 ends by re-running the upgrade, so the dev
   stack finishes Gate C on **0.50.5.0** — which is what `HANDOFF.md` must then describe and what
   Slice 4 inherits. It also means the rollback path is proven *before* Leg 2 depends on it.

### Measured numbers, recorded not estimated

Every leg prints a `MEASURED` line; Task 18 pastes them into the findings doc. The four required
numbers are the **quiesce window** (stop of `scheduler` → Postgres alone; L1 says expect ~1.2 s for
the supervisor drain, not the 40 s budget — do not design around 40 s), the **snapshot duration and
total bytes** across the four artifacts, the **image build time**, and the **total downtime** (first
service stopped → `/api/health` 200 again).

```ts
describe('Slice 3.5 seam gate C', () => {
  // ---- Leg 1: the failure leg -------------------------------------------
  test('C1. the brain is populated on a POST-F4 stack, and the digest is taken', async () => {
    requireGateBReady();
    // L6/F28: a Space is visible to an UNQUALIFIED read only when config.federated
    // is literally true, and volumes provisioned before the F4 wizard fix keep it
    // unset. Provision through the console/wizard path on this stack, then assert
    // the flag, so a later search failure is an upgrade defect and not F28.
    await provisionPopulatedBrain();              // >= 3 Spaces, >= 6 committed pages, 1 active Connection
    for (const s of await spaceSources()) expect(s.config.federated).toBe(true);
    // L13: qualify the scope explicitly — a non-empty --federated-read is the FULL scope.
    expect((await search('performance review', { sourceIds: '__all__' })).length).toBeGreaterThan(0);

    preDigest = await stateDigest();
    expect(preDigest.gbrainVersion).toBe('0.50.0.0');
    expect(preDigest.pages).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(preDigest.sources.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(3);
    expect(preDigest.controlRowCounts.connections).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(Object.keys(preDigest)).toContain('filePlane');   // L7: the file plane is IN the digest
  }, 1_800_000);

  test('C2. the fixture makes upstream migrations genuinely pending, and never ships', async () => {
    expect(await grepTree(['src', 'scripts'], FIXTURE_MARKERS)).toEqual([]);   // C0, folded in
    const applied = await applyMigrationCollisionFixture();                    // test/e2e/fixtures/
    console.log(`MEASURED FIXTURE: ${applied.summary}`);
    expect(applied.recordedSchemaVersionBefore).toBe('149');
    expect(applied.recordedSchemaVersionAfter).toBe('143');
    expect(applied.collidingObject).toBe('open_loops');

    // Pending is upstream's own computation, not ours: runMigrations filters
    // m.version > current, so 144..149 are now real work.
    const plan = await upgradeCli(['plan', '--to', '0.50.5.0']);
    expect(plan.exitCode).toBe(0);
    expect(plan.json.schema.pendingCount).toBe(6);
    expect(plan.json.schema.pending[0]).toBe(144);
  }, 600_000);

  test('C3. apply-migrations FAILS for real, and the run transitions to rolling_back', async () => {
    const t0 = Date.now();
    const run = await upgradeCli(['run', '--to', '0.50.5.0', '--yes']);
    downtimeFailureLeg = Date.now() - t0;
    expect(run.exitCode).not.toBe(0);

    const journal = await readJournal(run.runId);
    const schemaStep = journal.find((e: any) => e.type === 'step' && e.step === 'schema');
    expect(schemaStep.outcome).toBe('failed');
    // U3: exit codes are 0/1/2 and there is NO JSON. We record the code, we do
    // not parse the prose. The observed value goes in the findings doc.
    expect([1, 2]).toContain(schemaStep.exitCode);
    expect(schemaStep.exitCode).not.toBe(0);
    // The failure is upstream's, at upstream's migration number.
    expect(schemaStep.stderrDigest).toMatch(/42703|does not exist/);

    const states = journal.filter((e: any) => e.type === 'state').map((e: any) => e.state);
    expect(states).toContain('rolling_back');
    expect(states).not.toContain('completed');
    failedRunId = run.runId;
  }, 3_600_000);

  test('C4. one-command rollback restores the pre-upgrade state exactly', async () => {
    const t0 = Date.now();
    const rb = await upgradeCli(['rollback', '--run', failedRunId, '--yes']);
    console.log(`MEASURED ROLLBACK(failure leg): ${Date.now() - t0}ms`);
    expect(rb.exitCode).toBe(0);                  // honesty rule 5: ONE operation, no second step
    await waitForStackReady(600_000);

    const after = await stateDigest();
    const diff = compareDigests(preDigest, after);
    expect(diff.differences).toEqual([]);         // the exit gate's own words
    expect(diff.equal).toBe(true);

    // The digest reads CONTRACT-VISIBLE state. The two things the fixture broke
    // are below that line, so assert them directly, through the same test-only
    // lane that broke them — otherwise "restores exactly" is narrower than it sounds.
    expect(await recordedSchemaVersion()).toBe('149');
    expect(await columnsOf('open_loops')).toContain('source_id');
    expect(await columnsOf('open_loops')).toContain('dedup_key');
  }, 3_600_000);

  test('C5. the pin is back at 0.50.0.0 and the RUNNING BINARY agrees', async () => {
    // Honesty rule 9: the pin in .env is not evidence the binary moved (or
    // moved back). Today nothing cross-checks it; verifyRuntimePin (Task 2) does.
    expect(await declaredPin()).toBe('0.50.0.0');
    const runtime = await verifyRuntimePin();
    expect(runtime.declared).toBe('0.50.0.0');
    expect(runtime.running).toBe('0.50.0.0');
    expect(runtime.matches).toBe(true);
    const health = await readJson<any>(await get('/api/health'), 'GET /api/health');
    expect(health.gbrain.pinMatches).toBe(true);
  }, 600_000);

  // ---- Leg 2: the real leg ----------------------------------------------
  test('C6. the quiesce is PROVEN clean, not merely reported', async () => {
    realRunId = (await upgradeCli(['run', '--to', '0.50.5.0', '--yes', '--pause-after', 'quiesce'])).runId;
    const q = (await readJournal(realRunId)).find((e: any) => e.type === 'quiesce');
    console.log(`MEASURED QUIESCE: ${q.windowMs}ms (supervisor drain ${q.supervisor.drainMs}ms)`);
    // D3: the stop order IS the atomicity guarantee.
    expect(q.stopped).toEqual(['scheduler', 'gbrain-worker', 'control-plane', 'gbrain-serve', 'gbrain-ops']);
    expect(q.livePostgresOnly).toBe(true);
    // U9 / honesty rule 3: reason:'drained' only means the PID vanished in 40s.
    expect(q.supervisor.stopReport.reason).toBe('drained');
    expect(q.supervisor.auditExitCode).toBe(0);          // the proof
    expect(['SIGTERM', 'SIGINT']).toContain(q.supervisor.auditReason);
    expect(q.supervisor.statusRunning).toBe(false);
    expect(q.windowMs).toBeLessThan(40_000);             // L1 measured 1150ms; recorded, not designed to
  }, 1_800_000);

  test('C7. the snapshot is four artifacts and one manifest, and it verifies', async () => {
    const m = (await readJournal(realRunId)).find((e: any) => e.type === 'snapshot').manifest;
    console.log(`MEASURED SNAPSHOT: ${m.durationMs}ms, ${m.artifacts.reduce((a, x) => a + x.bytes, 0)} bytes`);
    expect(m.artifacts.map((a: any) => a.name).sort()).toEqual(
      ['brain-db', 'brain-repos', 'control-db', 'gbrain-home']);   // L7: the file plane IS captured
    expect(m.fromVersion).toBe('0.50.0.0');
    expect(m.driver).toBe('docker-volume');
    expect(m.runId).toBe(realRunId);
    for (const a of m.artifacts) expect(a.sha256).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/);
    expect(await verifyManifest(m)).toEqual({ ok: true, mismatches: [] });
  }, 1_800_000);

  test('C8. the swap moves the pin AND the running binary', async () => {
    const t0 = Date.now();
    await resumeUpgrade(realRunId);
    console.log(`MEASURED BUILD+SWAP: ${Date.now() - t0}ms`);
    await waitForStackReady(900_000);

    expect(await declaredPin()).toBe('0.50.5.0');
    const runtime = await verifyRuntimePin();
    expect(runtime.running).toBe('0.50.5.0');
    expect(runtime.matches).toBe(true);                   // honesty rule 9
    // D8: the :-0.50.0.0 fallbacks are gone, so a compose call without
    // --env-file fails loudly instead of silently running the old images.
    const bare = await composeWithoutEnvFile(['config']);
    expect(bare.exitCode).not.toBe(0);
    expect(bare.stderr).toMatch(/GBRAIN_VERSION/);
    // The harness picker follows the pin (connections.ts no longer hardcodes it).
    expect((await readJson<any>(await get('/api/connections/harnesses'), 'GET harnesses')).pin)
      .toBe('0.50.5.0');
  }, 3_600_000);

  test('C9. apply-migrations applied nothing, and that is RECORDED as this delta\'s outcome', async () => {
    const j = await readJournal(realRunId);
    const schemaStep = j.find((e: any) => e.type === 'step' && e.step === 'schema');
    expect(schemaStep.exitCode).toBe(0);
    expect(schemaStep.outcome).toBe('no_migrations_pending');
    // U10 stated in the run's own record, not waved through: "nothing applied"
    // is the EXPECTED result for this delta and the journal says why.
    expect(schemaStep.evidence).toMatch(/migrate\.ts is byte-identical at 0\.50\.0\.0 and 0\.50\.5\.0/);
    expect(await recordedSchemaVersion()).toBe('149');

    // U11 / honesty rule 7 / D6: four versions, one playbook, three explicit
    // no-playbook-required rows WITH the evidence that established them.
    const ledger = await ledgerRows();
    expect(ledger.map((r: any) => r.gbrainVersion).sort())
      .toEqual(['0.50.1.0', '0.50.2.0', '0.50.4.0', '0.50.5.0']);
    expect(ledger.map((r: any) => r.gbrainVersion)).not.toContain('0.50.3.0');  // it does not exist
    const byV = Object.fromEntries(ledger.map((r: any) => [r.gbrainVersion, r]));
    expect(byV['0.50.2.0'].state).toBe('applied');
    expect(byV['0.50.2.0'].notes).toMatch(/skills\/migrations\/v0\.50\.2\.0\.md/);
    expect(byV['0.50.2.0'].notes).toMatch(/sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}/);
    for (const v of ['0.50.1.0', '0.50.4.0', '0.50.5.0']) {
      // The CHECK constraint allows only pending/applied/skipped/awaiting_admin_decision,
      // so the distinction lives in `notes`. 'skipped' would be a lie: nothing
      // was skipped, there was nothing to do. See the findings doc row.
      expect(byV[v].state).toBe('applied');
      expect(byV[v].notes).toMatch(/upstream ships no playbook for this version/);
      expect(byV[v].notes).toMatch(/skills\/migrations\/ listing at v0\.50\.5\.0/);
    }
  }, 900_000);

  test('C10. doctor parity passes per-check, drift is versionChanged only, content survives', async () => {
    const parity = (await readJournal(realRunId)).find((e: any) => e.type === 'gate').parity;
    // D5: per-check, never the exit code (U7: `warnings` exits 0), never
    // `blockers` (L4: it does not exist), never health_score.
    expect(parity.regressed).toEqual([]);
    expect(parity.verdict).toBe('pass');
    expect(parity.after.checks.find((c: any) => c.name === 'schema_version').status).toBe('ok');
    expect(Object.keys(parity.after)).not.toContain('blockers');
    for (const c of parity.newChecks) expect(parity.verdict).toBe('pass');   // news, not regression

    // Slice 1's deferred item, now filled: a row per pin, and the drift is what
    // U10 predicts — every underlying registry file is byte-identical.
    const versions = await capabilitySnapshotVersions();
    expect([...new Set(versions)].sort()).toEqual(['0.50.0.0', '0.50.5.0']);
    expect(driftBetween('0.50.0.0', '0.50.5.0')).resolves.toEqual({
      versionChanged: true, toolsAdded: [], toolsRemoved: [],
      adaptersAdded: [], adaptersRemoved: [], profilesChanged: false,
    });

    // The populated brain is still the populated brain. Scope is qualified (L13).
    const after = await stateDigest();
    expect(after.pages).toBe(preDigest.pages);
    expect(after.sources.map((s: any) => s.id).sort()).toEqual(preDigest.sources.map((s: any) => s.id).sort());
    expect((await search('performance review', { sourceIds: '__all__' })).length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    console.log(`MEASURED TOTAL DOWNTIME(real leg): ${(await readJournal(realRunId))
      .find((e: any) => e.type === 'run_completed').downtimeMs}ms`);
  }, 1_800_000);

  // ---- Leg 3: rollback of a SUCCESS -------------------------------------
  test('C11. rolling back a SUCCESSFUL run restores the pre-upgrade digest exactly', async () => {
    // Rollback is not merely a failure path. An operator who upgrades cleanly
    // and then finds a behaviour they cannot live with has exactly one lever,
    // and this is the only test that proves it works.
    const rb = await upgradeCli(['rollback', '--run', realRunId, '--yes']);
    expect(rb.exitCode).toBe(0);
    await waitForStackReady(600_000);

    expect(compareDigests(preDigest, await stateDigest()).differences).toEqual([]);
    expect(await declaredPin()).toBe('0.50.0.0');
    expect((await verifyRuntimePin()).running).toBe('0.50.0.0');
    // U8: there is no down-migration anywhere upstream, so this is snapshot
    // restore or it is nothing (D4). The pin moved back because the IMAGES
    // moved back, never because a binary rewrote itself.
    expect(rb.json.method).toBe('snapshot-restore');
    expect(rb.json.ranUpstreamDownMigrations).toBe(false);
  }, 3_600_000);

  test('C12. re-running the upgrade lands the stack at 0.50.5.0 and leaves it healthy', async () => {
    const again = await upgradeCli(['run', '--to', '0.50.5.0', '--yes']);
    expect(again.exitCode).toBe(0);
    await waitForStackReady(900_000);
    expect(await declaredPin()).toBe('0.50.5.0');
    expect((await verifyRuntimePin()).matches).toBe(true);

    const parity = (await readJournal(again.runId)).find((e: any) => e.type === 'gate').parity;
    expect(parity.verdict).toBe('pass');
    expect((await ledgerRows()).length).toBe(4);            // UNIQUE(gbrainVersion): still four
    const health = await readJson<any>(await get('/api/health'), 'GET /api/health');
    expect(health.ok).toBe(true);
    expect((await readJson<any>(await get('/api/upgrades/pending-decisions'), 'GET pending')).items)
      .toEqual([]);
  }, 3_600_000);
});
```

## Task 18: Findings, runbook, progress, and the handoff

**Files:**
- Create: `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md`, `docs/runbooks/upgrade.md`
- Modify: `docs/runbooks/break-glass.md`, `docs/superpowers/plans/PROGRESS.md`, `HANDOFF.md`,
  `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-brainstand-roadmap.md`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write `docs/slice-3.5-findings.md`**

Same shape as `docs/slice-3-findings.md`: a `| # | Claim | Status |` table, then one expanded
`## U1 — <claim>` section per row with the **exact command and the exact output**. Status vocabulary
is `confirmed` / `contradicted` / `inference` — and `inference` means *no artifact carries the
observation*, stated as such, never dressed up as a measurement.

**Required rows — a row with an empty status is an incomplete task.**

| # | Claim |
|---|---|
| U1 | `check-update` never sets an exit code — always 0, including on network failure |
| U2 | `check-update` reads `VERSION` off master, not a release API; `published_at` is always `''` |
| U3 | `apply-migrations` has no `--json`; exit codes are 0 / 1 / 2 |
| U4 | `post-upgrade` does a lock-free read-modify-write on `config.json` (`self_upgrade.*`) |
| U5 | `GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE` env overrides config |
| U6 | There is no schema-version surface in any published contract |
| U7 | An older binary against a newer schema silently succeeds; doctor calls it `warn` and exits 0 |
| U8 | No down-migrations exist anywhere upstream; `OrchestratorResult` has no rollback state |
| U9 | `supervisor stop`'s `reason:'drained'` cannot distinguish a clean exit from `MAX_CRASHES` |
| U10 | v0.50.0.0 → v0.50.5.0 ships zero schema migrations and zero capability drift |
| U11 | Exactly one new playbook exists in the delta (`v0.50.2.0`); `v0.50.3.0` does not exist |
| U12 | v0.50.5.0 is a security-hardening wave; at our pin `serve --http`'s `server.close()` is unbounded |
| P1 | v0.50.5.0's `.env` guardrail does not quarantine `GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE` |
| P2 | The other `GBRAIN_*` vars our compose sets survive the guardrail |
| P3 | The `bunfig.toml` preload lockdown does not break our container entrypoints |
| P4 | The OAuth self-registration scope ceiling does not narrow an existing Connection's grant |
| P5 | Transcript secret redaction changes no `--json` envelope we parse |
| P6 | `serve --http`'s 5s `server.close()` bound is present at 0.50.5.0 and absent at 0.50.0.0 |
| P7 | `check-update` and `post-upgrade` exist with the same flag set at 0.50.5.0 |
| P8 | `migrate.ts`, the operation registry, `HARNESS_ADAPTERS` and the 7 schema packs are byte-identical — re-proven against the **built image**, not only the clone |
| F… | Every finding execution surfaces, numbered from F1 in order of discovery |

If Task 1 numbered its probe rows differently, **use Task 1's numbering** — but the count must still
be eight, and every P-row must carry the command it ran.

Rows this plan already knows will be needed, and must not be discovered late:

- The **exact `apply-migrations` exit code** observed in C3 (U3 says 1 or 2; record which, and the
  first line of stderr).
- The **exact fixture statements** and the pre/post recorded schema version (D9's transparency
  requirement).
- `playbook_ledger`'s CHECK allows only `pending|applied|skipped|awaiting_admin_decision`, so a
  no-playbook-required version is recorded as `applied` with the evidence in `notes`. State it, and
  file "the ledger needs a `not_required` state" as a Slice 4 carry.
- The **four measured numbers** from Gate C: quiesce window, snapshot duration + bytes, image build
  time, total downtime — real numbers, per leg.
- The path `check-update` actually writes its cache to, observed rather than assumed.
- Whether `compareDigests` reporting "no differences" was ever weaker than the fixture's own
  direct assertions (C4's `recordedSchemaVersion` / `columnsOf` half) — say plainly what the digest
  does **not** see.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write `docs/runbooks/upgrade.md`**

The operator runbook, and a companion to `docs/runbooks/break-glass.md` in the shape of
`docs/runbooks/scheduler.md` (compose command form spelled out once at the top, tables for states and
what each one means, a "What NOT to do" section, an "After the upgrade" section). It must cover:

1. **How to check for an update.** The banner, `GET /api/upgrades/available`, and what *"could not
   check"* means (honesty rule 1) — it is **not** "up to date". Say plainly that "latest" is
   upstream's master `VERSION`, **not a vetted release** (U2), so the banner offers and a human
   selects.
2. **How to run one.** `bun run scripts/upgrade.ts plan --to <version>` then
   `run --to <version>` — **from the host**, never from inside a container, because the control plane
   holds no Docker socket by design (S2/D1). Note the `--env-file .env` requirement and that compose
   now fails loudly without it (D8).
3. **What the downtime is.** The real measured numbers from Gate C, per phase, with the note that the
   quiesce window is dominated by the image pull/build and not by the supervisor drain (L1: ~1.2 s).
4. **How to roll back.** `bun run scripts/upgrade.ts rollback --run <id>` — one operation (honesty
   rule 5). State the consequence nobody expects: **the rollback restores the control DB too**, so
   anything an admin changed in the console during the upgrade window is lost. And state U8 — there
   are no reverse migrations anywhere upstream, so this is snapshot restore or it is nothing.
5. **What an `awaiting_admin_decision` item means and how to clear it.** Judgement residue from a
   playbook (G5: an LLM may propose it, never execute it). It keeps the banner up, **blocks nothing
   else**, survives restarts, and is cleared by an admin at `/upgrades` or
   `POST /api/upgrades/:id/checklist/:key/ack`. A viewer cannot.
6. **What to do when the control plane does not come back.** The run journal in `UPGRADE_DIR` is the
   authority while the control plane is down (D2) — read `journal.ndjson` directly, find the last
   `state` event, and either resume (`run --resume <id>`) or roll back. The reconciler imports it
   when the control plane returns; nothing is lost because nothing was only in memory.

- [ ] **Step 3: Fix `docs/runbooks/break-glass.md`**

It currently tells operators, under *"Is the brain healthy at all?"*:

> Exit 0 = no blockers. Non-zero = read the `blockers` array.

**There is no `blockers` key** (L4, Slice 0 V6, confirmed twice and re-asserted by Gate A6). Replace
it with the per-check reading:

> Exit 0 does **not** mean healthy: `status: "warnings"` exits 0, and *"this binary is behind the
> database schema"* is a warn. Read `checks[]` — `filter(c => c.status !== 'ok')` — and `top_issues`.
> The report has no `blockers` key; the only `blockers` in gbrain's doctor belongs to the unrelated
> `--locks` diagnostic.

And extend the existing *"Never run `gbrain upgrade` or a self-upgrade by hand"* bullet to point at
the new runbook:

> The product owns the upgrade lane: `bun run scripts/upgrade.ts` on the host, with a snapshot in
> front of it and a one-command rollback behind it — see [`upgrade.md`](./upgrade.md). An
> in-container self-upgrade desynchronizes the image from the running binary and leaves nothing to
> roll back to. `GBRAIN_SELF_UPGRADE_MODE=off` is set for this reason, and
> `scripts/lint-forbidden.ts` fails the build on a moving pin.

- No schema-version surface exists in any published contract (U6), so the ledger records the binary
  version plus doctor's `schema_version` check **status** — less than it should. Filed as an upstream
  contribution, never scraped.
- `playbook_ledger`'s CHECK has no `not_required` state, so a no-playbook version is `applied` with

- `check-update` reads master's `VERSION` (U2), so "latest" is not a vetted release; there is no

- The snapshot driver has exactly one implementation (`docker-volume`); the `SnapshotDriver` seam
  exists for Slice 5's ZFS/cloud-disk driver and is unexercised by a second driver.
- The Schema step is proven against a fixtured failure (D9) because the real delta has none.
- `check-update` is still not schedulable, deliberately (D7) — the two scheduler tests that assert it
  must stay green.
- Multi-version jumps are untested: this slice proves one hop (`0.50.0.0 → 0.50.5.0`).
- Everything Slice 3 carried that Slice 3.5 did not consume, re-stated rather than dropped.
