---

## Self-review

Run against the roadmap's Slice 3.5 section and the stated scope, after the plan was written.

**Scope coverage** — every requirement maps to a task:

| Requirement | Task |
|---|---|
| 1. Detect — `check-update --json` → banner with the changelog | 7, 14, 16 (B2/B3) |
| 2. Quiesce — scheduler, then worker + runner; SIGTERM drains; in-flight jobs drained | 6, 11, 15 (A7), 17 (C6) |
| 3. Snapshot — ONE consistent point-in-time set | 4, 5, 11, 15 (A8/A9), 17 (C7) |
| 4. Swap — pull the new pinned tag; recreate serve + worker + ops | 2, 11, 17 (C8) |
| 5. Schema — `apply-migrations --yes --non-interactive`; `post-upgrade` | 7, 11, 15 (A4/A5), 17 (C3/C9) |
| 6. Playbook — hand-written step, checklist with per-item ack, `awaiting_admin_decision` that persists, blocks nothing, survives restarts | 9, 13, 16 (B4/B6/B7), 17 (C9) |
| 7. Gate — `doctor --json` vs baseline | 15 (A6), 17 (C10) |
| 8. Rollback — previous tag + snapshot restore, one operation | 12, 17 (C4/C11) |
| Exit gate: upgrade a populated deployment; doctor parity; `playbook_ledger` records the version; deliberately fail a migration; one-click rollback restores the pre-upgrade state exactly | 17 (C1–C12) |
| Deferred by Slice 1: `capability_snapshots` **population** | 8, 17 (C10) |
| Deferred by Slice 1: the **drift banner** | 8, 14, 16 (B9), 17 (C10) |
| Deferred by Slice 1: `playbook_ledger` **writes** | 9, 16 (B4), 17 (C9) |
| Deferred by Slice 3: `check-update` is not schedulable — Slice 3.5 owns detect | 7 (D7), and the two scheduler tests stay green |
| Deferred by Slice 3: `scheduler` missing from backup/restore stop lists (L14) | 6 |
| Global: never fork, pin exact semver, no moving pin (G1) | 2, D8, `lint:forbidden` |
| Global: published contracts only, no table reads, no prose scraping (G2/G3) | D5, U6, and Task 17's explicit test-only fixture carve-out |
| Global: an LLM never executes a playbook (G5) | D6, 9, 16 (B6/B7) |
| Entry gate: Slice 3 exit gate green | Global Constraints |

**Gaps found and closed during review:**

1. **Gate A's RPC half would have been untestable on the slice0 stack.** `composeExec`
   (`src/docker/host.ts:3`) targets `compose/docker-compose.slice0.yml`, whose pinned base image is
   never rebuilt from the working tree — so the regenerated `operations.json` carrying `checkUpdate`
   and `postUpgrade` does not exist there. Slice 3 hit this and corrected mid-execution; Task 15 now
   states the rule up front (RPC tests → the MAIN stack at `127.0.0.1:3140`) instead of rediscovering
   it.
2. **A3 would have passed vacuously.** Asserting `expect([0]).toContain(exitCode)` on a healthy
   network proves nothing about U1 — the happy path exits 0 either way, so the test would stay green
   even if upstream added an exit code. Replaced with an observed failure in a `--network none`
   throwaway container, and the test **fails** if it could not induce one.
3. **The fixture collides with G3, and the plan had no place to say so.** Both fixture values live
   past the published contracts. Task 17 now names the mechanism (`psql` argv through `gbrain-ops`,
   from `test/e2e/fixtures/`), asserts mechanically that neither marker appears in `src/` or
   `scripts/`, and prints the fixture in the gate's own output.
4. **`compareDigests` is narrower than "restores the pre-upgrade state exactly" sounds.** The digest
   reads contract-visible surfaces; the two things the fixture breaks are below that line. C4 now
   asserts them directly through the same test-only lane that broke them, and the findings doc says
   what the digest does not see.
5. **`playbook_ledger` has no state for "no playbook required."** The CHECK allows only
   `pending|applied|skipped|awaiting_admin_decision`, and `skipped` would be a lie — nothing was
   skipped, there was nothing to do. Resolved as `applied` with the evidence in `notes` (honesty rule
   7), asserted per-row in C9, and filed as a Slice 4 carry rather than left as an implementation
   detail.
6. **The drift panel could have shipped a lie.** With only the baseline pin captured, an empty diff
   renders as "no drift" when the truth is "nothing to compare." B9 asserts the single-snapshot case
   says `no comparison yet`, and is written to hold whether Gate B runs before or after Gate C.
7. **Gate C's leg order was arbitrary until it was justified.** Failure-first builds each image
   exactly once, proves the rollback before Leg 2 depends on it, and leaves the stack on 0.50.5.0 —
   which is the state `HANDOFF.md` then has to describe.
8. **The exit-gate run was scoped to one file.** The swap moves the pin, and four existing tests read
   the pin (`test/build/pin.test.sh`, two contract files, `test/unit/registry.test.ts`). Task 17
   Step 3 runs the whole family, exactly as Slice 3's Gate C did.

**Known soft spots, stated rather than hidden:**

- **The delta has no real migrations, so the Schema step's failure half is proven by fixture.** The
  migration code, the DDL and the error are upstream's; the recorded schema version and one
  conflicting object are ours. That is the strongest available proof against this delta, it is named
  in the gate's output and in the findings doc, and it is **not** the same thing as having watched a
  real upstream migration fail.
- **The snapshot driver has exactly one implementation.** `SnapshotDriver { id, take, restore, verify }`
  is a seam with one occupant (`docker-volume`). Nothing proves a second driver drops in without
  touching the flow until Slice 5 writes one; A8/A9 exercise the seam, not its generality.
- **There is no schema-version surface in any published contract (U6).** The ledger records the
  binary version and doctor's `schema_version` check *status*, which is strictly less than the schema
  version itself. The gap is filed as an upstream contribution. We do not scrape the prose, and the
  consequence is that an operator reading the ledger cannot tell which numeric migration a brain sits
  on.
- **`check-update` reads master's `VERSION` (U2), so "latest" is not a vetted release.** The banner
  offers, a human selects, `published_at` is always `''` and nothing auto-upgrades — but the product
  has no notion of a release channel, and the version it offers may be one nobody has released.
- **One hop is proven, not many.** `0.50.0.0 → 0.50.5.0` is a single jump on a single stack. A
  multi-version backlog — the thing the roadmap says this slice is the moat for — is exercised only
  through `playbooksFor(from, to)` throwing on a version with neither a playbook nor an explicit
  `noPlaybookRequired`, which is a mechanism, not a proof.
- **The supervisor-crash half of A7 is a fixture too.** A real `MAX_CRASHES` exit is not induced; a
  real audit file is written and parsed by the shipped reader. Stated in the test and in the findings
  doc rather than dressed up as a live crash.
- **Gate C leaves real state on the dev stack**, exactly as Slice 3 did: a populated post-F4 brain at
  0.50.5.0, two capability snapshots, four ledger rows, and Slice 3's still-frozen calendar fixtures.
  `HANDOFF.md` describes it because the next session will otherwise be surprised by it.
